**Links:** IR-1065, IR-1067, IR-1090.
**Trap:** Presenting the $10 billion figure as current. It is period evidence only.
**Mains hook:** The economic grievance layers onto strategic mistrust rather than offsetting it. Qualify: the structural asymmetry predates any single figure.
**Back:** *"Raw in, manufactures out: the grievance is structural."*

### Flashcard IR-1067 — De-risking vs decoupling vs friend-shoring (concept comparison)
**Front:** How do de-risking, decoupling and friend-shoring differ, and which describes India's China policy?
**Definition (learn):** De-risking reduces overconcentration in a single supplier while sustaining engagement. Decoupling means severing engagement. Friend-shoring relocates or diversifies supply chains toward geopolitically aligned or trusted partners, distinct from cost-only offshoring (⚠️ owner analytical definitions).
**Breakdown:**
- De-risking: diversify, keep trade.
- Decoupling: disengage.
- Friend-shoring: relocate to trusted partners.
- India's China posture: de-risking, not decoupling (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §2 and advanced §2, §6 (⚠️ owner definitions).
**Links:** IR-1064, IR-1100 (replacement trap), IR-1085 (SCRI), IR-1087.
**Trap:** Using "decoupling" for India's China policy. The owner rejects that label.
**Mains hook:** Distinguish the three terms, then place India at de-risking. Qualify with the multi-year limits of diversifying from China's manufacturing scale.
**Back:** *"De-risk trims, decouple severs, friend-shore relocates to trust."*

### Flashcard IR-1068 — iCET: India-US initiative on critical and emerging technology
**Front:** What is iCET, when was it announced, and what were its NSA-level meetings?
**Definition (learn):** The initiative on Critical and Emerging Technology is an India-US cooperation initiative covering semiconductors, AI, defence-industrial cooperation and space. It is a cooperation initiative, not a treaty or framework agreement.
**Breakdown:**
- Announced: May 2022.
- Inaugural NSA-level meeting: 31 January 2023.
- Second NSA-level review: 17 June 2024.
- Scope: semiconductors, AI, defence-industrial cooperation, space.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §2 and §4 (✅ owner 03; MEA/White House documentation cited by owner).
**Links:** IR-1069 (TRUST), IR-1097 (2024 PYQ), IR-1101 (alliance trap).
**Trap:** Calling iCET a defence alliance or treaty.
**Mains hook:** iCET is an example of the West fostering India as a technology partner, but with conditions. Qualify with export-control friction.
**Back:** *"Announced 2022, NSA review 2023 and 2024, not a treaty."*

### Flashcard IR-1069 — TRUST and COMPACT (13 February 2025) and Mission 500
**Front:** What are TRUST and COMPACT, and do they replace iCET?
**Definition (learn):** Two India-US initiatives announced in the same joint leaders' statement on 13 February 2025. TRUST is "Transforming the Relationship Utilizing Strategic Technology" (critical technology). COMPACT is "Catalyzing Opportunities for Military Partnership, Accelerated Commerce & Technology" (defence commerce). The same statement announced Mission 500 (a goal of expanding bilateral trade) and the launch of trade-agreement negotiations.
**Breakdown:**
- TRUST: critical technology cooperation.
- COMPACT: military partnership and commerce.
- Mission 500: trade expansion goal.
- Status: the official text does not say TRUST terminated or superseded iCET; treat as parallel branding (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §2, §4 and §6 (📰 owner; ⚠️ succession not documented).
**Links:** IR-1068, IR-1071, IR-1101 (trap).
**Trap:** "TRUST replaced iCET." The owner says no documented replacement.
**Mains hook:** Avoid treating a rebranding as a legal succession. Qualify that the trade agreement was under negotiation, not concluded.
**Back:** *"TRUST for tech, COMPACT for defence, not a replacement."*

### Flashcard IR-1070 — Clean-energy supply-chain commitments (21 September 2024)
**Front:** What did India and the US commit to on clean-energy supply chains on 21 September 2024?
**Definition (learn):** Bilateral commitments on clean-energy supply-chain resilience, announced alongside the broader technology partnership. The advanced owner says they extend the resilience agenda from technology into clean-energy manufacturing inputs.
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 21 September 2024.
- Scope: clean-energy supply-chain resilience.
- Role: a dated example for the West fostering India as an alternative supply base.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §4 and advanced §4 (📰 owner dated; ⚠️ details of the commitments not stated in owner).
**Links:** IR-1097 (2024 PYQ), IR-1085 (SCRI), IR-1087.
**Trap:** Treating the commitments as a completed supply chain. They are commitments.
**Mains hook:** Cite as one dated track of diversification; qualify that commitments are not relocated production.
**Back:** *"Clean energy, September 2024: commitments, not factories."*

### Flashcard IR-1071 — US Major Defence Partner (2016) and the ten-year framework (31 October 2025)
**Front:** What is India's status as a Major Defence Partner, and what is the 31 October 2025 framework?
**Definition (learn):** The US designated India a Major Defence Partner in 2016. The ten-year Framework for the US-India Major Defense Partnership was signed at Kuala Lumpur on 31 October 2025. It is a framework, not a mutual-defence treaty.
**Breakdown:**
- 2016: Major Defence Partner designation.
- 31 October 2025: ten-year framework, Kuala Lumpur.
- Content: procurement, exercises, industrial cooperation, technology access.
- No collective-defence obligation; export controls and commercial negotiation still condition delivery.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §4 and §6 (✅ owner 03 for 2016; 📰 owner for 31 October 2025).
**Links:** IR-1072, IR-1069, IR-1101 (alliance trap).
**Trap:** Calling the 2025 framework an alliance treaty.
**Mains hook:** Use the framework as evidence of deeper defence convergence, qualified by the absence of a collective-defence duty.
**Back:** *"Major partner in 2016, ten-year framework in 2025, no treaty."*

### Flashcard IR-1072 — India-US enabling agreements: LEMOA (2016), COMCASA (2018), BECA (2020)
**Front:** What do LEMOA, COMCASA and BECA each enable, and what do they not do?
**Definition (learn):** Three India-US enabling agreements that improve interoperability, logistics and situational awareness. They are enabling agreements, not a collective-defence alliance or an automatic basing right.
**Breakdown:**
- LEMOA (2016): reciprocal logistics support.
- COMCASA (2018): secure communications.
- BECA (2020): exchange of geospatial information.
- Limit: none is a collective-defence commitment.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §4 and owner 04 basic §2 (✅ owner 03; owner 04 cites the same three).
**Links:** IR-1071, IR-1120 (Malabar; maritime enablers in owner 04).
**Trap:** Option sets that say LEMOA gives basing rights.
**Mains hook:** Enablers raise interoperability, not dependence in the alliance sense.
**Back:** *"Logistics 2016, communications 2018, geospatial 2020: none is a pact."*

### Flashcard IR-1073 — US tariff cycle on India, 2025–26: dated executive measures
**Front:** What dated US tariff measures affected India in 2025–26, and why must they be cited as reversible?
**Definition (learn):** A 25% India-specific reciprocal tariff took effect 7 August 2025 (Executive Order 14326). An additional 25% Russian-oil-linked duty took effect 27 August 2025 (EO 14329). It was removed from 7 February 2026 (EO 14384). IEEPA additional duties, including the reciprocal duty, were ended by EO 14389 in February 2026. A separate 10% Section 301 duty on specified Indian goods applied from 24 July 2026.
**Breakdown:**
- 7 August 2025: 25% reciprocal duty.
- 27 August 2025: additional 25% Russian-oil-linked duty.
- 7 February 2026: additional duty removed.
- February 2026: IEEPA duties ended.
- 24 July 2026: 10% Section 301 duty.
- India called the Russian-oil-linked action "unjustified and unreasonable" (4 and 6 August 2025).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §4 and advanced §4 and §7 (📰 owner dated; EO numbers ⚠️ not independently verified).
**Links:** IR-1028 (hedging cost), IR-1078 (23rd Summit), IR-1097 (2024 PYQ counter-evidence).
**Trap:** Quoting a standing tariff rate. The owner calls these time-bound, executive-order-based measures.
**Mains hook:** The tariff cycle is the clearest counter-evidence to a one-way "fostering" reading. Qualify with its reversibility.
**Back:** *"Imposed in August 2025, lifted February 2026, a new 301 duty July 2026."*

### Flashcard IR-1074 — Tharoor: "tactical partnership", not alliance; US high-technology restrictions persist
**Front:** How does Tharoor describe the India-US relationship, and what friction does he note?
**Definition (learn):** Tharoor recommends viewing the relationship "as a tactical partnership that serves both countries' short-term interests", cautioning against "hyperbole" that would frame it as an alliance. He also notes "continuing restrictions on the sale of US high technology to India" as unresolved friction.
**Breakdown:**
- Framing: tactical partnership, not alliance.
- Friction: US high-technology export restrictions.
- Sikri's observation: India and the US moving toward closer alignment after decades of wariness.
- Tharoor: the US remains "a leading arms supplier to India".
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §3 and advanced §2, §5 (✅ owner 03 quoting Tharoor and Sikri).
**Links:** IR-1064, IR-1072, IR-1101.
**Trap:** Describing India-US as an alliance because of deepening ties.
**Mains hook:** Use "tactical partnership" with precision, then cite a specific restriction that qualifies convergence.
**Back:** *"Tactical, not treaty; restricted, not unconditional."*

### Flashcard IR-1075 — Russia: energy-deficient India, energy-surplus Russia, nuclear submarines (Sikri)
**Front:** What defence and energy complementarity does Sikri describe between India and Russia?
**Definition (learn):** Sikri writes that "India is an energy-deficient country and Russia an energy-surplus one". He also describes Russia as "the only foreign military supplier from whom India is able to procure hi-tech equipment unavailable from elsewhere, including nuclear-powered submarines".
**Breakdown:**
- Energy: India deficit, Russia surplus.
- Defence: unique supplier of certain high-technology equipment, including nuclear-powered submarines (Sikri).
- Continuity: sustains the partnership independent of Western alignment shifts.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §2–§3 and advanced §2, §7 (✅ owner 03 quoting Sikri; period-dated).
**Links:** IR-1076 (installed base), IR-1077 (CAATSA), IR-1078 (23rd Summit).
**Trap:** Treating Russia as a current sole supplier of all high-tech equipment. The owner's claim is Sikri's period assessment.
**Mains hook:** Complementarity explains continuity; qualify with dependence risk on the installed base.
**Back:** *"Energy-deficient India, energy-surplus Russia, submarines unique."*

### Flashcard IR-1076 — India-Russia installed base: Su-30MKI, BrahMos, S-400 (2018), AK-203 at Korwa
**Front:** Which Russian co-production and acquisition links form India's defence installed base?
**Definition (learn):** Su-30MKI licensed production and the BrahMos joint venture demonstrate co-production alongside procurement. The 2018 S-400 contract is a named high-end acquisition. The Indo-Russian Rifles Private Limited venture at Korwa is an attempt to localise AK-203 production.
**Breakdown:**
- Su-30MKI: licensed production.
- BrahMos: joint venture.
- S-400: 2018 contract.
- AK-203: Indo-Russian Rifles Pvt Ltd, Korwa (localisation, depth to be verified separately).
- Limit: installed-base and spares concentration creates dependence risk.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §4 (✅ owner 03). Owner notes S-400 delivery status and Russian-crude volumes are unverified without an official figure (advanced §4).
**Links:** IR-1075, IR-1077, IR-1027 (dependence trade-off).
**Trap:** Treating the S-400 contract as proof of delivered capability without delivery evidence.
**Mains hook:** Name the installed base and the spares dependence precisely, rather than calling the relationship simply "dependent".
**Back:** *"Su-30 built, BrahMos joint, S-400 contracted 2018, AK-203 localised at Korwa."*

### Flashcard IR-1077 — CAATSA exposure from the S-400 purchase
**Front:** What is CAATSA exposure, and why is it not an international-law obligation on India?
**Definition (learn):** The S-400 purchase created exposure to possible US sanctions under another state's domestic law. The owner treats this as third-party sanction pressure that can narrow strategic-autonomy choices.
**Breakdown:**
- Source: another state's domestic law (US).
- Effect: narrows strategic choice.
- Limit: exposure is not an international-law duty on India.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §4 (⚠️ owner analysis; CAATSA named as in owner).
**Links:** IR-1027, IR-1076, IR-1075.
**Trap:** "India is legally bound by CAATSA." The owner says exposure, not obligation.
**Mains hook:** Third-party sanctions are a constraint on autonomy, not a breach of law. Qualify accordingly.
**Back:** *"Exposure is not obligation."*

### Flashcard IR-1078 — 23rd India-Russia Annual Summit (New Delhi, 4–5 December 2025)
**Front:** What did the 23rd India-Russia Annual Summit produce?
**Definition (learn):** Held in New Delhi on 4–5 December 2025 during President Putin's state visit. Its joint statement was titled "Russia-India: A Time-Tested Progressive Partnership, Anchored in Trust & Mutual Respect". Among the documents adopted was a Programme for Development of Strategic Areas of Economic Cooperation until 2030.
**Breakdown:**
- Dates: 4–5 December 2025.
- Venue: New Delhi.
- Document: joint statement with the 2030 programme.
- Owner caution: S-400 delivery and Russian-crude volumes are unverified without official figures.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §4 and §6, advanced §4 (📰 owner dated; ⚠️ not re-verified).
**Links:** IR-1075, IR-1073, IR-1080 (2025 China track), IR-1069 (US track).
**Trap:** Treating the joint statement as delivery evidence.
**Mains hook:** One of three dated tracks in 2025 that show simultaneous hedging. Qualify with delivery gaps.
**Back:** *"December 2025 Delhi: time-tested, 2030 programme, delivery still to show."*

### Flashcard IR-1079 — India-China LAC patrolling arrangement (21 October 2024)
**Front:** What did MEA announce on 21 October 2024 about the India-China border, and where does it apply?
**Definition (learn):** MEA announced "an agreement... on patrolling arrangements along the Line of Actual Control... leading to disengagement and a resolution of the issues that had arisen in these areas in 2020". Later specified as covering Depsang and Demchok.
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 21 October 2024.
- Scope: patrolling along the LAC; disengagement.
- Specified areas: Depsang and Demchok.
- Status: patrolling and process arrangement, not a boundary settlement (⚠️ owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §4 and §6 (✅ owner 03 quoting MEA).
**Links:** IR-1080, IR-1082 (trap), IR-1081.
**Trap:** The patrolling arrangement settled the boundary. It did not.
**Mains hook:** Separate process arrangements from a boundary settlement; qualify with the open delimitation track.
**Back:** *"Patrol deal, October 2024: Depsang and Demchok, not the border."*

### Flashcard IR-1080 — India-China Special Representatives rounds: Beijing (18 December 2024), New Delhi (19 August 2025), Tianjin (31 August 2025)
**Front:** What did the 23rd and 24th Special Representatives rounds agree, and what was the Tianjin meeting?
**Definition (learn):** The 23rd SR round met in Beijing on 18 December 2024. The 24th met in New Delhi on 19 August 2025 and agreed an Expert Group on "early harvest" boundary delimitation, a WMCC working group on border management and General-Level Mechanisms in the Eastern and Middle sectors. The Prime Minister met President Xi at Tianjin on 31 August 2025.
**Breakdown:**
- 18 December 2024: 23rd SR round, Beijing.
- 19 August 2025: 24th SR round, New Delhi; Expert Group on early-harvest delimitation.
- General-Level Mechanisms: Eastern and Middle sectors.
- 31 August 2025: Modi-Xi meeting, Tianjin.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §4 and §6, advanced §4 (✅ owner 03 quoting MEA; dates as stated).
**Links:** IR-1079, IR-1081, IR-1082.
**Trap:** Confusing the Expert Group (process) with a delimitation agreement.
**Mains hook:** The SR channel is a process track. Qualify with the fact that "early harvest" is still under study.
**Back:** *"Beijing December 2024, Delhi August 2025, Tianjin 31 August."*

### Flashcard IR-1081 — WMCC rounds (35th: 27 May 2026; 36th: 6 August 2026) and reopened border-trade points
**Front:** What do the 2026 WMCC meetings and the border-trade provision show?
**Definition (learn):** The 35th Working Mechanism for Consultation and Coordination (WMCC) met in Beijing on 27 May 2026 and prepared for the next SR meeting. The 36th met on 6 August 2026 and superseded the earlier May-only chronology. The SR round also provided for reopening border trade through designated points: Lipulekh, Shipki La and Nathu La.
**Breakdown:**
- 27 May 2026: 35th WMCC, Beijing.
- 6 August 2026: 36th WMCC.
- Border trade: Lipulekh, Shipki La, Nathu La (designated points).
- Status: process architecture, not a settlement.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 advanced §4 and owner 03 basic verified block (✅ owner 03; ⚠️ WMCC dates per owner, not re-fetched).
**Links:** IR-1080, IR-1082, IR-1079.
**Trap:** Describing the WMCC as a boundary-resolution body.
**Mains hook:** Consultation mechanisms manage the boundary; they do not settle it. Qualify with the 2026 meeting dates.
**Back:** *"Thirty-five in May, thirty-six in August: talks, not treaty."*

### Flashcard IR-1082 — Trap: patrolling arrangements are not a boundary settlement
**Front:** Does the October 2024 patrolling arrangement settle the India-China boundary?
**Definition (learn):** No. MEA's own wording covers patrolling arrangements leading to disengagement in named areas. The boundary question continues through the SR channel. The Expert Group on "early harvest" delimitation was agreed only on 19 August 2025.
**Breakdown:**
- Patrolling arrangement: process.
- Settlement: a final boundary agreement, not reached (owner).
- Consultation mechanism: not a settlement.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §7 and advanced §4 (✅ owner 03).
**Links:** IR-1079, IR-1080, IR-1081.
**Trap:** Option sets that say the boundary is "settled" or "resolved".
**Mains hook:** Use the distinction between border claim, LAC perception, agreed boundary, ceasefire arrangement and final settlement.
**Back:** *"Patrolling is a process, settlement is a signature."*

### Flashcard IR-1083 — India-Japan: Special Strategic and Global Partnership (2014), 2+2 (20 August 2024) and reciprocal logistics (ACSA)
**Front:** What dated milestones define India-Japan strategic relations in the owner?
**Definition (learn):** The relationship was elevated to a Special Strategic and Global Partnership in 2014. The third Foreign and Defence Ministers' 2+2 met in New Delhi on 20 August 2024 and agreed to revise the 2008 Joint Declaration on Security Cooperation. The Agreement concerning Reciprocal Provision of Supplies and Services was signed in September 2020 and became operational in 2021.
**Breakdown:**
- 2014: Special Strategic and Global Partnership.
- 20 August 2024: third 2+2, New Delhi; revise 2008 security declaration.
- September 2020: reciprocal logistics agreement (ACSA); operational 2021.
- Limit: logistics access is not collective defence.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §4 (✅ owner 03).
**Links:** IR-1085 (SCRI), IR-1084 (HSR), IR-1099 (2019 India-Japan PYQ).
**Trap:** Calling the reciprocal logistics agreement a mutual-defence pact.
**Mains hook:** Japan supplies a security, infrastructure and technology pillar outside a treaty alliance. Qualify with Japan's own constraints.
**Back:** *"Special 2014, 2+2 in 2024, logistics 2020, no alliance."*

### Flashcard IR-1084 — Mumbai-Ahmedabad High-Speed Rail: Japanese finance and Shinkansen technology
**Front:** What is the Mumbai-Ahmedabad High-Speed Rail project's India-Japan significance?
**Definition (learn):** A high-speed rail project with Japanese cooperation. JICA records cumulative commitments exceeding JPY 1 trillion since 2017, alongside Shinkansen technology, training and station-area cooperation.
**Breakdown:**
- Finance: JICA commitments above JPY 1 trillion since 2017.
- Technology: Shinkansen; training; station-area development.
- Limit: finance and technology choice do not remove construction, localisation or delivery risk.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §4 (✅ owner 03; JICA figure as stated by owner).
**Links:** IR-1083, IR-1085.
**Trap:** Treating commitments as completed track.
**Mains hook:** Shows partnership converted into infrastructure and technology transfer; qualify with delivery risk.
**Back:** *"Yen since 2017, Shinkansen tech, delivery still ahead."*

### Flashcard IR-1085 — Supply Chain Resilience Initiative (SCRI), April 2021
**Front:** What is the Supply Chain Resilience Initiative, who launched it, and what does it do?
**Definition (learn):** A plurilateral initiative launched in April 2021 by Australia, India and Japan to promote supply-chain diversification, digital tools and buyer-seller matching, translating de-risking from China into an economic instrument.
**Breakdown:**
- Launched: April 2021.
- Members: Australia, India, Japan.
- Tools: diversification, digital tools, buyer-seller matching.
- Limit: matching does not itself establish relocated production.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §4 (✅ owner 03).
**Links:** IR-1067, IR-1083, IR-1087.
**Trap:** SCRI as a US-led initiative. It is trilateral among Australia, India and Japan.
**Mains hook:** Use SCRI as an example of plurilateral de-risking; qualify that dialogue is not production.
**Back:** *"April 2021, three partners, matching not manufacturing."*

### Flashcard IR-1086 — India-EU: Trade and Technology Council and the FTA concluded on 27 January 2026
**Front:** What India-EU pillars does the owner list, and what is the status of the trade agreement?
**Definition (learn):** The EU-India Trade and Technology Council (TTC) provides a channel for technology, green-transition and standards cooperation. FTA negotiations concluded on 27 January 2026. Legal review and ratification remained necessary before entry into force.
**Breakdown:**
- TTC: technology, green transition, standards.
- FTA: negotiations concluded 27 January 2026.
- Status: not in force (per owner).
- Limits: regulatory differences; agreement not yet in force.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §4 (⚠️ owner; not re-verified).
**Links:** IR-1087, IR-1104 (trade mechanics owned by Economy), IR-1100.
**Trap:** "Concluded" means in force. Concluded negotiations are not entry into force.
**Mains hook:** The EU widens India's options beyond US-China competition; qualify with ratification risk.
**Back:** *"Concluded January 2026, not yet in force."*

### Flashcard IR-1087 — Quad and I2U2 as non-treaty supply-chain and technology channels
**Front:** How are Quad and I2U2 used as supply-chain and technology vehicles in the advanced owner?
**Definition (learn):** Non-treaty coalitions that provide additional, non-exclusive channels for diversification cooperation, distinct from the bilateral track. Quad covers maritime and technology cooperation; I2U2 covers technology, food and energy (⚠️ owner analytical framing).
**Breakdown:**
- Quad: non-treaty; maritime and technology.
- I2U2: technology, food and energy (membership in owner 01 IR-1030).
- Coordination cost: partners' differing priorities slow delivery.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 advanced §4 and §6 (⚠️ owner analysis).
**Links:** IR-1030, IR-1085, IR-1067.
**Trap:** Treating minilaterals as alliances.
**Mains hook:** Minilaterals add channels without symmetry; qualify with coordination costs.
**Back:** *"Quad and I2U2 open doors, not treaty gates."*

### Flashcard IR-1088 — Belt and Road Initiative: an umbrella, not one road, treaty or institution
**Front:** What is the Belt and Road Initiative in the owner's framing?
**Definition (learn):** The BRI is an umbrella for policy coordination, infrastructure, trade, finance, logistics, digital links and people-to-people engagement through overland corridors and a Maritime Silk Road. It is not one road, treaty or institution (⚠️ owner framing).
**Breakdown:**
- Overland corridors and Maritime Silk Road.
- Finance: loans, grants, policy banks, commercial credit, SPVs.
- Chain: connectivity deficit → Chinese finance and SOE/EPC capacity → faster delivery → economic gain and strategic influence.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §4A (⚠️ owner analytical framing).
**Links:** IR-1089, IR-1090, IR-1093, IR-1098 (2026 PYQ).
**Trap:** Describing BRI as a single treaty.
**Mains hook:** Separate economic connectivity, political economy, strategic access and sovereignty when answering.
**Back:** *"Umbrella of roads, ports, power, finance: no single treaty."*

### Flashcard IR-1089 — CPEC: India's categorical sovereignty objection
**Front:** Why does India object to the China-Pakistan Economic Corridor, and what is the correct formulation of India's position?
**Definition (learn):** CPEC combines transport, energy, Gwadar and related economic-zone projects. India's official objection is that CPEC passes through territory in the former State of Jammu and Kashmir under Pakistan's control, thereby impinging on India's sovereignty and territorial integrity. The correct formulation: India opposes connectivity that disregards sovereignty, territorial integrity, transparency and local ownership, not connectivity as such.
**Breakdown:**
- Objection: sovereignty over territory under Pakistan's control.
- Third-country participation does not cure the objection.
- Standard: sovereignty, transparency, viability, local ownership.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §4A (✅ owner 03 for the official objection).
**Links:** IR-1088, IR-1093, IR-1102 (trap).
**Trap:** "India opposes all China-promoted connectivity." The owner rejects that reading.
**Mains hook:** Precise sovereignty objection plus a broader standard for all connectivity.
**Back:** *"Sovereignty on the route, not all roads."*

### Flashcard IR-1090 — Debt-trap debate: three propositions and the defensible formulation
**Front:** How does the owner assess the claim that BRI projects are debt traps?
**Definition (learn):** A proposition table tests three claims. "Every BRI project is a deliberate debt trap" is too deterministic. "BRI debt creates no strategic risk" is untenable. The defensible formulation: not every BRI project is a trap, but non-transparent, foreign-currency, debt-heavy and low-return projects increase fiscal, sovereignty and strategic risks.
**Breakdown:**
- Deterministic trap claim: rejected.
- No-risk claim: rejected.
- Defensible: opacity, foreign currency, debt-heavy, low return create risk.
- Distress causes: project selection, domestic borrowing, weak appraisal, exchange-rate and commodity shocks, global rates.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §4A (⚠️ owner analysis).
**Links:** IR-1091 (six-variable test), IR-1066, IR-1102 (trap).
**Trap:** Option sets that claim a single cause or no risk.
**Mains hook:** Reject the monocausal claim, then explain the real risk. Qualify that outcomes are multi-causal.
**Back:** *"Not every loan is a trap; opaque, dollar, weak-return loans are."*

### Flashcard IR-1091 — Six-variable debt-distress test (advanced)
**Front:** What six variables should be tested before using the term "debt trap"?
**Definition (learn):** Project viability; financing terms (currency, maturity, interest, grace period, security); borrower choices (procurement, guarantees, off-budget liabilities); pre-existing fragility; external shocks (exchange rates, commodities, tourism, conflict, global rates); and restructuring leverage (what concession is actually sought and evidenced).
**Breakdown:**
- 1. Viability: demand and revenue.
- 2. Terms: currency, maturity, interest, grace, security.
- 3. Borrower choices.
- 4. Pre-existing fragility.
- 5. External shocks.
- 6. Restructuring leverage.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 advanced §4 (⚠️ owner analysis).
**Links:** IR-1090, IR-1092.
**Trap:** Asserting intent without evidence. The owner's verdict: deliberate debt-trap diplomacy is a strong claim requiring evidence.
**Mains hook:** Use the six-variable test as a scoring habit; conclude with "asymmetric dependence", the safer claim.
**Back:** *"Viability, terms, borrower, fragility, shocks, leverage."*

### Flashcard IR-1092 — Dual-use test: reason for scrutiny, not proof of a base
**Front:** What evidence is required before calling a BRI asset dual-use?
**Definition (learn):** Dual-use is a risk assessment, not proof of militarisation. Tests: can the asset physically support military or logistics activity; does a foreign operator control access or data; is there recurrent security use; is civilian use commercially significant; what does the host state permit.
**Breakdown:**
- Physical capacity: depth, runway, repair, storage, communications.
- Control: contract, concession, operational control.
- Recurrent use: verified visits, agreements, deployments.
- Civilian use: cargo, passenger, energy.
- Host consent: domestic law and politics.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §4A and advanced §4 (⚠️ owner analysis).
**Links:** IR-1094, IR-1091.
**Trap:** Strategic location plus Chinese finance equals a military base. It equals a reason to scrutinise.
**Mains hook:** Apply the five tests to a port example; qualify that scrutiny is not proof.
**Back:** *"Location plus loan is a question, not a base."*

### Flashcard IR-1093 — South Asia country matrix: BRI patterns in six states
**Front:** How do BRI patterns differ across Pakistan, Sri Lanka, Bangladesh, Nepal, Maldives and Afghanistan?
**Definition (learn):** Pakistan: CPEC energy, transport, Gwadar and economic zones; deep strategic alignment; direct sovereignty and two-front concern. Sri Lanka: ports, urban infrastructure, power and transport; debt stress with domestic choices; competition in India's maritime neighbourhood. Bangladesh: transport, power, bridge or tunnel, trade and port cooperation; pragmatic diversification. Nepal: proposed roads, rail, tunnels, dry ports, energy; mountain feasibility and bargaining. Maldives: bridge, housing, airport; small-state fiscal exposure; maritime influence. Afghanistan: limited direct implementation; connectivity and security spillover.
**Breakdown:**
- Pakistan: two-front and Arabian Sea access concern.
- Sri Lanka: sovereignty sensitivity over ports and land.
- Bangladesh: competitive delivery; scope for complementarity.
- Nepal: transit diversification; reliability of Indian transit.
- Maldives: maritime-domain and political-influence concern.
- Afghanistan: conflict, recognition and sanctions dominate.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §4A matrix (⚠️ owner analytical matrix; project status requires dated re-verification).
**Links:** IR-1088, IR-1089, IR-1045 (Maldives Vision), IR-1051 (Afghanistan).
**Trap:** Treating every BRI partner as aligned with China. The owner's country-matrix rule: partners hedge, bargain and diversify.
**Mains hook:** Use at least four countries with different patterns, including partner agency and domestic political economy.
**Back:** *"Six states, six patterns, none a puppet."*

### Flashcard IR-1094 — Security implications for India of BRI-linked infrastructure (six points)
**Front:** What six security implications does the owner list for India?
**Definition (learn):** Sovereignty (CPEC crosses territory India claims); strategic access (ports and logistics may support replenishment or surveillance without a declared base); dual-use infrastructure (risk, not proof); neighbourhood influence (finance and visible delivery increase access to elites and standards); two-front pressure (continental and maritime linked); technology dependence (cyber, data, vendor lock-in).
**Breakdown:**
- 1. Sovereignty.
- 2. Strategic access.
- 3. Dual-use infrastructure.
- 4. Neighbourhood influence.
- 5. Two-front pressure.
- 6. Technology dependence.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §4A (⚠️ owner analysis).
**Links:** IR-1092, IR-1089, IR-1093.
**Trap:** Presenting dual-use potential as evidence of militarisation.
**Mains hook:** Map each implication to a concrete asset and a mitigation.
**Back:** *"Sovereignty, access, dual-use, influence, two fronts, dependence."*

### Flashcard IR-1095 — India's response architecture: competitive public goods, not veto politics
**Front:** Which instruments does India use to respond to BRI competition, and what is the owner's verdict?
**Definition (learn):** The response architecture: Neighbourhood First (political priority, development partnership, crisis support); SAGAR and MAHASAGAR (maritime security, capacity, growth); BIMSTEC (Bay of Bengal platform); BBIN (subregional transit); Chabahar (access to Afghanistan and Central Asia bypassing Pakistan, constrained by sanctions and finance); IMEC (a proposed westward corridor concept, not an operational route); development partnership and lines of credit; domestic maritime and border capacity.
**Breakdown:**
- Match projects to partner demand.
- Publish financing and implementation status.
- Improve speed without bypassing consultation.
- Combine credit and grants with capacity and market access.
- Link projects through BBIN, BIMSTEC and digital public goods where consent exists.
- Protect security through surveillance, cyber standards and domestic capability.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §4A table and advanced §4 (✅ owner 03 for instruments; ⚠️ IMEC status per owner; Chabahar sanctions per owner).
**Links:** IR-1041 (BBIN), IR-1015 (SAGAR), IR-1102, IR-1103.
**Trap:** Treating IMEC as an operational route. The owner calls it a memorandum or corridor concept.
**Mains hook:** India must compete on trust, speed and sustainability, not only oppose. Qualify that alternatives need resources.
**Back:** *"Compete on delivery: Chabahar, BBIN, IMEC on paper, trust in practice."*

### Flashcard IR-1096 — Russia-Ukraine: India's posture and the energy, fertiliser and spares channels
**Front:** How does the owner describe India's position on the Russia-Ukraine conflict and its interests?
**Definition (learn):** Russia's full-scale military action against Ukraine began in February 2022. India called for dialogue, invoked sovereignty and territorial integrity, provided humanitarian assistance and generally abstained on polarising UN resolutions rather than joining either bloc (✅ owner 03).
**Breakdown:**
- Posture: dialogue, sovereignty, humanitarian aid, abstention on polarising UN resolutions.
- Energy and fertiliser: discounted Russian supplies redirected to India (⚠️ gain qualified by sanctions and payment friction).
- Defence: legacy Russian-origin spares concern.
- Global spillovers: Black Sea disruption to food, fertiliser, freight and energy.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 advanced/basic §5 (✅ February 2022 start; ⚠️ rest of analysis).
**Links:** IR-1075, IR-1076, IR-1073.
**Trap:** Describing abstention as joining Russia or as neutrality on principles. The owner says India separates principle from supply management.
**Mains hook:** Demonstrate strategic autonomy with a balanced diplomatic posture and name the limits (reputational criticism; reduced norm-enforcement room).
**Back:** *"Abstain, aid, supply, never sign a bloc."*

### Flashcard IR-1097 — PYQ: West fostering India as an alternative to China's supply chain (2024 GS-II Q9)
**Front:** What is the 2024 GS-II Q9 demand and how should the answer be structured?
**Definition (learn):** The verbatim paper text reads: "The West is fostering India as an alternative to reduce dependence on China's supply chain and as a strategically to counter China's political and econimic dominance." The question asks students to explain with examples (10 marks, 150 words). The printed text contains defects, retained as printed.
**Breakdown:**
- Route: define friend-shoring or de-risking.
- Examples: iCET (17 June 2024 review), clean-energy commitments (21 September 2024), TRUST and COMPACT (13 February 2025).
- Counterpoint: diversification of dependence, not replacement; 2025–26 US tariff cycle shows fostering is conditional and reversible.
- Close: India pursues this without abandoning China trade or Russia ties (23rd Summit, 4–5 December 2025).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §5 and §9, advanced §5 and §10 (✅ owner 03 ledger).
**Links:** IR-1067, IR-1068, IR-1069, IR-1070, IR-1073, IR-1078.
**Trap:** Writing that India is "replacing" China.
**Mains hook:** Diversify dependence; avoid the anti-China-bloc reading.
**Back:** *"Foster, diversify, don't replace: iCET, clean energy, TRUST."*

### Flashcard IR-1098 — PYQ: BRI and the transformation of South Asia (2026 GS-II Q20, 15 marks)
**Front:** What does the 2026 GS-II Q20 ask, and what is the answer architecture?
**Definition (learn):** The question states that BRI has transformed South Asia from a regional space into a theatre of great power competition, and asks students to analyse the strategic implications for India's security and regional influence (15 marks, 250 words).
**Breakdown:**
- Thesis: BRI converts finance into geo-economic influence; South Asian states diversify rather than form blocs.
- Architecture: connectivity plus finance plus state-linked implementation.
- CPEC: state the sovereignty objection precisely.
- Matrix: Pakistan plus at least three others.
- Security: dual-use, two-front pressure, dependence, influence.
- Balance: reject monocausal debt-trap; explain opacity and viability risk.
- Response: delivery, transparency, subregional connectivity, maritime capacity.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §4A "2026 GS-II answer architecture" and §9, advanced §10 (✅ owner 03 OCR-verified demand text).
**Links:** IR-1088 to IR-1095.
**Trap:** Reducing the answer to "debt trap" or "string of pearls".
**Mains hook:** Build a balanced answer with a country matrix and a response.
**Back:** *"Thesis, architecture, CPEC, matrix, security, balance, response, verdict."*

### Flashcard IR-1099 — Earlier PYQ routings: India-Japan (2019 Q9), India-US friction (2019 Q20), US challenge from China (2021 Q10), nuclear action plan (2019 Prelims Q89)
**Front:** Which earlier GS-II and Prelims questions route to the major-power owner, and what do they ask?
**Definition (learn):** 2019 GS-II Q9 asks for comment on the India-Japan contemporary global and strategic partnership (10 marks, 150 words). 2019 GS-II Q20 asks for friction in India-US ties and India's place in US global strategy (15 marks, 250 words). 2021 GS-II Q10 asks whether the USA faces an existential challenge from China (10 marks, 150 words). 2019 Prelims GS-I Q89 asks about the India nuclear-field cooperation bilateral action plan; key unavailable locally.
**Breakdown:**
- 2019 Q9: IR-1083, IR-1084.
- 2019 Q20: IR-1074, IR-1069.
- 2021 Q10: IR-1064, IR-1065.
- 2019 Prelims Q89: key not held locally; no answer inferred.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic and advanced PYQ integration blocks (✅ owner 03 ledger).
**Links:** IR-1074, IR-1083, IR-1064.
**Trap:** Answering the 2021 Q10 as a binary question. The owner expects the three-track logic.
**Mains hook:** Use the three-track model for the 2021 question and the tactical-partnership frame for 2019 Q20.
**Back:** *"Japan comment 2019, US friction 2019, US versus China 2021."*

### Flashcard IR-1100 — Trap: supply-chain diversification is not India "replacing" China
**Front:** Does the West's diversification toward India mean India is replacing China?
**Definition (learn):** No. It means diversifying dependence, not substitution. China remains a major trading partner even as alternative capacity develops elsewhere.
**Breakdown:**
- Diversification: spreading dependence across suppliers.
- Replacement: full substitution of China (not the owner's reading).
- Scale: China's manufacturing scale makes full diversification multi-year.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §7 and advanced §6 (✅ owner 03).
**Links:** IR-1067, IR-1097.
**Trap:** Option sets that describe "replacing" China.
**Mains hook:** Use "diversification of dependence" as the exact term.
**Back:** *"Diversify dependence, don't replace China."*

### Flashcard IR-1101 — Trap: iCET, TRUST, COMPACT and the MDP framework are not alliance treaties
**Front:** Are India-US technology and defence frameworks formal military alliances?
**Definition (learn):** No. iCET and the defence-industrial cooperation are partnership frameworks. The 31 October 2025 instrument is a ten-year framework for a major defence partnership, not a mutual-defence treaty. The official 13 February 2025 statement does not say TRUST terminated iCET.
**Breakdown:**
- iCET: cooperation initiative.
- TRUST and COMPACT: parallel branding.
- MDP framework: framework, not treaty.
- Tharoor: "tactical" not alliance.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §7 (✅ owner 03).
**Links:** IR-1068, IR-1069, IR-1071, IR-1074.
**Trap:** Any option that calls a 2025 framework a "mutual-defence treaty".
**Mains hook:** Precision on instrument type scores higher than generic praise for partnership.
**Back:** *"Framework, initiative, branding: no collective defence."*

### Flashcard IR-1102 — Trap: a single debt case does not prove a universal debt-trap plan; India does not oppose all Chinese connectivity
**Front:** What two overstatements about BRI does the owner reject?
**Definition (learn):** (a) Any single debt-distress case proves a universal debt-trap master plan: rejected, because outcomes are multi-causal, though opacity and weak viability can create leverage and sovereignty risk. (b) India opposes all connectivity promoted by China: rejected, because the CPEC objection is sovereignty-specific and the broader standard is sovereignty, transparency, viability and local ownership.
**Breakdown:**
- Overstatement 1: universal master plan.
- Overstatement 2: blanket opposition.
- Correct: specific risk with specific standard.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §7 (✅ owner 03).
**Links:** IR-1089, IR-1090, IR-1091.
**Trap:** Options that generalise from a single case.
**Mains hook:** Qualify both overstatements in the same answer.
**Back:** *"One case is not a plan; CPEC is not all roads."*

### Flashcard IR-1103 — Trap: not every South Asian BRI participant is aligned with China
**Front:** Does accepting a Chinese-financed project mean a state is aligned with China?
**Definition (learn):** No. Neighbours hedge among partners and retain agency. Project acceptance is not a defence alliance.
**Breakdown:**
- Agency: hedging, bargaining, diversifying.
- Elite incentives and public backlash shape choices (advanced).
- Policy reset: government change can renegotiate or rebrand projects.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §7 and advanced §4A (⚠️ owner analysis).
**Links:** IR-1093, IR-1095.
**Trap:** Assuming automatic alignment with the financing power.
**Mains hook:** Include partner agency and political economy in any South Asia answer.
**Back:** *"Accepting a loan is not joining a camp."*

### Flashcard IR-1104 — Critical minerals and semiconductors: strategic supply chains under the resilience agenda
**Front:** Why are critical minerals and semiconductors part of IR's supply-chain analysis?
**Definition (learn):** Inputs such as rare earths, lithium and cobalt, and chip fabrication and packaging, are treated as strategic-supply-chain priorities. Their security and technology-access dimension is IR's domain; the trade and production mechanics belong to Economy (⚠️ owner framing).
**Breakdown:**
- Inputs: rare earths, lithium, cobalt.
- Semiconductors: fabrication and packaging.
- IR role: technology access and supply-chain resilience diplomacy.
- Economy role: trade and production mechanics.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 03 basic §2 (⚠️ owner analytical; no dates).
**Links:** IR-1068, IR-1070, IR-1067.
**Trap:** Treating these only as Economy topics, or only as security topics.
**Mains hook:** State both the economic and strategic dimensions; qualify with the multi-year timeline.
**Back:** *"Minerals and chips: strategic inputs, shared between IR and Economy."*

### GROUP — Indo-Pacific, Indian Ocean and Maritime Security

### Flashcard IR-1105 — Indian Ocean as strategic space: maritime security stack (concept)
**Front:** How do the owner's sea-lane, doctrine, institution, island-partner and external-power layers fit together?
**Definition (learn):** India's Indian Ocean engagement links sea-lane dependence to a declared doctrine (SAGAR, extended as MAHASAGAR), an institutional stack (IORA, IONS, IFC-IOR), island-state partnerships, non-treaty coalitions (Quad) and HADR diplomacy, in a space where other major powers are present and competing (⚠️ owner analytical framing).
**Breakdown:**
- Interest: sea lanes carrying most of India's trade and energy imports.
- Doctrine: SAGAR (2015) → MAHASAGAR (2025).
- Institutions: IORA, IONS, IFC-IOR, Colombo Security Conclave, ReCAAP, UNCLOS and BBNJ framework.
- Partners: Maldives, Seychelles, Mauritius, Sri Lanka.
- Threats: piracy, maritime terrorism, HADR needs, illegal fishing, trafficking.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §1 (✅ owner 04 for the architecture list; ⚠️ analytical).
**Links:** IR-1015, IR-1016, IR-1107, IR-1111, IR-1126.
**Trap:** Presenting the stack as one body. IORA, IONS and IFC-IOR are three different mechanisms.
**Mains hook:** Use the chain: interest → rules → awareness → partnerships → island trust → outcome.
**Back:** *"Sea lanes, doctrine, institutions, island partners, outside powers."*

### Flashcard IR-1106 — Strait of Malacca: more than half of India's trade (Tharoor)
**Front:** What share of India's trade does Tharoor say transits the Strait of Malacca, and why does it matter?
**Definition (learn):** Tharoor notes that "more than half of India's trade" traverses the Strait of Malacca, making sea-lane security a direct economic-security interest, not only a naval concern (✅ owner 04 quoting Tharoor).
**Breakdown:**
- Chokepoint: Strait of Malacca.
- Share: more than half of India's trade (Tharoor's figure; period-dated).
- Consequence: sea-lane security is first-order economic security.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §3 and §6, advanced §3 (✅ owner 04 quoting Tharoor).
**Links:** IR-1105, IR-1114 (Malacca and Singapore fusion), IR-1152 (route risk).
**Trap:** Quoting the figure as a current statistic without dating it. Use the owner's attribution.
**Mains hook:** Use the chokepoint dependence to motivate maritime domain awareness; qualify with a current trade-data source.
**Back:** *"More than half of India's trade passes Malacca (Tharoor, dated)."*

### Flashcard IR-1107 — Indian Ocean Rim Association (IORA): membership, secretariat and India's chair
**Front:** What are IORA's membership, dialogue partners, secretariat and India's chair status?
**Definition (learn):** IORA is a regional intergovernmental organisation with 23 member states and 12 dialogue partners, headquartered in Ebène, Mauritius. India holds the chair for 2025–27 (assumed November 2025).
**Breakdown:**
- Members: 23.
- Dialogue partners: 12 (one passage on Wikipedia mentions 10; the infobox and membership section give 12).
- Secretariat: Ebène, Mauritius.
- Origin: launched in March 1995 as the Indian Ocean Rim Initiative; charter 1997 as IOR-ARC (✅ Wikipedia).
- India: current chair (✅ Wikipedia; 2025–27 per owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §2, §4 and §6 (✅ owner 04; ✅ Wikipedia for founding and membership).
**Links:** IR-1108, IR-1109, IR-1110, IR-1145 (chairship trap).
**Trap:** Chairship equals control. IORA is consensus-based; a chairship gives agenda-setting only.
**Mains hook:** Name IORA's function (forum for littoral economic and security dialogue) rather than asserting maritime cooperation in the abstract.
**Back:** *"Twenty-three members, twelve partners, Ebène, India chairs 2025–27."*

### Flashcard IR-1108 — Indian Ocean Naval Symposium (IONS): voluntary navy forum, 2008
**Front:** What is IONS, when was it founded, and when did India assume its chair?
**Definition (learn):** IONS is a voluntary initiative, established in 2008, to increase maritime cooperation among the navies of Indian Ocean littoral states. India assumed the IONS chair on 20 February 2026 (owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Type: naval cooperation symposium (not an intergovernmental organisation).
- Founded: 2008.
- Chair: India from 20 February 2026.
- Role: operationalises naval cooperation among littoral navies.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §2 and §6 (✅ owner 04; 📰 chair date per owner, not re-verified).
**Links:** IR-1107, IR-1110, IR-1111.
**Trap:** Treating IONS as the same as IORA.
**Mains hook:** IONS is the naval layer of the stack; IORA is the political forum. Qualify with a chairship's limits.
**Back:** *"Navies, not ministers: IONS, 2008, India chairs from February 2026."*

### Flashcard IR-1109 — Information Fusion Centre-Indian Ocean Region (IFC-IOR): 2018, Gurugram
**Front:** What is IFC-IOR, where is it hosted, and what is its reach?
**Definition (learn):** A regional maritime-security centre hosted by the Indian Navy, established in 2018 in Gurugram, for maritime domain awareness and information sharing. As stated in November 2025 it collaborated with 25 partner countries and hosted International Liaison Officers from 15 countries.
**Breakdown:**
- Established: 2018.
- Host: Indian Navy.
- Location: Gurugram.
- Reach (November 2025): 25 partner countries; ILOs from 15 countries.
- Role: information fusion for maritime domain awareness.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §2, §6 and advanced §4 (✅ owner 04; 📰 November 2025 figures per owner).
**Links:** IR-1114 (IPMDA nodes), IR-1105, IR-1113.
**Trap:** Confusing IFC-IOR with Singapore's Information Fusion Centre. They are different nodes.
**Mains hook:** IFC-IOR is India's institutional contribution to a wider data architecture; qualify that India contributes reach more than the sensor layer.
**Back:** *"Gurugram 2018: twenty-five partners, fifteen liaison countries."*

### Flashcard IR-1110 — Sagar Prahari Bal: Indian Navy force raised in 2009
**Front:** What is Sagar Prahari Bal and when was it raised?
**Definition (learn):** A specialised Indian Navy force raised in 2009 to protect naval bases and adjacent vulnerable areas and points.
**Breakdown:**
- Raised: 2009.
- Role: protection of naval bases and vulnerable points.
- Type: internal capacity-building instrument supporting the diplomatic architecture.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §4 and advanced §4 (✅ owner 04).
**Links:** IR-1109, IR-1105, IR-1148 (Quad supplements, not replaces).
**Trap:** Treating Sagar Prahari Bal as a multinational force.
**Mains hook:** Domestic capacity reduces vulnerability independent of partner choices; qualify that it is a national force.
**Back:** *"Sagar Prahari Bal: 2009, national, for bases and points."*

### Flashcard IR-1111 — Quad: four members, non-treaty coalition; Tokyo 2022 and New Delhi 2026
**Front:** Who are the Quad's members, what is its legal character, and when did its last two leaders' and foreign ministers' meetings occur?
**Definition (learn):** The Quadrilateral Security Dialogue is a non-treaty minilateral coalition of Australia, India, Japan and the United States. Its fourth leaders' summit was held in Tokyo on 24 May 2022. The 11th Foreign Ministers' Meeting was held in New Delhi on 26 May 2026.
**Breakdown:**
- Members: Australia, India, Japan, United States.
- Legal character: non-treaty consultation and coordination.
- 24 May 2022: Tokyo leaders' summit (✅ Wikipedia).
- 26 May 2026: New Delhi FMM; launched IPMSC, Critical Minerals Framework, Ports of the Future Partnership with Fiji (✅ Wikipedia).
- Positions: free and open Indo-Pacific; UNCLOS-consistent maritime order.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §4, §4A, §8 (✅ owner 04; ✅ Wikipedia for dates and membership).
**Links:** IR-1113 (IPMDA), IR-1115 (IPMSC), IR-1121 (AUKUS), IR-1148 (asymmetry).
**Trap:** Calling the Quad a NATO-like military alliance or a trade bloc. The owner says it is neither.
**Mains hook:** Quad is a coordinating minilateral; qualify its asymmetry of treaty ties.
**Back:** *"Four states, no treaty: Tokyo 2022, New Delhi 2026."*

### Flashcard IR-1112 — Quad 2020 PYQ: a trade bloc or a military alliance? (route)
**Front:** What is the 2020 GS-II question on the Quad, and how should it be answered?
**Definition (learn):** The 2020 GS-II question states that the Quadrilateral Security Dialogue "is transforming itself into a trade bloc from a military alliance, in present times" and asks students to discuss.
**Breakdown:**
- Route: use this file for the maritime and Indo-Pacific function.
- Correction: the Quad is neither a treaty alliance nor a trade bloc.
- Owner 10 owns the taxonomy; this file owns the maritime function.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §9 (✅ owner 04 ledger).
**Links:** IR-1111, IR-1148, IR-1121.
**Trap:** Accepting the "trade bloc" premise.
**Mains hook:** Reject both premises and describe the coalition's actual function.
**Back:** *"Neither alliance nor trade bloc, a maritime coalition."*

### Flashcard IR-1113 — IPMDA: Quad's Indo-Pacific Partnership for Maritime Domain Awareness (Tokyo, 24 May 2022)
**Front:** What is IPMDA, when was it launched, and what data does it use?
**Definition (learn):** The Indo-Pacific Partnership for Maritime Domain Awareness was announced by Quad leaders at the Tokyo Summit on 24 May 2022, with Quad countries committing to investment over five years. It offers a near-real-time, integrated and cost-effective maritime domain awareness picture, using commercially available, unclassified data (AIS tracking and commercial satellite RF; later analyses add SAR fusion).
**Breakdown:**
- Launch: 24 May 2022, Tokyo.
- Investment: five years.
- Data: commercial, unclassified AIS and RF (and later SAR).
- Coverage: Pacific Islands, Southeast Asia and Indian Ocean region.
- Aims: track "dark shipping", climate and HADR response, protect fisheries.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 advanced §4A (✅ owner 04 quoting the 2022 official announcement; ✅ Wikipedia for the summit date context).
**Links:** IR-1114, IR-1115, IR-1116, IR-1109.
**Trap:** IPMDA as a treaty or a joint military command. It is a data-sharing initiative.
**Mains hook:** Explain IPMDA as a public good with a commercial-data basis; qualify its funding horizon and its Quad-branded reception.
**Back:** *"Tokyo 2022, commercial data, five-year pledge, dark ships in sight."*

### Flashcard IR-1114 — IPMDA fusion-centre nodes and the Pacific trap
**Front:** Which regional fusion centres does IPMDA use, and what must not be merged?
**Definition (learn):** IPMDA extends support through existing regional fusion centres rather than creating new bodies: IFC-IOR (Gurugram, Indian Navy); the Information Fusion Centre (Singapore); the Pacific Islands Forum Fisheries Agency (Solomon Islands); and the Pacific Fusion Centre (Vanuatu), the last two with Australian support.
**Breakdown:**
- IFC-IOR: Gurugram, India.
- Information Fusion Centre: Singapore.
- Pacific Islands Forum Fisheries Agency: Solomon Islands.
- Pacific Fusion Centre: Vanuatu.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 advanced §4A and basic §6 (✅ owner 04 quoting the 2022 announcement).
**Links:** IR-1109, IR-1113, IR-1149.
**Trap:** Merging the Pacific Islands Forum Fisheries Agency (Solomon Islands) with the Pacific Fusion Centre (Vanuatu). The owner says they are distinct; and IPMDA did not create new centres.
**Mains hook:** The bridge runs through one Indian institution (IFC-IOR), not through a fused doctrine.
**Back:** *"Gurugram, Singapore, Solomons, Vanuatu: four nodes, none new."*

### Flashcard IR-1115 — IPMSC (26 May 2026) and the IPMDA expansion toward a Common Operating Picture
**Front:** What did the 11th Quad Foreign Ministers' Meeting launch on 26 May 2026, and how does it differ from IPMDA?
**Definition (learn):** Quad partners launched the first-ever Indo-Pacific Maritime Surveillance Collaboration (IPMSC), leveraging maritime-surveillance capabilities with an initial focus on the Indian Ocean Region through expert exchanges and tabletop exercises, while expanding IPMDA toward a comprehensive Common Operating Picture (COP). India will host the next Quad-at-Sea Ship Observer Mission.
**Breakdown:**
- IPMSC (2026): newer, IOR-focused surveillance collaboration track.
- IPMDA (2022): data-fusion and awareness initiative.
- COP: the expansion goal.
- Ship Observer Mission: India hosts next.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §4A (📰 owner from US State Department fact sheet; ✅ Wikipedia records the IPMSC launch at the 26 May 2026 New Delhi FMM).
**Links:** IR-1113, IR-1116, IR-1111.
**Trap:** Using IPMDA and IPMSC as the same name. They are related but not identical instruments.
**Mains hook:** Evidence of deepening, not completed, integration.
**Back:** *"IPMDA 2022 data, IPMSC 2026 surveillance, COP the goal."*

### Flashcard IR-1116 — The SAGAR-Quad bridge: critical assessment of IPMDA (2026 GS-II Q9, 10 marks)
**Front:** Does IPMDA bridge the gap between India's SAGAR vision and the Quad's Indo-Pacific strategy?
**Definition (learn):** The 2026 question states that IPMDA "bridges the gap between India's SAGAR vision and the Quad's collective Indo-Pacific strategy" and asks for a critical assessment focusing on IPMDA (10 marks, 150 words). The owner's verdict: a genuine structural link runs through one Indian institution (IFC-IOR), but calling IPMDA a full bridge between SAGAR and Quad strategy overstates the case.
**Breakdown:**
- Where the bridge holds: IFC-IOR is SAGAR-era and an IPMDA delivery node.
- Where it overstates: SAGAR is unilateral and Indian-Ocean-specific; Quad is four-country, wider Indo-Pacific.
- Capability asymmetry: sensors and bulk of investment are Quad (largely US-technology) contributions.
- Balanced verdict: IPMDA connects an institution more than it fuses two strategies.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §4A and §5 (✅ owner 04 OCR-verified demand; ✅ owner 04 for analysis).
**Links:** IR-1015, IR-1109, IR-1113, IR-1115, IR-1117.
**Trap:** Answering "yes, it bridges" or "no, it doesn't" without the qualifier.
**Mains hook:** Ten-mark spine: define IPMDA; state the genuine link; give the qualifier; name the 2026 update as deepening, not completion; close with a qualified verdict.
**Back:** *"Real link through IFC-IOR, overstated as a bridge of doctrines."*

### Flashcard IR-1117 — Indo-Pacific Oceans Initiative (IPOI): 4 November 2019, seven pillars
**Front:** When and where did India announce IPOI, and what are its seven pillars?
**Definition (learn):** India announced the Indo-Pacific Oceans Initiative at the 14th East Asia Summit in Bangkok on 4 November 2019. It converts SAGAR's vision into voluntary, practical, partner-led cooperation across the maritime commons.
**Breakdown:**
- 1. Maritime security.
- 2. Maritime ecology.
- 3. Maritime resources.
- 4. Capacity building and resource sharing.
- 5. Disaster risk reduction and management.
- 6. Science, technology and academic cooperation.
- 7. Trade, connectivity and maritime transport.
- Limit: not a treaty, alliance or centralised funding body.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §4 (✅ owner 04).
**Links:** IR-1015, IR-1118, IR-1119.
**Trap:** Treating IPOI as a funding body or a Quad product. It is India's voluntary initiative.
**Mains hook:** IPOI is India's own Indo-Pacific pillar; qualify its voluntary character.
**Back:** *"Bangkok 2019, seven pillars, voluntary."*

### Flashcard IR-1118 — ASEAN centrality and the ASEAN Outlook on the Indo-Pacific
**Front:** How does India treat ASEAN in its Indo-Pacific approach?
**Definition (learn):** India treats ASEAN as the central regional architecture and describes IPOI and the ASEAN Outlook on the Indo-Pacific as complementary.
**Breakdown:**
- Position: ASEAN centrality.
- Complement: IPOI and ASEAN Outlook.
- Implication: India's policy is broader than a China-containment coalition.
- Limit: ASEAN consensus and internal diversity slow common action.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §4 (✅ owner 04).
**Links:** IR-1117, IR-1119, IR-1121.
**Trap:** Describing ASEAN as a bloc that endorsed the Quad. The owner rejects that reading.
**Mains hook:** Use ASEAN centrality to show that India's Indo-Pacific is not a bloc; qualify with ASEAN's internal diversity.
**Back:** *"ASEAN at the centre, IPOI beside it."*

### Flashcard IR-1119 — East Asia Summit vs ASEAN Regional Forum (comparison)
**Front:** How do the East Asia Summit and the ASEAN Regional Forum differ?
**Definition (learn):** The East Asia Summit is a leaders-led strategic forum. The ASEAN Regional Forum provides wider confidence-building and security dialogue.
**Breakdown:**
- EAS: leaders-level, strategic.
- ARF: confidence-building and security dialogue, wider.
- Common limit: dialogue forums have weak enforcement.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §4 (✅ owner 04).
**Links:** IR-1117, IR-1118.
**Trap:** Option sets that describe ARF as leaders-led.
**Mains hook:** India combines broad institutions with smaller minilaterals; qualify that dialogue lacks enforcement.
**Back:** *"EAS leads, ARF talks."*

### Flashcard IR-1120 — Exercise Malabar: India-US naval exercise, expanded to Japan and Australia
**Front:** What is Exercise Malabar, and what does it not create?
**Definition (learn):** The India-US naval exercise, which expanded to include Japan and, from 2020, regular Australian participation. It provides interoperability evidence among all four Quad states.
**Breakdown:**
- Core: India and US.
- Expansion: Japan; Australia from 2020 on a regular basis.
- Significance: interoperability.
- Limit: an exercise creates no collective-defence obligation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §4 (✅ owner 04).
**Links:** IR-1111, IR-1072, IR-1116.
**Trap:** Treating an exercise as an alliance commitment.
**Mains hook:** Exercises build interoperability and deterrence; qualify that they do not create obligations.
**Back:** *"Malabar grows; obligations do not."*

### Flashcard IR-1121 — AUKUS: Australia, UK and US (15 September 2021)
**Front:** What is AUKUS, when was it announced, and what is its first initiative?
**Definition (learn):** AUKUS is the enhanced trilateral security partnership of Australia, the United Kingdom and the United States, announced on 15 September 2021. Its first initiative supports Australia's acquisition of conventionally armed, nuclear-powered submarines. The advanced-capabilities track covers cyber, AI, quantum and additional undersea capabilities.
**Breakdown:**
- Members: Australia, UK, US.
- Date: 15 September 2021.
- Pillar 1: nuclear-powered submarines (conventionally armed).
- Pillar 2: advanced capabilities (cyber, AI, quantum, undersea).
- India: not a member.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §4 (✅ owner 04).
**Links:** IR-1122, IR-1123, IR-1124, IR-1125.
**Trap:** Calling India an AUKUS member. It is not.
**Mains hook:** AUKUS shows the overlapping instruments of the Indo-Pacific order; qualify that it should not be reduced to a binary pro/anti position.
**Back:** *"Three signatories, September 2021, submarines first."*

### Flashcard IR-1122 — AUKUS submarine pathway (March 2023): rotational presence, Virginia-class sales, SSN-AUKUS
**Front:** What timeline does the March 2023 submarine pathway envisage?
**Definition (learn):** Rotational UK-US submarine presence in Australia from as early as 2027; sale of Virginia-class submarines from the early 2030s subject to US approval; later Australian-built SSN-AUKUS boats.
**Breakdown:**
- From 2027: rotational UK-US presence.
- Early 2030s: Virginia-class sales (subject to US approval).
- Later: Australian-built SSN-AUKUS.
- Constraint: industrial capacity, approvals, political commitment.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §4 (✅ owner 04).
**Links:** IR-1121, IR-1124.
**Trap:** Treating the schedule as fixed; the owner says it depends on approvals and capacity.
**Mains hook:** Deterrence and technology integration, qualified by schedule risk.
**Back:** *"2027 visits, 2030s sales, later Australian boats."*

### Flashcard IR-1123 — AUKUS non-proliferation: Article 14 IAEA safeguards still under negotiation (November 2025)
**Front:** What non-proliferation question does AUKUS raise, and what was its status in November 2025?
**Definition (learn):** Australia remains a non-nuclear-weapon state. An Article 14 safeguards arrangement with the IAEA for naval nuclear propulsion was still under negotiation in November 2025 (owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Australia: non-nuclear-weapon state.
- Issue: safeguards for naval nuclear propulsion without proscribed military nuclear use.
- Status (Nov 2025): negotiation ongoing.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §4 (⚠️ owner-stated; not re-verified).
**Links:** IR-1121, IR-1122.
**Trap:** Saying the safeguards are complete, or that the negotiation proves violation. Neither.
**Mains hook:** AUKUS tests whether safeguards can protect non-proliferation; qualify that negotiation is neither violation nor removal of risk.
**Back:** *"Article 14 still in talks in November 2025."*

### Flashcard IR-1124 — AUKUS alliance-management cost: EUR 555 million settlement (June 2022)
**Front:** What diplomatic cost followed the AUKUS submarine decision?
**Definition (learn):** Australia's cancellation of the French Naval Group submarine programme produced a EUR 555 million settlement in June 2022 (owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Programme: French Naval Group submarines (cancelled).
- Settlement: EUR 555 million, June 2022.
- Lesson: minilateral capability gains can impose costs on other partners.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §4 (✅ owner 04).
**Links:** IR-1121, IR-1118.
**Trap:** Treating the settlement as the full strategic cost. The owner says it does not measure it.
**Mains hook:** Diplomatic cost is a real part of minilateral capability gains.
**Back:** *"EUR 555 million, June 2022: the price of a switch."*

### Flashcard IR-1125 — Colombo Security Conclave: members, observer and guest (20 November 2025)
**Front:** Who were the verified members, observer and guest at the 20 November 2025 NSA-level meeting of the Colombo Security Conclave?
**Definition (learn):** The Colombo Security Conclave is a maritime-security grouping. At the NSA-level meeting in New Delhi on 20 November 2025 its verified members were Bangladesh, India, Maldives, Mauritius and Sri Lanka. Seychelles attended as an observer and Malaysia as a guest. On 9 February 2026 India welcomed Seychelles' decision to become a full member.
**Breakdown:**
- Members (Nov 2025): Bangladesh, India, Maldives, Mauritius, Sri Lanka.
- Observer: Seychelles.
- Guest: Malaysia.
- 9 February 2026: India welcomed Seychelles' decision to become a full member; no effective accession date recorded.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §4 and advanced §4 and §8 (📰 owner; ⚠️ not re-fetched; Wikipedia page not available at check).
**Links:** IR-1105, IR-1045, IR-1151.
**Trap:** Describing Seychelles as a member in 2025. It was an observer then.
**Mains hook:** Distinguish member, observer, guest and "decided to join".
**Back:** *"Five members, Seychelles watching, Malaysia visiting."*

### Flashcard IR-1126 — Indian Ocean Conference: track-1.5 convenings in Muscat and Port Louis
**Front:** Where and when were the 8th and 9th editions of the Indian Ocean Conference held?
**Definition (learn):** The Indian Ocean Conference is a track-1.5 regional convening. The 8th edition was held in Muscat on 16 February 2025; the 9th in Port Louis on 10 April 2026.
**Breakdown:**
- 8th: Muscat, 16 February 2025.
- 9th: Port Louis, 10 April 2026.
- Type: track-1.5 (officials and non-officials).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §4 (📰 owner; ⚠️ not re-verified).
**Links:** IR-1107, IR-1125.
**Trap:** Mixing editions and venues.
**Mains hook:** Track-1.5 forums support dialogue without binding outcomes.
**Back:** *"Muscat February 2025, Port Louis April 2026."*

### Flashcard IR-1127 — UNCLOS: signed 10 December 1982, ratified 29 June 1995; India's domestic maritime-zones law (1976)
**Front:** When did India sign and ratify UNCLOS, and which domestic Act defines India's maritime zones?
**Definition (learn):** The UN Convention on the Law of the Sea is the treaty framework for territorial waters, EEZs and high-seas navigation. India signed it on 10 December 1982 and ratified it on 29 June 1995. The domestic Territorial Waters, Continental Shelf, Exclusive Economic Zone and Other Maritime Zones Act, 1976 defines India's zones.
**Breakdown:**
- Signed: 10 December 1982.
- Ratified: 29 June 1995.
- Domestic law: Act of 1976 (zones).
- Owner: treaty chronology belongs to World History and Polity.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §2 and §6 (✅ owner 04).
**Links:** IR-1128, IR-1129, IR-1130, IR-1131.
**Trap:** Confusing signature with ratification (see IR-1133 for BBNJ).
**Mains hook:** Legal precision: cite ratification, not "India supports UNCLOS".
**Back:** *"Signed 1982, ratified 1995, zones by the 1976 Act."*

### Flashcard IR-1128 — UNCLOS Article 3 (12 nm territorial sea) and Article 57 (200 nm EEZ): a comparison pair
**Front:** What do UNCLOS Articles 3 and 57 control?
**Definition (learn):** Article 3 permits a coastal state to establish a territorial sea up to, but not exceeding, 12 nautical miles from Convention-compliant baselines, with sovereignty subject to UNCLOS. Article 57 states that the EEZ shall not extend beyond 200 nautical miles from the baselines used to measure the territorial sea. The EEZ confers specified sovereign rights and jurisdiction, not territorial sovereignty.
**Breakdown:**
- Article 3: territorial sea, maximum 12 nm, sovereignty.
- Article 57: EEZ outer limit 200 nm, specified rights.
- Comparison: sovereignty (territorial sea) vs specified rights (EEZ).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §4 treaty table and §6 (✅ UNCLOS text as quoted by owner; ⚠️ not separately fetched).
**Links:** IR-1127, IR-1129, IR-1130.
**Trap:** Treating the EEZ as sovereign territory. The owner says the EEZ confers specified rights only.
**Mains hook:** Use Articles 3 and 57 to frame any maritime-zone question; the owner withholds option mapping for objective questions.
**Back:** *"Twelve for territory, two hundred for rights."*

### Flashcard IR-1129 — UNCLOS innocent passage: Articles 17, 18 and 19
**Front:** What do Articles 17, 18 and 19 of UNCLOS say about innocent passage?
**Definition (learn):** Article 17 gives ships of all States, coastal or land-locked, the right of innocent passage through the territorial sea. Article 18 requires passage to be continuous and expeditious; stopping or anchoring is covered only when incidental, required by force majeure or distress, or needed to assist persons, ships or aircraft in danger. Article 19 makes passage innocent only while it is not prejudicial to the coastal State's peace, good order or security.
**Breakdown:**
- Art 17: right of all States' ships.
- Art 18: continuous and expeditious passage.
- Art 19: non-prejudicial; weapons exercises, intelligence collection, fishing, research and wilful serious pollution can make passage non-innocent.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §4 treaty table and §6 (✅ UNCLOS text as quoted by owner).
**Links:** IR-1128, IR-1130 (trap), IR-1127.
**Trap:** Port calls. The owner notes a port call can still be passage under Article 18's terms.
**Mains hook:** Innocent passage is a conditional navigation right, not an unlimited one.
**Back:** *"Ships pass, continuously; not prejudicing peace; no fishing, no spying."*

### Flashcard IR-1130 — Trap: innocent passage allows any activity in the territorial sea or the EEZ
**Front:** Does innocent passage permit any activity by a foreign ship in the territorial sea, or apply because the EEZ is sovereign territory?
**Definition (learn):** No on both counts. Articles 18–19 confine innocent passage to continuous, expeditious and non-prejudicial passage. The right concerns the territorial sea; the EEZ carries specified sovereign rights, not territorial sovereignty.
**Breakdown:**
- Wrong: any activity is allowed.
- Right: fishing, research, weapons exercises and wilful serious pollution make passage non-innocent.
- Wrong: EEZ is sovereign territory.
- Right: the EEZ confers specified rights.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §7 (✅ owner 04).
**Links:** IR-1129, IR-1128.
**Trap:** Option sets that combine both errors.
**Mains hook:** State the limits of passage before defending navigation freedom.
**Back:** *"Passage is conditional; the EEZ is rights, not land."*

### Flashcard IR-1131 — India-Bangladesh Annex VII maritime-boundary award (7 July 2014) and the MEA South China Sea statement (12 July 2016)
**Front:** What do these two dates show about India's use of UNCLOS dispute machinery?
**Definition (learn):** The India-Bangladesh maritime-boundary award was delivered on 7 July 2014 by a tribunal administered by the Permanent Court of Arbitration under UNCLOS Annex VII; India accepted it. On 12 July 2016 MEA noted the South China Sea Annex VII award and called for freedom of navigation and overflight, peaceful settlement, self-restraint and respect for UNCLOS, without pronouncing on the merits.
**Breakdown:**
- 7 July 2014: India-Bangladesh award (PCA, Annex VII), accepted by India.
- 12 July 2016: MEA statement on the South China Sea award.
- Stance: user and defender of the treaty order; no merits ruling on the SCS.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §4 and §6 (✅ owner 04).
**Links:** IR-1127, IR-1133.
**Trap:** Saying India "endorsed" the SCS award on its merits. The owner says India did not pronounce on the merits.
**Mains hook:** India as rules-user: accept what suits its interests, note process rules.
**Back:** *"2014 accepted, 2016 called for UNCLOS, no verdict on merits."*

### Flashcard IR-1132 — Maritime-claim disputes and the Senkaku question (2022 Prelims Q86)
**Front:** What is the 2022 Prelims routing on the Senkaku Islands, and what status applies?
**Definition (learn):** 2022 Prelims GS-I Q86 asks about the Senkaku Islands maritime territorial dispute in the East China Sea. The routing ledger records the key status as not available. No answer is inferred.
**Breakdown:**
- Subject: territorial dispute in the East China Sea.
- Key status: not available.
- Study: the dispute's parties and the legal basis claimed by each.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic PYQ integration block (✅ owner 04 ledger).
**Links:** IR-1131, IR-1128.
**Trap:** Inferring a party's claim from India's position; India's stated position is not a Senkaku position.
**Mains hook:** Use it as a statement-level check on sovereignty claims, not as an India-specific issue.
**Back:** *"Senkaku: East China Sea, key not held."*

### Flashcard IR-1133 — BBNJ Agreement: adoption, entry into force (17 January 2026) and India as signatory, not party
**Front:** When was the BBNJ Agreement adopted, opened and brought into force, and what is India's status?
**Definition (learn):** The Biodiversity Beyond National Jurisdiction Agreement ("High Seas Treaty") implements UNCLOS on marine biodiversity of areas beyond national jurisdiction. It was adopted on 19 June 2023, opened for signature on 20 September 2023, and entered into force on 17 January 2026, after the 60th ratification (owner: deposited 19 September 2025). India signed on 25 September 2024 and had not deposited ratification as of 5 October 2026 (owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Adopted: 19 June 2023.
- Open for signature: 20 September 2023.
- Accession possible from 21 September 2025 (✅ UN BBNJ page).
- In force: 17 January 2026 (✅ UN BBNJ page; ✅ Wikipedia).
- India: signatory; not yet party (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §2, §3 and §6 (✅ UN BBNJ page for adoption, opening, entry into force; ⚠️ India's status per owner, not confirmed by the UN page fetched).
**Links:** IR-1134, IR-1135, IR-1127.
**Trap:** Saying India is a party because it signed.
**Mains hook:** Cite "signatory, not party" with the date; the owner notes India cannot vote at the Conference of the Parties as a non-party (⚠️ inference).
**Back:** *"Adopted 2023, in force January 2026, India signed, not yet party."*

### Flashcard IR-1134 — Trap: signature is not ratification or a seat at the Conference of the Parties
**Front:** Does signing the BBNJ Agreement make India a party or give it a seat at the Conference of the Parties?
**Definition (learn):** No. Signature expresses consent to the text and an obligation not to defeat its object. Party status requires ratification. The owner says a non-party cannot vote at the COP that will design implementation.
**Breakdown:**
- Signature: consent to text; not binding as party.
- Ratification: party status.
- COP: party-only voting.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §7 and advanced §8 (✅ owner 04; COP voting is owner inference, ⚠️).
**Links:** IR-1133.
**Trap:** Option sets equating signing with party status.
**Mains hook:** Legal precision about the signature-ratification gap.
**Back:** *"Signed is not sealed."*

### Flashcard IR-1135 — International Maritime Organization: 1948 Convention, 1958 in force, and its principal instruments
**Front:** When was the IMO Convention adopted and entered into force, and what are its key instruments?
**Definition (learn):** The IMO Convention was adopted in 1948 and entered into force in 1958. It is the UN specialised agency for global shipping standards. Its Assembly includes all members; its Council is the executive organ; core committees are MSC, MEPC, Legal, Technical Cooperation and Facilitation.
**Breakdown:**
- Adopted: 1948. In force: 1958.
- SOLAS: safety of life at sea.
- MARPOL: ship-source pollution.
- Ballast Water Management Convention: in force 2017.
- Hong Kong ship-recycling Convention: in force 26 June 2025.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §4 (✅ owner 04).
**Links:** IR-1136, IR-1137, IR-1138, IR-1142.
**Trap:** Confusing SOLAS (safety) with MARPOL (pollution).
**Mains hook:** IMO converts maritime risk into common rules; qualify with flag, port and coastal implementation.
**Back:** *"1948 adopted, 1958 in force, SOLAS for safety, MARPOL for pollution."*

### Flashcard IR-1136 — IMO sulphur limit (1 January 2020): 3.50% to 0.50% m/m outside emission-control areas
**Front:** What changed on 1 January 2020 under IMO rules, and what limits apply?
**Definition (learn):** From 1 January 2020 the global sulphur limit outside emission-control areas fell from 3.50% to 0.50% m/m.
**Breakdown:**
- Before: 3.50% m/m.
- After (1 January 2020): 0.50% m/m.
- Scope: outside emission-control areas.
- Limit: compliance and fuel-cost burdens vary.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §4 (✅ owner 04).
**Links:** IR-1135, IR-1137.
