**Trap:** Confusing the global limit with the emission-control-area limit; the owner gives only the global one.
**Mains hook:** A universal technical standard reduces pollution across a mobile industry.
**Back:** *"Three-and-a-half to half, January 2020."*

### Flashcard IR-1137 — IMO climate governance: 2023 GHG Strategy and the Net-Zero Framework not adopted (October 2025)
**Front:** What is the IMO's 2023 GHG Strategy target, and what happened to the Net-Zero Framework?
**Definition (learn):** The 2023 IMO GHG Strategy aims at net-zero emissions from international shipping by or around 2050, with 2030 and 2040 checkpoints. The proposed Net-Zero Framework was approved at committee level but not adopted in October 2025; talks were adjourned.
**Breakdown:**
- Target: net-zero by or around 2050.
- Checkpoints: 2030 and 2040.
- Net-Zero Framework: approved at committee level; not adopted October 2025.
- Status: talks adjourned.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §4 (✅ owner 04; ⚠️ not re-verified).
**Links:** IR-1135, IR-1136.
**Trap:** Describing the framework as adopted.
**Mains hook:** IMO is the principal sectoral arena; qualify that ambition and burden-sharing remain contested.
**Back:** *"2050 target, framework approved in committee, not adopted October 2025."*

### Flashcard IR-1138 — India on the IMO Council (Category (b), 2026–27 biennium)
**Front:** What IMO Council category did India win for 2026–27, and what does it mean?
**Definition (learn):** India was elected to IMO Council Category (b) for the 2026–27 biennium, representing states with a major interest in international seaborne trade.
**Breakdown:**
- Category (b): states with major interest in international seaborne trade.
- Significance: rule-shaping access.
- Limit: membership does not guarantee outcomes.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §4 (📰 owner; ⚠️ not re-verified).
**Links:** IR-1135, IR-1137.
**Trap:** Confusing Council category with a chairship or a veto.
**Mains hook:** Use Council membership as evidence of maritime stake and rule-shaping access.
**Back:** *"Category (b) for 2026–27: seaborne-trade stake."*

### Flashcard IR-1139 — Maldives: geostrategic importance for India in the maritime frame (2024 GS-II Q20)
**Front:** Why is Maldives important to India in the owner's maritime frame?
**Definition (learn):** Maldives' salience is primarily maritime: its location relates to sea lanes carrying trade and energy flows and to international competition in the Indian Ocean. The owner treats it in this file rather than the general neighbourhood frame.
**Breakdown:**
- Location: near key Indian Ocean sea lanes (Geography owns route detail).
- Concrete instrument: India-Maldives Joint Vision (7 October 2024; IR-1045).
- Backdrop: SAGAR, MAHASAGAR, IORA, IONS, IFC-IOR.
- Competition: external naval presence (Djibouti and Gwadar-type bases).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §5 and §9 (✅ owner 04; ✅ owner 02 cross-reference).
**Links:** IR-1045, IR-1046, IR-1140, IR-1151.
**Trap:** Treating Maldives as purely bilateral (the owner's trap).
**Mains hook:** Connect Maldives to concrete sea-lane and energy data; qualify island-state agency.
**Back:** *"Maldives: sea lanes first, neighbourhood second."*

### Flashcard IR-1140 — PYQ route: Maldives' geopolitical and geostrategic importance (2024 GS-II Q20, 15 marks)
**Front:** What must a strong answer to the 2024 GS-II Q20 include?
**Definition (learn):** The question asks to discuss Maldives' geopolitical and geostrategic importance for India with a focus on global trade and energy flows, and how the relationship affects India's maritime security and regional stability amid international competition (15 marks, 250 words).
**Breakdown:**
- 1. Location astride key sea lanes (trade and energy).
- 2. Joint Vision of 7 October 2024 as the instrument.
- 3. SAGAR, MAHASAGAR and IORA/IONS/IFC-IOR as backdrop; India's IORA (2025–27) and IONS (from 20 February 2026) chairships.
- 4. External-power naval presence (Djibouti, Gwadar-type) as "international competition"; no overstated "net security provider" claim.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §9, advanced §10 (✅ owner 04 ledger).
**Links:** IR-1139, IR-1146, IR-1147.
**Trap:** Leaving out the external-competition dimension.
**Mains hook:** Answer both the importance and the effect on maritime security.
**Back:** *"Sea lanes, Joint Vision, institutions, competition, no overclaim."*

### Flashcard IR-1141 — PYQ routes: 2020 defence deals and Indo-Pacific stability; 2021 AUKUS
**Front:** What do the 2020 and 2021 GS-II Q20 routings ask?
**Definition (learn):** 2020 GS-II Q20 asks to discuss Indo-US and Indo-Russian defence deals and Indo-Pacific stability (15 marks, 250 words). 2021 GS-II Q20 asks to discuss the strength and impact of AUKUS in the Indo-Pacific and existing regional partnerships (15 marks, 250 words); the printed stem's opening clause is recorded as truncated in the scan.
**Breakdown:**
- 2020: LEMOA, COMCASA, BECA (IR-1072); S-400 (IR-1076); stability via crisis communication and non-alliance autonomy.
- 2021: AUKUS (IR-1121 to IR-1124); partnership impact on India.
- Note: the owner records the 2021 stem as truncated in the scan.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic PYQ and §11A (✅ owner 04 ledger).
**Links:** IR-1072, IR-1076, IR-1121, IR-1125.
**Trap:** Treating AUKUS as an alliance India joined.
**Mains hook:** Stability needs crisis communication and non-alliance autonomy.
**Back:** *"2020 deals and stability, 2021 AUKUS strength and impact."*

### Flashcard IR-1142 — PYQ route: role of the IMO in environment and maritime safety (2023 GS-II Q20, 15 marks)
**Front:** What does the 2023 GS-II Q20 ask about the IMO, and what evidence should be used?
**Definition (learn):** The question asks to discuss the role of the IMO in protecting the environment and maritime safety (15 marks, 250 words).
**Breakdown:**
- Evidence: SOLAS, MARPOL, ballast water, Hong Kong Convention, sulphur limit.
- Limit: enforcement depends on flag, port and coastal states.
- Climate: 2023 GHG Strategy and the framework not adopted in October 2025.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic PYQ integration (✅ owner 04 ledger).
**Links:** IR-1135, IR-1136, IR-1137, IR-1138.
**Trap:** Describing IMO rules as enforceable by IMO itself.
**Mains hook:** Use the instruments and the implementation gap.
**Back:** *"Safety, pollution, climate: rules without police."*

### Flashcard IR-1143 — PYQ route: Quad, a trade bloc or a military alliance (2020 GS-II)
**Front:** Which owner carries the grouping classification for the 2020 Quad question?
**Definition (learn):** This file carries the maritime function; the owner for grouping classification corrects the premise that the Quad is either a treaty alliance or a trade bloc.
**Breakdown:**
- Maritime function: this file.
- Taxonomy: the groupings owner (owner topic 10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §9 (✅ owner 04 ledger).
**Links:** IR-1112, IR-1111.
**Trap:** Answering on the grouping taxonomy without the maritime function.
**Mains hook:** Combine taxonomy with function.
**Back:** *"Function here, taxonomy there."*

### Flashcard IR-1144 — Objective routings: UNCLOS innocent passage and EEZ (2022 Prelims Q85) and Senkaku (Q86) — key not available
**Front:** What do the 2022 Prelims routings Q85 and Q86 test, and what must a student not infer?
**Definition (learn):** Q85 tests Convention provisions on the territorial sea, innocent passage and the EEZ (IR-1128 to IR-1130). Q86 tests the Senkaku dispute (IR-1132). The routing ledger records the key status as not available; no answer letter, truth set or option is recorded or inferred.
**Breakdown:**
- Q85: Articles 3, 17–19, 57.
- Q86: East China Sea dispute.
- Key: not available.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic PYQ and objective-question firewall (✅ owner 04 ledger).
**Links:** IR-1128, IR-1129, IR-1132.
**Trap:** Inferring an answer from the owner's cards. The owner forbids it.
**Mains hook:** Not applicable (objective question); use for statement-level revision only.
**Back:** *"Articles three, seventeen to nineteen, fifty-seven; keys withheld."*

### Flashcard IR-1145 — Trap: chairship is convening power, not control
**Front:** Does chairing IORA and IONS give India control over Indian Ocean security?
**Definition (learn):** No. Both are consensus-based bodies. A chairship confers agenda-setting and convening advantage, not decision-making authority over members.
**Breakdown:**
- IORA chair (2025–27): agenda.
- IONS chair (from 20 February 2026): convening.
- Control: none conferred.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §7 and advanced §5 (✅ owner 04).
**Links:** IR-1107, IR-1108.
**Trap:** Option sets that say "control" or "decision authority".
**Mains hook:** Conflating chairship with control is the most common overstatement; name the limit.
**Back:** *"Chair the agenda, not the sea."*

### Flashcard IR-1146 — Trap: "net security provider" is a commentary label, not settled doctrine
**Front:** Is "net security provider" an official Indian doctrine?
**Definition (learn):** The phrase is used in strategic-studies commentary to describe India's aspirational regional role. The owner does not treat it as fixed official doctrine without a specific dated official source; it should be framed as an aspiration.
**Breakdown:**
- Status: commentary framing.
- Safer alternative: "maritime-security partner".
- Advanced framing: aspiration under development.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §7 and §10, advanced §5 and §7 (⚠️ owner analysis).
**Links:** IR-1139, IR-1140.
**Trap:** Asserting "net security provider" as settled policy.
**Mains hook:** Qualify any use of the term with its source.
**Back:** *"A label to test, not a doctrine to quote."*

### Flashcard IR-1147 — Trap: Quad as a symmetric treaty alliance; Quad asymmetry as a limit (Tharoor)
**Front:** Is the Quad a symmetric military alliance akin to NATO?
**Definition (learn):** No. Tharoor notes "a serious asymmetry in the relations among the various countries": "Washington enjoys long-established treaty relationships" that other members do not share with one another. The Quad is a non-treaty coalition.
**Breakdown:**
- Asymmetry: US treaty ties with some partners; India does not share them.
- Consequence: Quad supplements, not replaces, India's own capacity.
- Full profile: owner topic 10.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §5 and §7, advanced §2 and §5 (✅ owner 04 quoting Tharoor).
**Links:** IR-1111, IR-1110, IR-1148.
**Trap:** NATO analogy.
**Mains hook:** Use asymmetry as a limit on Quad's unity and on India's reliance on it.
**Back:** *"Quad is not NATO: one treaty web, four flags."*

### Flashcard IR-1148 — Quad asymmetry as a limitation on maritime cooperation (advanced)
**Front:** Why does Quad asymmetry limit its maritime-security cooperation?
**Definition (learn):** Unequal treaty relationships among members constrain how far Quad can function as a unified security architecture rather than a consultative coalition. India cannot rely on Quad as a substitute for its own bilateral naval capacity-building (Sagar Prahari Bal, IFC-IOR).
**Breakdown:**
- Constraint: unequal treaty ties.
- Result: consultative coalition.
- Implication: Quad supplements India's institutions.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 advanced §5 and §6 (⚠️ owner analysis).
**Links:** IR-1147, IR-1110, IR-1109.
**Trap:** Assuming Quad substitutes for Indian capacity.
**Mains hook:** Assess the Quad as a limitation on maritime cooperation; qualify with its real contributions.
**Back:** *"Quad supplements, never substitutes."*

### Flashcard IR-1149 — IPMDA framing ambiguity: public good or soft-balancing instrument? (advanced)
**Front:** How is IPMDA read by partner states and by China?
**Definition (learn):** Partner littoral states read IPMDA as transparency, capacity and no cost (anti-IUU fishing, anti-piracy, HADR, dark-vessel detection). China reads it as a US-anchored surveillance architecture, soft-balancing or containment-adjacent. The owner says both readings are plausible and not mutually falsifying.
**Breakdown:**
- Reading A: public good.
- Reading B: soft balancing.
- Advanced rule: hold both readings; do not resolve into one verdict.
- Transparency contrast: commercial, unclassified data versus China's own surveillance network.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 advanced §4A (⚠️ owner analysis).
**Links:** IR-1113, IR-1116, IR-1114.
**Trap:** Presenting either reading as settled fact.
**Mains hook:** A strong answer states the ambiguity explicitly.
**Back:** *"Public good to some, soft balance to others: hold both."*

### Flashcard IR-1150 — IPMDA and ASEAN hedging; the technology-dependency and funding-cycle questions (advanced)
**Front:** What three advanced questions does IPMDA raise about hedging, technology and funding?
**Definition (learn):** (1) Southeast Asian recipients of IPMDA data, via the Singapore Information Fusion Centre, welcome the awareness benefit while avoiding public association with the Quad label. (2) The AIS and commercial-RF baseline deepens reliance on US and allied commercial-satellite and analytics providers. (3) The original Quad investment (Tokyo 2022) was framed as five years, so a renewal question arises around 2027; the 2026 expansion is a mid-cycle deepening, not a confirmed post-2027 renewal.
**Breakdown:**
- ASEAN: data without bloc endorsement.
- Technology: sensor base not diversified.
- Funding: five-year horizon; 2027 renewal open.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 advanced §4A (⚠️ owner analysis; 2027 is owner's arithmetic from the five-year term).
**Links:** IR-1113, IR-1115, IR-1118.
**Trap:** Assuming post-2027 continuity is confirmed.
**Mains hook:** Flag the open question rather than assume continuity.
**Back:** *"Data without endorsement, sensors from partners, funding to 2027 then open."*

### Flashcard IR-1151 — Island-state agency: Maldives, Seychelles, Mauritius and Sri Lanka balance many powers (advanced concept)
**Front:** Why does the owner insist that island states are not objects of India-China competition?
**Definition (learn):** Treating these states as objects understates their agency in balancing multiple partners for developmental and security interests. Island states weigh India's offer against other powers' offers and domestic political considerations, so outcomes are not simply a function of India's effort.
**Breakdown:**
- Agency: parallel engagement with other powers.
- Constraint on India: investment does not translate into exclusive partnership.
- Example: Maldives' dated arc (IR-1046).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 advanced §2, §3, §6 (⚠️ owner analysis).
**Links:** IR-1046, IR-1152, IR-1093.
**Trap:** Assuming island states are aligned automatically with India or China.
**Mains hook:** Counter the dependency framing; qualify with the island states' own constraints.
**Back:** *"Islands balance too."*

### Flashcard IR-1152 — Red Sea and Bab-el-Mandeb route risk: diversion, not closure
**Front:** How can Red Sea disruption affect Asia-Europe shipping, and what must be distinguished?
**Definition (learn):** Disruption around the southern Red Sea and Bab-el-Mandeb can divert Asia-Europe shipping around the Cape of Good Hope, raising time, freight and insurance costs. The owner distinguishes route disruption from complete closure.
**Breakdown:**
- Effect: longer routes, higher costs.
- Distinction: disruption vs closure.
- Evidence: dated shipping data needed before quantifying.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §3 (⚠️ owner analysis; no quantified figure given).
**Links:** IR-1106, IR-1105.
**Trap:** Treating disruption as closure.
**Mains hook:** Maritime security affects trade even outside India's waters; qualify with data.
**Back:** *"Divert, not shut; measure before claiming."*

### Flashcard IR-1153 — Trap: IORA, IONS and IFC-IOR are three distinct mechanisms, not one body
**Front:** Are IORA, IONS and IFC-IOR interchangeable names?
**Definition (learn):** No. IORA is a regional intergovernmental organisation; IONS is a naval cooperation symposium; IFC-IOR is an information-sharing centre.
**Breakdown:**
- IORA: intergovernmental organisation (23 members).
- IONS: voluntary naval symposium (2008).
- IFC-IOR: information fusion centre (2018, Gurugram).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §7 (✅ owner 04).
**Links:** IR-1107, IR-1108, IR-1109.
**Trap:** Matching a mechanism to the wrong type.
**Mains hook:** Name each by function when answering.
**Back:** *"Organisation, symposium, centre: three different things."*

### Flashcard IR-1154 — HADR diplomacy vs HADR operations: IR owns frameworks, Disaster Management owns relief
**Front:** What is the boundary between HADR diplomacy and HADR operations?
**Definition (learn):** HADR diplomacy is the cooperative framework and readiness partnership, owned by IR. HADR operations are the relief execution cycle (evacuation, logistics, command), owned by Disaster Management.
**Breakdown:**
- IR: frameworks, partnerships, readiness.
- Disaster Management: operational relief cycle.
- Route: Disaster-Management basic 17 and 18 for operations.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 basic §2 and §10 (✅ owner 04 cross-owner boundary).
**Links:** IR-1105, IR-1113.
**Trap:** Mixing diplomacy with operations in an answer.
**Mains hook:** Distinguish the two and show how each serves maritime security.
**Back:** *"IR writes the framework; DM runs the relief."*

### Flashcard IR-1155 — Maritime commons vs territorial waters (concept)
**Front:** How does the "maritime commons" differ from a state's territorial waters?
**Definition (learn):** The commons framing treats sea lanes and high-seas areas as shared strategic space requiring cooperative management under UNCLOS. Territorial waters and the EEZ are sovereign or specified-rights zones of a coastal state.
**Breakdown:**
- Commons: high seas and sea lanes; shared management.
- Territorial waters: coastal sovereignty (up to 12 nm).
- EEZ: specified rights (up to 200 nm).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 advanced §2 (⚠️ owner analysis; distances per IR-1128).
**Links:** IR-1128, IR-1129.
**Trap:** Treating the commons as ungoverned.
**Mains hook:** Commons need rules; name the treaty that provides them.
**Back:** *"Commons are shared, waters are owned, zones are rights."*

### Flashcard IR-1156 — IPMDA funding cycle and the India chairship track as deepening evidence (advanced)
**Front:** What does the advanced owner ask examiners to flag about the funding horizon of IPMDA?
**Definition (learn):** The original Quad commitment was framed as five years; the 2026 IPMSC launch and COP expansion is a mid-cycle deepening announcement, not a confirmed post-2027 renewal. An advanced answer should flag this as open.
**Breakdown:**
- 2022: five-year commitment.
- 2026: mid-cycle expansion.
- Open question: renewal after the five-year term.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 04 advanced §4A (⚠️ owner analysis).
**Links:** IR-1150, IR-1115.
**Trap:** Assuming continuity beyond the term.
**Mains hook:** Flag open questions explicitly.
**Back:** *"Five years pledged; renewal is open."*

## INTERNATIONAL RELATIONS — Part 2: Central Asia, West Asia, Africa, Global South and Diaspora

> Source basis: repository owners `upsc-ai-kit/knowledge/International-Relations/basic/` and `advanced/`, files 05–09 (read in full). Fetched checks: Wikipedia articles on INSTC, Chabahar Port, IMEC, Ashgabat Agreement, Overseas Citizenship of India, Pravasi Bharatiya Divas and the India-Africa Forum Summit (fetched 2026-11-01). The TAPI article returned HTTP 404, so TAPI cards are ⚠️ only. Time-varying facts carry the owner's as-of date (3 August 2026 or the 6 September 2026 recheck) or the fetched-page date.

### GROUP — 05 Central Asia, Eurasia and Connectivity

### Flashcard IR-2001 — Central Asia: the no-direct-land-access problem
**Front:** Why is India's Central Asia connectivity structurally constrained?
**Definition (learn):** India has no direct land border with any of the five Central Asian Republics (CARs); Pakistan and Afghanistan lie between India and the region, so every route must be negotiated, rerouted or substituted. (✅ owner 05)
**Breakdown:**
- Route A: through Afghanistan/Pakistan, politically and security constrained.
- Route B: via Xinjiang, the Karakoram and the Himalaya, geographically extreme and dependent on China. (✅ owner 05, Sikri)
- Work-around: maritime-linked INSTC and Chabahar (via Iran), which substitute one dependency (Iran/sanctions) for another. (✅ owner 05)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Five CARs: Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzstan, Tajikistan, Turkmenistan, Uzbekistan. (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** IR-2002, IR-2011, IR-2013, IR-2014, IR-2016, IR-2021
**Trap:** "Central Asia is directly accessible to India by land" is false; SCO membership does not create physical connectivity. (✅ owner 05)
**Mains hook:** Start any Central Asia answer with the access constraint, then list instruments. Conclude that the problem is structural, not only technical, and that goodwill must be converted into reliable transit access.
**Back:** *"No land gate: Pakistan and Afghanistan stand between India and the CARs."*

### Flashcard IR-2002 — Five Central Asian Republics (CARs)
**Front:** Name the five Central Asian Republics and their capitals if known.
**Definition (learn):** The five post-Soviet states of Central Asia that India's policy addresses as a regional group.
**Breakdown:**
- K-K-T-T-U: Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzstan, Tajikistan, Turkmenistan, Uzbekistan.
- All five are landlocked. (✅ owner 05 for list; landlocked status is standard geography, ⚠️ not separately fetched)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Five states listed in owner 05 as the policy's target group. (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** IR-2001, IR-2003, IR-2004
**Trap:** Afghanistan is not a CAR, although it is a neighbour of the region and sits on the connectivity route. Mongolia is not a CAR.
**Mains hook:** Use the group name precisely when citing the Connect Central Asia Policy; do not extend it to Afghanistan or Mongolia.
**Back:** *"Kazakh, Kyrgyz, Tajik, Turkmen, Uzbek: five Stans, no Afghan."*

### Flashcard IR-2003 — Connect Central Asia Policy (2012)
**Front:** What is the Connect Central Asia Policy, when was it launched and what does it cover?
**Definition (learn):** India's broad-based policy towards the five CARs, launched in 2012, aiming to strengthen and expand relations through political, security, economic and cultural connections. (✅ owner 05)
**Breakdown:**
- Political engagement.
- Security cooperation.
- Economic and energy partnership.
- Cultural connections.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Launched 2012. (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** IR-2002, IR-2004, IR-2007, IR-2023
**Trap:** It is not an energy-only policy; the owner stresses the "broad-based" four-pillar character. (✅ owner 05)
**Mains hook:** Cite the 2012 policy and its four pillars when answering the 2024 Q10 question on evolving relations; then test the pillars against the connectivity constraint.
**Back:** *"Connect 2012: Political, Security, Economic, Cultural, never energy alone."*

### Flashcard IR-2004 — India-Central Asia Summit (first, 27 January 2022)
**Front:** When and how was the first India-Central Asia Summit held?
**Definition (learn):** The first leader-level India-Central Asia Summit, a dedicated region-specific mechanism between India and the five CARs. (✅ owner 05)
**Breakdown:**
- Format: virtual.
- Participants: India and the five CARs.
- Follow-up: a second leader-level summit was envisaged but not recorded as held by 3 August 2026. (✅ owner 05, as-of date)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 27 January 2022, virtual. (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** IR-2005, IR-2006, IR-2010
**Trap:** The summit mechanism is region-specific (India plus CARs only), unlike the SCO, which includes China, Russia and Pakistan. (✅ owner 05)
**Mains hook:** Use the 2022 summit and the missing second summit to show that institutionalisation at leader level has lagged. This is an honest indicator, not a failure claim.
**Back:** *"Twenty-seven January twenty-twenty-two, virtual: the first, not yet the second."*

### Flashcard IR-2005 — India-Central Asia Dialogue (fourth, New Delhi, 6 June 2025)
**Front:** What is the India-Central Asia Dialogue and what did its fourth edition do?
**Definition (learn):** The foreign-minister-level India-CAR forum. Its fourth edition met in New Delhi on 6 June 2025 and envisaged preparing for a second summit. (✅ owner 05)
**Breakdown:**
- Level: foreign ministers.
- Venue and date: New Delhi, 6 June 2025.
- Outcome: preparation for a second India-Central Asia Summit.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Fourth Dialogue, 6 June 2025. Joint statement referenced in the owner's MEA source list. (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** IR-2004, IR-2006, IR-2010
**Trap:** The Dialogue is not the summit. A foreign-minister meeting does not equal a leader-level summit.
**Mains hook:** Use the Dialogue as evidence of continuity and the missing second summit as evidence of thin leader-level follow-through.
**Back:** *"Fourth Dialogue, June 2025, Delhi: foreign ministers talk, leaders still wait."*

### Flashcard IR-2006 — Third meeting of Secretaries of Security Councils / NSAs (Bishkek, 16 October 2025)
**Front:** Where and when was the third meeting of Security Council Secretaries/NSAs of India and the CARs held?
**Definition (learn):** A security-level India-CAR meeting on counter-terrorism and regional security. (✅ owner 05)
**Breakdown:**
- Format: Secretaries of Security Councils and NSAs.
- Venue: Bishkek.
- Date: 16 October 2025.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 16 October 2025, Bishkek. (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** IR-2004, IR-2005
**Trap:** Do not confuse this security track with the foreign-minister Dialogue (IR-2005).
**Mains hook:** Use the security track to show that counter-terrorism is part of the Central Asia agenda, not only energy.
**Back:** *"Bishkek, October 2025: spies and security chiefs, not ministers."*

### Flashcard IR-2007 — India-EAEU free-trade negotiation (the "CECA" proposal)
**Front:** What is India's proposed Comprehensive Economic Cooperation Agreement with the Eurasian space, and what is its status?
**Definition (learn):** India's proposal to integrate its markets with the Eurasian space, now pursued as an India-Eurasian Economic Union (EAEU) free-trade negotiation. (✅ owner 05)
**Breakdown:**
- Proposal origin: stated in the Connect Central Asia framework.
- Current form: India-EAEU FTA talks.
- Formal start: 3 June 2017, St Petersburg. (✅ owner 05)
- Latest round recorded by owner: second round, Moscow, 22–25 June 2026. (⚠️ owner only)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Negotiations since 3 June 2017; still under negotiation. Not signed and not in force. (⚠️ owner 05)
**Links:** IR-2003, IR-2008, IR-2009
**Trap:** A "proposed CECA" is not a signed agreement; negotiation rounds are not conclusion.
**Mains hook:** Cite economic integration as an evolving dimension, but qualify it with the fact that the FTA is unconcluded.
**Back:** *"CECA became the EAEU talks: since 2017, still talking, not signed."*

### Flashcard IR-2008 — SCO membership and status as of 3 August 2026
**Front:** How many full members, observers and dialogue partners does the SCO have, and which states changed status?
**Definition (learn):** The Shanghai Cooperation Organisation's membership and participation tiers as recorded by the owner for 3 August 2026. (✅ owner 05, as-of date)
**Breakdown:**
- Full members (10): Belarus, China, India, Iran, Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzstan, Pakistan, Russia, Tajikistan, Uzbekistan.
- Observers (2): Afghanistan, Mongolia.
- Dialogue partners: 15.
- Pakistan (joined 2017) and Iran (joined 2023) are now full members, not observers. Belarus joined in 2024.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 10 full members; 2 observers; 15 dialogue partners; as of 3 August 2026. (✅ owner 05; Belarus date ✅ owner 05)
**Links:** IR-2009, IR-2010, IR-2022
**Trap:** Sikri's older book-period list (Pakistan, Iran and Mongolia as observers) is period-specific and out of date.
**Mains hook:** If you quote SCO membership, always date it; state that membership does not solve access.
**Back:** *"Ten full, two watchers, fifteen partners: Pakistan and Iran graduated."*

### Flashcard IR-2009 — SCO Tianjin summit (31 August–1 September 2025) and the 2026 Bishkek summit
**Front:** What did the 2025 SCO summit at Tianjin adopt, who chairs 2025–26, and what did the owner record for 2026?
**Definition (learn):** The 2025 SCO Heads of State Council summit, Tianjin, adopted the Tianjin Declaration. (✅ owner 05)
**Breakdown:**
- Venue and dates: Tianjin, 31 August–1 September 2025.
- Output: Tianjin Declaration.
- Chair: Kyrgyzstan, 2025–26.
- Owner's 6 September 2026 recheck records India attending the 26th SCO Summit at Bishkek on 1 September 2026 (cites MEA; ⚠️ not fetched here).
- A 1 September 2025 decision envisages a single "SCO Partner" status; enabling amendments had not entered into force (owner, as of 3 August 2026).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Tianjin, 31 Aug–1 Sep 2025; Kyrgyz chair 2025–26. (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** IR-2008, IR-2010
**Trap:** Holding the SCO chair does not mean owning its agenda; a declaration is not an implemented treaty.
**Mains hook:** Use the SCO as the multilateral overlay for Central Asia, while pointing out that it does not deliver transit.
**Back:** *"Tianjin 2025, Kyrgyz chair next: declarations travel, corridors do not."*

### Flashcard IR-2010 — Comparison pair: SCO vs India-Central Asia Dialogue
**Front:** How do the SCO and the India-Central Asia Dialogue differ?
**Definition (learn):** Two India-linked channels for Central Asia with different membership and purpose. (✅ owner 05)
**Breakdown:**
- SCO: broad, includes China, Russia and Pakistan; engagement filtered through great-power dynamics.
- India-Central Asia Dialogue: region-specific, India plus the five CARs; more direct engagement.
- Summit track (IR-2004): region-specific, leader level.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Dialogue fourth edition 6 June 2025; SCO Tianjin 31 Aug–1 Sep 2025. (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** IR-2004, IR-2005, IR-2008, IR-2009
**Trap:** Treating the SCO as India's private Central Asia platform is wrong; India's seat carries no privileged influence. (✅ owner 05, advanced)
**Mains hook:** Match each forum to a function: broad security-economic dialogue (SCO) versus focused bilateral-region engagement (Dialogue).
**Back:** *"SCO is wide with China and Pakistan inside; the Dialogue is India and five CARs."*

### Flashcard IR-2011 — The two-route geography problem (Sikri)
**Front:** Why do both overland routes from Eurasia to India face serious constraint?
**Definition (learn):** Sikri's observation that any Eurasia-to-India energy pipeline avoiding Afghanistan/Pakistan must be routed via Xinjiang and across the Karakoram and Himalaya, which makes both plausible land routes politically or geographically difficult. (✅ owner 05)
**Breakdown:**
- Route A: via Afghanistan/Pakistan, politically blocked.
- Route B: via Xinjiang/Karakoram/Himalaya, geographically extreme and China-dependent.
- Therefore the constraint is structural.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Sikri, *Challenges and Strategy* (book period; no date needed). (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** IR-2001, IR-2014, IR-2016, IR-2024
**Trap:** Engineering alone does not dissolve the problem; Route B is not a simple "build it" solution. (✅ owner 05 advanced)
**Mains hook:** Use this as the centre of a 15-mark answer on connectivity constraints.
**Back:** *"Both doors locked: Pakistan's route is closed, China's route is steep."*

### Flashcard IR-2012 — "New Great Game" framing (Sikri)
**Front:** What does the "New Great Game" mean in the Central Asia context, and how does it differ from the 19th-century version?
**Definition (learn):** Sikri's framing of ongoing competition among Russia, China, the US and regional powers for influence and resources in Central Asia. (⚠️ owner 05 analytical framing)
**Breakdown:**
- Contemporary: multi-actor competition; no single power determines the outcome.
- Historical: the 19th-century British-Russian Great Game (owned by World History, not this file).
- India is one actor among several.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Sikri's framing; no independent date. (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** IR-2001, IR-2010, IR-2024
**Trap:** The phrase does not mean India can determine outcomes unilaterally. (✅ owner 05)
**Mains hook:** Place India's Central Asia choices inside a competitive field that limits, without eliminating, India's influence.
**Back:** *"Many players, no single winner: New Great Game is competition, not destiny."*

### Flashcard IR-2013 — Ashgabat Agreement (Central Asia-Persian Gulf transit)
**Front:** What is the Ashgabat Agreement, when was it signed and when did it enter into force, and when did India join?
**Definition (learn):** A multimodal international transport and transit agreement to set up a corridor between Central Asia and the Persian Gulf/Gulf of Oman. (✅ owner 05; ✅ Wikipedia: Ashgabat Agreement)
**Breakdown:**
- Signed: 25 April 2011 (owner; Wikipedia gives the same date).
- Original signatories (Wikipedia): Iran, Oman, Qatar, Turkmenistan and Uzbekistan. Qatar withdrew in 2013.
- Entry into force: April 2016.
- India's accession: asked March 2016; formally joined 3 February 2018.
- Current parties (Wikipedia): Kazakhstan, Uzbekistan, Turkmenistan, Iran, India, Pakistan (joined from November 2016), Oman.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 25 April 2011 (signed); April 2016 (in force); 3 February 2018 (India joined). (✅ owner 05; ✅ Wikipedia)
**Links:** IR-2014, IR-2015, IR-2016, IR-2023
**Trap:** It is a facilitation framework, not an infrastructure-finance mechanism. Joining it does not build a railway. (✅ owner 05)
**Mains hook:** Use Ashgabat as the rules-and-transit layer under INSTC/Chabahar, and mention its dependence on physical segments and sanctions exposure.
**Back:** *"Ashgabat 2011, in force 2016, India in 2018: rules first, rails later."*

### Flashcard IR-2014 — International North-South Transport Corridor (INSTC): framework and parties
**Front:** What is the INSTC, when was it founded, and who are its members?
**Definition (learn):** A multimodal (ship, rail, road) corridor for moving freight between India, the Persian Gulf, Iran, the Caspian, Russia, Caucasus and Central Asia, intended to bypass Pakistan. (✅ owner 05 for bypass aim; ✅ Wikipedia: INSTC)
**Breakdown:**
- Founding agreement: 16 May 2002, signed by Russian, Iranian and Indian representatives (Wikipedia).
- Length: about 7,200 km.
- Overall corridor: Mumbai (south) to Moscow (north).
- Members per Wikipedia: India, Iran, Russia, Azerbaijan, Kazakhstan, Armenia, Belarus, Oman, Kyrgyzstan, Tajikistan, Syria, Turkey, Ukraine. Bulgaria is an observer. Turkmenistan is not a formal member per Wikipedia.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 16 May 2002; about 7,200 km. (✅ Wikipedia: INSTC)
**Links:** IR-2013, IR-2015, IR-2016, IR-2018
**Trap:** INSTC is a framework with operating segments, not a completed full-capacity corridor. (✅ owner 05, ⚠️ status)
**Mains hook:** Present INSTC as a 2002 framework whose full capacity is still developing, and tie it to Iran dependence.
**Back:** *"Sixteen May 2002: Mumbai to Moscow, seven thousand two hundred kilometres, many half-built links."*

### Flashcard IR-2015 — INSTC milestones and Rasht–Astara / Azerbaijan link
**Front:** Which INSTC segments were dated as operational, and which remained open?
**Definition (learn):** The Wikipedia-recorded dated milestones of INSTC's operating and unfinished segments. (✅ Wikipedia: INSTC, fetched 2026-11-01)
**Breakdown:**
- Kazakhstan–Turkmenistan–Iran railway (677 km, Uzen–Bereket–Etrek–Gorgan): official opening 3 December 2014; Bereket–Uzen completed May 2013; Bereket–Etrek completed February 2014.
- 2014 dry runs via Bandar Abbas.
- Astara port (Caspian): inaugurated March 2013.
- Astara–Rasht–Qazvin railway (205 km) and road: linked to the Azerbaijan route.
- Rasht–Astara railway: Wikipedia records 90% completion of Qazvin–Rasht in January 2017 but gives no completion date for Rasht–Astara.
- Armenia–Iran railway: planned, about 305 km; "remained only on paper" as of April 2017.
- 7 July 2022: RZD Logistics announced first goods transport to India via INSTC.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 3 Dec 2014 Kazakh-Turkmen-Iran opening; March 2013 Astara port; 7 July 2022 first RZD goods. (✅ Wikipedia: INSTC)
**Links:** IR-2014, IR-2016, IR-2018
**Trap:** Operating segments do not equal full INSTC capacity. Armenia–Iran railway is a plan, not an operating link.
**Mains hook:** Use segment-by-segment evidence to argue that INSTC is partly operational and partly paper.
**Back:** *"Bereket in 2014, Astara in 2013, Rasht still waiting: segments, not a single finished corridor."*

### Flashcard IR-2016 — Chabahar port: dated history (Shahid Beheshti)
**Front:** What is the dated history of India's Chabahar port in Iran?
**Definition (learn):** Iran's southeastern deep-sea port developed with Indian participation as a route to Afghanistan and Central Asia that avoids Pakistan. (✅ owner 05; ✅ Wikipedia: Chabahar Port)
**Breakdown:**
- 1973: development first proposed by the last Shah.
- 1983: first phase opened.
- 2003: India and Iran agreed to develop Shahid Beheshti port; sanctions stalled it.
- 2013: Indian cabinet approved about USD 115 million investment.
- May 2016: bilateral deal, twelve MoUs and a trilateral transit agreement with Afghanistan; 10-year operating arrangement with two berths run by India Ports Global.
- 29 October 2017: first shipment (Indian wheat for Afghanistan).
- 3 December 2017: Rouhani inaugurated first phase.
- November 2018: US exemption from Iran sanctions.
- December 2018: India took over port operations (cited Dec 24, 2018).
- February 2019: Afghanistan launched a new export route to India via Chabahar.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above. (✅ Wikipedia: Chabahar Port; ✅ owner 05 for 2018 waiver)
**Links:** IR-2017, IR-2015, IR-2013, IR-2024
**Trap:** The 2016 operating arrangement and the 2024 long-term contract (IR-2017) are different instruments. Do not merge them without a source.
**Mains hook:** Argue that Chabahar is India's Afghanistan and Central Asia route, dependent on Iran-specific sanctions policy.
**Back:** *"Seventy-three proposed, sixteen signed, seventeen first wheat: Chabahar is an Iran-dependent gate."*

### Flashcard IR-2017 — Chabahar 10-year contract (13 May 2024) and the 2025 US waiver revocation
**Front:** What is the owner's dated account of the Chabahar contract and the US waiver revocation?
**Definition (learn):** Owner-recorded dated facts about the 10-year contract between India Ports Global Ltd and Iran's Ports and Maritime Organisation, and about the US waiver. (✅ owner 05 for dates; ⚠️ not confirmed by fetched Wikipedia)
**Breakdown:**
- Contract: 10-year long-term contract signed 13 May 2024 (owner).
- Investment: about USD 120 million in equipment; USD 250 million credit window (owner).
- US State Department revoked the 2018 sanctions waiver on 16 September 2025, effective 29 September 2025 (owner, ⚠️).
- Non-exposure assurance to 26 April 2026 (owner); April 2026 negotiation on an Iranian-managed mechanism with reversion to India (fetched Wikipedia page).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 13 May 2024; 16 Sep 2025 revocation; 29 Sep 2025 effective; 26 Apr 2026 assurance end. (⚠️ 2025 dates owner only)
**Links:** IR-2016, IR-2024, IR-2045
**Trap:** Sanctions exposure is created by another state's domestic law, not an international legal bar on India. (✅ owner 05)
**Mains hook:** Use the revocation as the clearest current example of third-party sanctions risk to India's connectivity options.
**Back:** *"Thirteen May 2024 contract, sixteen September 2025 revocation, twenty-sixth April 2026 cliff."*

### Flashcard IR-2018 — TAPI gas pipeline (Turkmenistan-Afghanistan-Pakistan-India)
**Front:** What is TAPI and why is it relevant to India's Central Asia energy and connectivity strategy?
**Definition (learn):** A proposed gas pipeline from Turkmenistan through Afghanistan and Pakistan to India. It is not covered in the repository owners; the Wikipedia article returned HTTP 404 on fetch. (⚠️ not verified)
**Breakdown:**
- Route: Turkmenistan, Afghanistan, Pakistan, India (⚠️ memory).
- Its route passes through Pakistan, so it runs into the access constraint in IR-2001.
- Status: announced and long-delayed (⚠️ not verified in this build).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not verified. (⚠️)
**Links:** IR-2001, IR-2011, IR-2013
**Trap:** Do not assume TAPI is operational or that it avoids Pakistan. (⚠️)
**Mains hook:** TAPI shows that energy routes through Afghanistan and Pakistan carry the same transit-risk logic as the Sikri two-route problem; cite only after dated verification.
**Back:** *"TAPI goes through Pakistan: energy wants the same gate the corridors avoid."*

### Flashcard IR-2019 — India-Uzbekistan bilateral brief (as on April 2026)
**Front:** Which dated MEA reference should be cited for current India-Uzbekistan claims?
**Definition (learn):** The MEA India-Uzbekistan bilateral brief, recorded as "as on April 2026", is the owner's current authoritative bilateral record for that relationship. (✅ owner 05, as-of April 2026)
**Breakdown:**
- Use: cite this dated brief for any current specific claim about the relationship.
- Do not cite undated press language for present status.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As on April 2026. (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** IR-2003, IR-2004
**Trap:** An old bilateral record is not current status; check the as-of date.
**Mains hook:** Demonstrate source discipline by quoting the dated brief rather than general language.
**Back:** *"Uzbekistan's April 2026 brief: date it or do not cite it."*

### Flashcard IR-2020 — India-China energy competition (Sikri's call for an "understanding")
**Front:** What does Sikri recommend about India-China competition for energy in Central Asia?
**Definition (learn):** Sikri argues India must develop an understanding on energy with China, because both are large consumers often seeking energy from the same sources, and their competition benefits producers. (✅ owner 05)
**Breakdown:**
- Quote: "competition is only benefiting the energy producers."
- Implication: cooperative management of a shared dependency.
- Limit: broader strategic mistrust remains (topic 03 of the Basic owners).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Book-period argument from Sikri; undated. (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** IR-2010, IR-2012, IR-2021
**Trap:** Competition and cooperation can coexist on a functional issue; do not claim that India and China only compete in Central Asia. (✅ owner 05 advanced)
**Mains hook:** The 10-mark question on India-China cooperation in Central Asia rewards naming the energy-producer benefit and the mistrust constraint.
**Back:** *"Two thirsty consumers, one well: understanding beats bidding war."*

### Flashcard IR-2021 — Trap pair: INSTC/Chabahar as completed corridors versus developing frameworks
**Front:** Are INSTC and Chabahar completed, fully functioning corridors at full capacity?
**Definition (learn):** No. They are developing connectivity frameworks with some operating segments, and their capacity depends on Iran-related geopolitics and sanctions. (✅ owner 05)
**Breakdown:**
- Operating: some Caspian and Kazakh-Turkmen-Iran segments (IR-2015).
- Developing: Rasht–Astara and Armenia–Iran segments.
- Contingent: Chabahar's sanctions status (IR-2017).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner classification; capacity claims require a dated source. (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** IR-2014, IR-2015, IR-2017
**Trap:** The examiner trap is treating an announced, signed or partially built route as fully delivered.
**Mains hook:** Use "operating segment, not completed corridor" as a precise phrase.
**Back:** *"Segments run, corridors wait: never say completed without a dated source."*

### Flashcard IR-2022 — Trap pair: SCO membership gives connectivity; Pakistan and Iran are observers
**Front:** Does SCO membership resolve India's connectivity problem, and who are SCO observers now?
**Definition (learn):** SCO is a political-security-economic forum; membership does not create physical transit. Afghanistan and Mongolia are observers; Pakistan and Iran are full members. (✅ owner 05)
**Breakdown:**
- SCO membership: forum, not route.
- Pakistan (2017) and Iran (2023) are full members.
- Observers: Afghanistan, Mongolia.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As of 3 August 2026, per owner. (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** IR-2008, IR-2010, IR-2001
**Trap:** Sikri's observer list is period-specific; an answer citing it as current is wrong.
**Mains hook:** Separate forum membership from route access in one sentence.
**Back:** *"Forum is not a road: Pakistan and Iran graduated, Afghanistan still watches."*

### Flashcard IR-2023 — PYQ route: 2024 GS-II Q10, Central Asia (10 marks, 150 words)
**Front:** How should a 2024 GS-II Q10 answer on India-CAR relations be structured?
**Definition (learn):** Verified PYQ: "Critically analyse India's evolving diplomatic, economic and strategic relations with the CARs highlighting their increasing significance in regional and global geopolitics." (✅ owner 05, ledger 2024–2025)
**Breakdown:**
- (1) List the five CARs and the 2012 policy (IR-2002, IR-2003).
- (2) Cite the proposed CECA/EAEU talks and SCO (IR-2007, IR-2008).
- (3) Name the access constraint and the Xinjiang/Karakoram route (IR-2001, IR-2011).
- (4) Place it in New Great Game competition (IR-2012).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024 GS-II Q10, Critically analyse, 10 marks, 150 words. (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** IR-2003, IR-2007, IR-2011, IR-2012
**Trap:** Listing opportunities without the connectivity constraint is a critical-analysis failure. (✅ owner 05)
**Mains hook:** The answer thesis: evolution in diplomacy is real, but structural access limits it.
**Back:** *"Evolving, not arrived: five states, one policy, one wall."*

### Flashcard IR-2024 — PYQ route: 2025 Prelims Q62, INSTC connectivity (key not inferred)
**Front:** What is the 2025 Prelims GS-I Q62 about and how should it be prepared?
**Definition (learn):** Objective question on INSTC connectivity. The official Set-A key is held locally, but the owner records no answer letter and this card does not infer one. (✅ owner 05, ledger 2024–2025)
**Breakdown:**
- Cover INSTC's members, route, bypass purpose and operating status.
- Keep statement-level distinctions: framework versus operating segment.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 Prelims GS-I Q62. Answer not recorded. (✅ owner 05)
**Links:** IR-2014, IR-2015, IR-2021
**Trap:** Do not state an answer letter; the owner does not record one.
**Mains hook:** Not a Mains question; use it to test statement-level knowledge of INSTC.
**Back:** *"Sixty-two asks INSTC: know the route, the partners and the status gap."*
