### GROUP — 06 West Asia, Energy Security and Connectivity

### Flashcard IR-2025 — West Asia: energy security as the organising interest
**Front:** Why is energy security the organising interest of India's West Asia policy?
**Definition (learn):** India's West Asia policy is organised around energy security, with diaspora welfare, Israel technology and connectivity layered on top. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Energy: crude-oil import dependence and equity in overseas oilfields (IR-2027, IR-2028).
- Diaspora: millions in the Gulf (IR-2031).
- Technology and defence: Israel (IR-2033).
- Connectivity: I2U2, IMEC, GCC framework (IR-2037, IR-2038).
- Balancing principle (IR-2032).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner-level structure. (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** IR-2026, IR-2027, IR-2029, IR-2032, IR-2051
**Trap:** Energy is a foreign-policy kingpin, not only a pricing question. (✅ owner 06 PYQ 2025 Q19)
**Mains hook:** Integrate energy, diaspora, technology and connectivity in one thesis.
**Back:** *"Energy first, diaspora next, Israel and corridors on top: a non-exclusive portfolio."*

### Flashcard IR-2026 — "Look West" policy (Tharoor)
**Front:** What is the "Look West" policy, and how is it paired with "Look East"?
**Definition (learn):** Tharoor's term for India's engagement with the Arab world, parallel to the better-known Look East policy. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Look East: Asia-Pacific engagement.
- Look West: Gulf and Arab world engagement.
- Both are regionally targeted doctrines.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Tharoor, *Pax Indica*; undated framing. (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** IR-2025, IR-2032
**Trap:** Present "Look West" as Tharoor's term, not as a formally named MEA doctrine (owner 06 attributes it to Tharoor only).
**Mains hook:** Use Look West and Look East as parallel regional doctrines when showing India's regional targeting.
**Back:** *"Look East for Asia, Look West for the Arab world: two lenses, one foreign policy."*

### Flashcard IR-2027 — Crude-oil import dependence: 70% (Sikri), 88.2% (FY2024-25), 88.7% (FY2025-26 provisional)
**Front:** What are India's crude-oil import dependence figures, and why must the date be stated?
**Definition (learn):** Share of crude oil India imports, measured on a consumption basis. (✅ owner 06, PPAC)
**Breakdown:**
- Sikri, book period: "approximately 70 per cent".
- PPAC FY2024-25: 88.2%.
- PPAC FY2025-26 (provisional): about 88.7%.
- Use: trend direction only for the book figure.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 88.2% (FY2024-25); about 88.7% (FY2025-26, provisional). (📰 owner 06, PPAC)
**Links:** IR-2025, IR-2028, IR-2029, IR-2049
**Trap:** Quoting Sikri's 70% as current. (✅ owner 06)
**Mains hook:** Show the dependence trend and then argue that diversification has not reduced aggregate dependence.
**Back:** *"Seventy then, eighty-eight now: date the number or lose the mark."*

### Flashcard IR-2028 — Supplier diversification: Economic Survey 2025-26
**Front:** What do the Economic Survey 2025-26 supplier shares show for April–November FY26?
**Definition (learn):** Change in the countries from which India imports crude oil, reported by the Economic Survey 2025-26. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- US share: 8.1% (from 4.6% a year earlier).
- UAE share: 11.1% (from 9.4%).
- Also rising: Egypt, Nigeria, Libya.
- Declining: Russia, Saudi Arabia, Iraq, Venezuela.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** April–November FY26, Economic Survey 2025-26. (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** IR-2027, IR-2029, IR-2049
**Trap:** Diversification of sources is not reduction of aggregate dependence. (✅ owner 06)
**Mains hook:** Cite the shares, then argue that refinery configuration limits how fast the mix can change.
**Back:** *"US up to eight, UAE up to eleven: more suppliers, same dependence."*

### Flashcard IR-2029 — Energy trilemma (security, affordability, sustainability)
**Front:** What is the energy trilemma and how does it apply to West Asia?
**Definition (learn):** A standard framework balancing reliable supply (security), price (affordability) and transition pressure (sustainability). (⚠️ owner 06 analytical framework)
**Breakdown:**
- Security: supply reliability and import dependence.
- Affordability: price exposure.
- Sustainability: transition pressure.
- Applied: diversification, diaspora exposure and connectivity design share the same trade-offs.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Framework; no date. (⚠️ owner 06)
**Links:** IR-2027, IR-2028, IR-2030, IR-2051
**Trap:** A purely security lens misses affordability and sustainability; a pure price lens misses supply risk.
**Mains hook:** The 15-mark route in advanced owner 06: apply the trilemma to critically evaluate India's West Asia energy strategy.
**Back:** *"Secure, affordable, sustainable: pick three, trade two."*

### Flashcard IR-2030 — Refinery lock-in as a hidden switching cost (Tharoor)
**Front:** Why do Indian refineries limit how quickly India can change its crude mix?
**Definition (learn):** Tharoor notes many Indian refineries are configured to process the quality of crude that Iran supplies. Configuration-specific capacity is a physical switching cost. (✅ owner 06, Tharoor; ⚠️ causal inference)
**Breakdown:**
- Refinery configuration matches crude grade.
- Switching supplier requires matched crude quality or refinery change.
- Result: headline diversification moves slowly.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Tharoor, *Pax Indica*; undated. (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** IR-2028, IR-2029, IR-2041
**Trap:** Refinery lock-in is not the same as a sanctions bar; it is a physical capacity constraint. (⚠️)
**Mains hook:** Use it to explain why diversification shares move slowly.
**Back:** *"Refineries are tuned to Iranian crude: change the feed and the furnace complains."*

### Flashcard IR-2031 — Gulf diaspora figures: Sikri, Tharoor, MEA January 2026
**Front:** How many Indians live in the Gulf and Arab world, according to Sikri, Tharoor and MEA?
**Definition (learn):** Period-specific estimates of the Indian population in the Gulf and Arab world. They measure population stock, not citizenship or OCI count. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Sikri: about 5 million in the Gulf.
- Tharoor: nearly 6 million Indians in the Arab world.
- MEA overseas-Indian table (as on January 2026): UAE 4,344,008; Saudi Arabia 2,750,551.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above. (✅ owner 06; MEA table ✅ owner 09 as-of Jan 2026)
**Links:** IR-2025, IR-2112, IR-2113
**Trap:** Treating any of these as a fixed or citizenship count. (✅ owner 06)
**Mains hook:** Use the diaspora number as a welfare-obligation indicator, with the date stated.
**Back:** *"Five, six, four million and change: stocks by date, never citizenship."*

### Flashcard IR-2032 — Israel-Arab balancing principle (Tharoor)
**Front:** What is India's Israel-Arab balancing principle in Tharoor's words?
**Definition (learn):** India values its relationship with Israel "but not at the expense of its friendships with Arab and other Muslim states." This describes active, non-exclusive balancing. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Active balancing: deepen several ties at once.
- Not passive equidistance.
- Applies also to Iran in the triangle (IR-2040).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Tharoor quote. (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** IR-2025, IR-2033, IR-2036, IR-2048
**Trap:** India does not choose sides between Israel and the Gulf; the two relationships are parallel. (✅ owner 06)
**Mains hook:** Quote Tharoor and show the operational tests (energy, technology, diaspora) for the balancing.
**Back:** *"Israel ties yes, Arab friendship first: balance, not choosing sides."*

### Flashcard IR-2033 — India-Israel relations: depth and diversity
**Front:** What are the key dated and named elements of the India-Israel partnership?
**Definition (learn):** The partnership covering diplomacy, defence, agriculture, innovation and minilateral extension. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Full diplomatic relations: 1992.
- First Indian PM visit to Israel: July 2017.
- Defence: Barak-8 air-defence system as named co-development example.
- Agriculture: Indo-Israel Agricultural Project centres of excellence.
- Innovation: India-Israel Industrial R&D and Technological Innovation Fund (launched 2017).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1992; July 2017; Barak-8. (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** IR-2032, IR-2037, IR-2053
**Trap:** Defence imports do not equal technology transfer; limitation is cost and depth. (✅ owner 06)
**Mains hook:** Show diversity beyond defence (agriculture, innovation) and its limits.
**Back:** *"Ninety-two ties, seventeen visit, Barak-8 leads: defence first, farms and fund next."*

### Flashcard IR-2034 — India-GCC Joint Action Plan (2024–2028)
**Front:** What is the India-GCC Joint Action Plan and when and where was it adopted?
**Definition (learn):** The current dated framework for India's strategic dialogue with the Gulf Cooperation Council, adopted at the first India-GCC Joint Ministerial Meeting for Strategic Dialogue. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Period: 2024–2028.
- Adopted: Riyadh, 9 September 2024.
- Forum: first India-GCC Joint Ministerial Meeting for Strategic Dialogue.
- Covers trade, energy, security and people-to-people.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Riyadh, 9 September 2024. (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** IR-2035, IR-2025, IR-2051
**Trap:** Prelims often asks the period: 2024–2028. (✅ owner 06)
**Mains hook:** Cite its date and forum for any current Gulf structure claim.
**Back:** *"Riyadh, nine September twenty-twenty-four: Gulf plan for 2024 to 2028."*

### Flashcard IR-2035 — Bilateral Gulf architecture: Saudi, Qatar and UAE
**Front:** What are the dated bilateral strategic instruments with Saudi Arabia, Qatar and the UAE?
**Definition (learn):** Dated bilateral frameworks that sit under the GCC plan. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- India-Saudi Arabia Strategic Partnership Council: second meeting, Jeddah, 22 April 2025.
- India-Qatar Agreement on Establishment of a Bilateral Strategic Partnership: 18 February 2025.
- India-UAE Comprehensive Strategic Partnership: elevated 25 January 2017.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 22 Apr 2025; 18 Feb 2025; 25 Jan 2017. (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** IR-2034, IR-2036
**Trap:** Dates differ by country; do not transfer one country's dates to another.
**Mains hook:** Use these to show that Gulf engagement is bilateral as well as GCC-level.
**Back:** *"Jeddah April, Qatar February, UAE January: three dates, three partners."*

### Flashcard IR-2036 — UAE-India CEPA and India-Oman CEPA
**Front:** When did the UAE-India CEPA enter into force and what is the status of the India-Oman CEPA?
**Definition (learn):** Two Gulf trade agreements with different legal status. (⚠️ owner 06; Economy owns tariff mechanics)
**Breakdown:**
- UAE-India CEPA: in force 1 May 2022 (⚠️ owner).
- Officially reported bilateral trade about USD 100 billion in FY2024-25 (⚠️ owner).
- India-Oman CEPA: signed 18 December 2025; entry into force needs separate verification (📰 owner, ⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1 May 2022; 18 December 2025. (⚠️)
**Links:** IR-2035, IR-2034
**Trap:** Signature is not entry into force. (✅ owner 06 caution)
**Mains hook:** Cite the UAE CEPA for strategic-economic depth, but state the Oman status precisely.
**Back:** *"UAE in force 2022, Oman signed 2025: signature is not commencement."*

### Flashcard IR-2037 — I2U2 (India-Israel-UAE-US) minilateral
**Front:** What is I2U2 and what is its status in 2024–2026?
**Definition (learn):** A minilateral framework of India, Israel, the UAE and the US covering food security, water, energy and technology. (⚠️ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Members: India, Israel, UAE, US.
- Themes: food, water, energy, technology.
- First I2U2 leaders' virtual summit: 14 July 2022 (⚠️ owner 06 Mains cross-route; date recorded in MEA source list).
- No four-party meeting or outcome in 2024–2026 verified from official sources; 13 February 2025 US statement recorded an intention to convene partners.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 13 Feb 2025 statement; first summit 14 Jul 2022 (⚠️ owner source list). (⚠️)
**Links:** IR-2033, IR-2038, IR-2052
**Trap:** Treating I2U2 as a completed infrastructure project; it is a forum, not a corridor. (✅ owner 06)
**Mains hook:** Use I2U2 as an issue-based forum and state the delivery gap honestly.
**Back:** *"Four partners, four themes, one meeting in 2022, many promises after."*

### Flashcard IR-2038 — IMEC: MoU of 9 September 2023 and signatories
**Front:** When was the IMEC MoU signed, by whom, and what are its components?
**Definition (learn):** India-Middle East-Europe Economic Corridor, a planned transport, energy and digital connectivity corridor. Announced at the G20 New Delhi summit. (✅ owner 06; ✅ Wikipedia: IMEC)
**Breakdown:**
- Signed: 9 September 2023, New Delhi, eight parties.
- Signatories: India, US, UAE, Saudi Arabia, France, Germany, Italy, European Union (✅ Wikipedia).
- Jordan and Israel did not sign; described as unofficially included because the route passes through them (✅ Wikipedia).
- Eastern corridor: shipping across the Gulf; Northern corridor: rail via UAE, Saudi, Jordan and Israel to Haifa, then sea to Europe (✅ Wikipedia).
- Components: rail, shipping, electricity grid, green hydrogen, undersea and fibre digital cables (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 9 Sep 2023; eight parties. (✅ owner 06; ✅ Wikipedia)
**Links:** IR-2037, IR-2039, IR-2048
**Trap:** Jordan and Israel are not MoU signatories, despite being on the route. (✅ Wikipedia)
**Mains hook:** Describe IMEC as a framework of intent with physical segments still to be built.
**Back:** *"Nine September twenty-three: eight signatures, two countries on the map but not on the page."*

### Flashcard IR-2039 — IMEC status: planned, not built
**Front:** What is IMEC's current status and what has stalled it?
**Definition (learn):** IMEC remains a broad declaration of intent, not a funded, built or operating corridor. (✅ owner 06; ✅ Wikipedia: IMEC, latest dated information in the article)
**Breakdown:**
- No detailed overarching funding plan or implementation mechanism (Wikipedia).
- The 7 October 2023 attacks and the Gaza war have "significantly stalled" the project (Wikipedia; ⚠️ article characterisation).
- Jordan's lack of a functioning railway network is a bottleneck (Wikipedia).
- India and the UAE signed intergovernmental framework agreements in early 2024 for the eastern leg (Wikipedia).
- France hosted an IMEC summit in Marseille in June 2025 (Wikipedia).
- MEA's 24 June 2024 India-UAE readout referred to "commencement of work on IMEEC" (✅ owner 06).
- Owner's 6 September 2026 recheck: MEA still describes IMEC as a transcontinental corridor initiative (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As above. (✅ owner 06; ✅ Wikipedia)
**Links:** IR-2038, IR-2037, IR-2048
**Trap:** "Corridor announced" or "MoU signed" does not mean a built segment. (✅ owner 06)
**Mains hook:** Use the stalled-by-conflict point to show that connectivity projects depend on regional security.
**Back:** *"Planned, not poured: a declaration, a conflict, a railway that does not yet connect."*

### Flashcard IR-2040 — JCPOA (14 July 2015) and US withdrawal (8 May 2018)
**Front:** When was the JCPOA concluded, when did the US withdraw, and what is its status?
**Definition (learn):** The Joint Comprehensive Plan of Action, Iran's nuclear deal, endorsed by UNSC Resolution 2231. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Concluded: 14 July 2015 between Iran and the E3/EU+3.
- Endorsed by UNSC Resolution 2231.
- US withdrawal announced: 8 May 2018; sanctions restored in stages.
- 2025 E3 snapback: status of restored UN measures disputed by Iran, Russia and China (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 14 Jul 2015; 8 May 2018; UNSC Res 2231. (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** IR-2041, IR-2042, IR-2025
**Trap:** Do not describe the JCPOA as normally functioning. (✅ owner 06)
**Mains hook:** Nuclear diplomatic space widened energy ties first, then sanctions exposed Indian channels.
**Back:** *"Fifteen deal, eighteen exit: a 2231 deal with a disputed snapback."*

### Flashcard IR-2041 — Iranian crude cessation (May 2019) and Significant Reduction Exceptions
**Front:** What happened to India's Iranian crude purchases after the US exceptions ended?
**Definition (learn):** India ceased Iranian crude purchases after US Significant Reduction Exceptions ended in May 2019, and substituted other suppliers. (⚠️ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Cause: end of US Significant Reduction Exceptions, May 2019.
- Effect: supplier substitution.
- Limit: reduced sanctions exposure, but aggregate dependence unchanged and Iran relationship narrowed.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** May 2019. (⚠️)
**Links:** IR-2030, IR-2040, IR-2028
**Trap:** Supplier substitution is not dependence reduction. (✅ owner 06)
**Mains hook:** Show how external policy changed India's energy basket directly.
**Back:** *"May 2019 exceptions end, Iranian barrels stop: the basket changes, the dependence does not."*

### Flashcard IR-2042 — Chabahar exception and reversal as a national-interest carve-out
**Front:** How does the Chabahar exception show India's room for national-interest carve-outs?
**Definition (learn):** The 2018 US exception for Chabahar and Afghanistan-related connectivity, revoked effective 29 September 2025. A reversible executive measure, not permanent law. (⚠️ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Shows India can seek carve-outs during US-Iran confrontation.
- The exception is reversible.
- Cross-link to the Central Asia connectivity case (IR-2017).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018 exception; revoked effective 29 Sep 2025 (⚠️ owner). (⚠️)
**Links:** IR-2017, IR-2040, IR-2041
**Trap:** An exception is not a legal guarantee. (✅ owner 06)
**Mains hook:** Argue that carve-outs give limited but real room, always reversible.
**Back:** *"Carve-out granted, carve-out revocable: national interest on a waiver."*

### Flashcard IR-2043 — Operation Sindhu (June 2025): evacuation from Iran and Israel
**Front:** How many nationals did Operation Sindhu evacuate, from where, and by what date?
**Definition (learn):** India's evacuation of nationals from Iran and Israel during the June 2025 crisis, a concrete case of consular protection in West Asia. (📰 owner 06, 09)
**Breakdown:**
- Total: 4,415 nationals by 27 June 2025.
- From Iran: 3,597.
- From Israel: 818.
- Relief and logistics: `Disaster-Management/basic/17`. Host-government coordination: IR owns it.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 4,415; 3,597; 818; 27 June 2025. (📰 owner 06)
**Links:** IR-2025, IR-2126, IR-2128
**Trap:** Evacuation is not rescue from every private risk. (✅ owner 09)
**Mains hook:** Show standing consular obligation in West Asia with dated evidence.
**Back:** *"Four thousand four hundred fifteen, Iran three thousand five hundred ninety-seven, Israel eight hundred eighteen."*

### Flashcard IR-2044 — Strait of Hormuz and the 2026 Prelims Q30 anchor
**Front:** Why is the Strait of Hormuz important, and what does the 2026 Prelims Q30 anchor cover?
**Definition (learn):** The narrow outlet from the Persian Gulf to the Gulf of Oman, the principal route-risk chokepoint for Gulf energy shipments. (⚠️ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Disruption transmits to freight, insurance and supply insecurity.
- Diversification does not remove common-route exposure.
- 2026 Prelims GS-I Q30: Strait of Hormuz and West Asian maritime access to the Indian Ocean. Provisional 2026 Set-A key present locally; no answer recorded or inferred. (✅ owner 06)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Prelims 2026 GS-I Q30. (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** IR-2025, IR-2029, IR-2028
**Trap:** Diversified suppliers still share a route. (✅ owner 06)
**Mains hook:** Use the chokepoint to show why strategic reserves reduce but do not remove risk.
**Back:** *"One narrow gate for Gulf oil: diversify suppliers, still share the strait."*

### Flashcard IR-2045 — Palestine policy: recognition in 1988 and the two-state position
**Front:** When did India recognise the State of Palestine, and what is its current position?
**Definition (learn):** India recognised the State of Palestine in 1988 and supports a negotiated, sovereign, viable two-state solution alongside Israel. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Recognition: 1988.
- Position: negotiated, sovereign, viable two-state solution.
- Balance: Israel ties pursued alongside Palestine support.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1988. (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** IR-2032, IR-2046, IR-2047
**Trap:** Recognition in 1988 is not the same as UN membership or full statehood status. (⚠️)
**Mains hook:** Show two-track balance: principle on Palestine, pragmatic ties with Israel.
**Back:** *"Eighty-eight recognition, two states on paper: balance is the policy."*

### Flashcard IR-2046 — Abraham Accords (2020) and their limit
**Front:** What did the Abraham Accords do for regional minilateral space, and what did they not resolve?
**Definition (learn):** Accords (2020) normalised Israel's relations with several Arab states, widening economic and minilateral cooperation. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Effect: normalisation widened economic space; I2U2 emerged in that environment.
- Limit: did not resolve the Palestinian question.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2020. (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** IR-2037, IR-2045
**Trap:** Normalisation is not Palestinian statehood. (✅ owner 06)
**Mains hook:** Argue that normalisation created connectivity space while leaving the core dispute.
**Back:** *"Twenty-twenty accords: handshakes spread, the Palestinian question stays."*

### Flashcard IR-2047 — October 2023 conflict: India's stated position (⚠️)
**Front:** How did India position itself after the October 2023 conflict?
**Definition (learn):** India condemned terrorism, called for humanitarian protection, release of hostages, dialogue and a two-state settlement, while continuing relations with Israel and assistance to Palestinians. (⚠️ owner 06 analytical summary)
**Breakdown:**
- Condemn terrorism.
- Humanitarian protection and hostage release.
- Two-state settlement.
- Continue Israel ties and Palestinian assistance.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** October 2023 onward (⚠️ not verified in this build).
**Links:** IR-2045, IR-2032, IR-2039
**Trap:** Owner summary only; verify before quoting a specific statement. (⚠️)
**Mains hook:** Use the conflict as the test of IMEC and I2U2 resilience.
**Back:** *"Condemn terror, protect civilians, keep both ties: balance under fire."*

### Flashcard IR-2048 — Trap pair: India's Israel and Gulf ties are not exclusive
**Front:** Does India's Israel partnership replace its Gulf and Arab relationships?
**Definition (learn):** No. Tharoor frames them as parallel, non-exclusive relationships. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Israel: technology, defence, agriculture.
- Gulf: energy, diaspora, GCC plan.
- Iran: energy, Chabahar.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Tharoor balancing principle (IR-2032). (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** IR-2032, IR-2033, IR-2035, IR-2038
**Trap:** This is the trap pair "exclusive choice versus parallel engagement". (✅ owner 06)
**Mains hook:** Use the parallel frame to refute a "choosing sides" thesis.
**Back:** *"Parallel, not replacement: Israel, Gulf, Iran each on its own track."*

### Flashcard IR-2049 — Trap pair: 70% figure, IMEEC as completed, and "energy only"
**Front:** Which West Asia numbers and claims are traps in a Prelims or Mains answer?
**Definition (learn):** Three common errors on West Asia. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- 70% is Sikri's book-period figure; PPAC gives 88.2% and about 88.7%.
- IMEEC is not a completed corridor (IR-2039).
- Energy policy is not energy-only; diaspora and connectivity are layered on it.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** See IR-2027, IR-2039, IR-2025. (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** IR-2027, IR-2039, IR-2025
**Trap:** Each error is a common answer-killing mistake. (✅ owner 06)
**Mains hook:** Always date the energy figure and state the delivery status of corridors.
**Back:** *"Seventy is old, IMEEC is planned, energy is never alone."*

### Flashcard IR-2050 — Trap: diaspora figures are period stocks, not citizenship counts
**Front:** Do Gulf diaspora figures measure citizens, OCI holders or population stock?
**Definition (learn):** Gulf diaspora numbers are population-stock estimates that vary by date and source. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Sikri's 5 million and Tharoor's 6 million are period-specific.
- MEA January 2026 table: population stock.
- Not OCI counts.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** See IR-2031. (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** IR-2031, IR-2112
**Trap:** Quoting a single "fixed" diaspora figure as permanent. (✅ owner 06)
**Mains hook:** Date and define the stock before using it in a welfare argument.
**Back:** *"Stock by date, not passport: the Gulf count moves."*

### Flashcard IR-2051 — PYQ route: 2025 GS-II Q19 (15 marks, 250 words)
**Front:** How should the 2025 Q19 answer on energy security and India's Middle East influence be built?
**Definition (learn):** Verified PYQ: "Energy security constitutes the dominant kingpin of India's foreign policy, and is linked with India's overarching influence in Middle Eastern countries. How would you integrate energy security with India's foreign policy trajectories in the coming years?" (✅ owner 06, ledger 2024–2025)
**Breakdown:**
- (1) Baseline: 88.2% and about 88.7% (IR-2027), trend from 70%.
- (2) Equity investment and diversification (IR-2028).
- (3) GCC Joint Action Plan and I2U2/IMEEC (IR-2034, IR-2037, IR-2038).
- (4) Israel-Arab-Iran balance and diaspora protection (IR-2032, IR-2043).
- (5) Avoid predicting oil-price or corridor outcomes.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 GS-II Q19; 15 marks; 250 words. (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** IR-2025, IR-2029, IR-2034
**Trap:** Limiting the answer to energy pricing. (✅ owner 06)
**Mains hook:** Thesis: energy security is the kingpin, but its integration needs a non-exclusive, dated architecture.
**Back:** *"Kingpin energy, balanced ties, dated frameworks: no crystal ball on oil."*

### Flashcard IR-2052 — PYQ route: 2022 I2U2 cross-route
**Front:** How should a 2022 I2U2 question on India's global position be answered?
**Definition (learn):** Verified cross-route: "How will I2U2 (India, Israel, UAE and USA) grouping transform India's position in global politics?" Use this topic for West Asia, investment, food/energy and connectivity. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Investment, technology, food and energy projects.
- Strategic autonomy constraints.
- Grouping classification belongs to topic 10 (not in this card set).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 GS-II. (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** IR-2037, IR-2038
**Trap:** Treating I2U2 as a completed transformation. (✅ owner 06)
**Mains hook:** Qualify the transformation claim with the delivery gap.
**Back:** *"Two-twenty-two I2U2: four partners, big claims, measured delivery."*

### Flashcard IR-2053 — PYQ route: 2018 Q9 Israel and 2018 Q20 US-Iran nuclear pact
**Front:** What are the 2018 GS-II Q9 and Q20 demands, and how do they route to this topic?
**Definition (learn):** 2018 Q9: depth and diversity of India's relations with Israel (Discuss, 10 marks, 150 words). 2018 Q20: US-Iran nuclear pact controversy and India's national interest (In what ways / How should, 15 marks, 250 words). Both route to this owner's core. (✅ owner 06, ledger 2018–2023)
**Breakdown:**
- Q9: use IR-2033 and IR-2032.
- Q20: use IR-2040, IR-2041, IR-2042.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018 Q9 and Q20. Word limit on Q9 corrupted in scan (owner). (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** IR-2033, IR-2040, IR-2041, IR-2042
**Trap:** Q9's printed word-limit tail is corrupted; do not invent it. (✅ owner 06)
**Mains hook:** Use balance for Q9 and national-interest carve-outs for Q20.
**Back:** *"Eighteen-nine Israel, eighteen-twenty Iran: balance, then national interest."*

### Flashcard IR-2054 — Prelims coverage: 2018 Q24, 2018 Q37, 2023 Q94 (keys not inferred)
**Front:** What do the 2018 Q24, 2018 Q37 and 2023 Q94 objective demands cover, and what is the key status?
**Definition (learn):** Objective demands routed to the West Asia owner: two-state solution in international affairs (2018 Q24); conflict-zone towns and country matching (2018 Q37); Israel, Arab states, diplomatic relations and the Arab Peace Initiative (2023 Q94). Official keys not held locally. (✅ owner 06)
**Breakdown:**
- Cover each named concept and its statement-level distinctions.
- No answer letter is recorded or inferred.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018 Q24; 2018 Q37; 2023 Q94. (✅ owner 06)
**Links:** IR-2045, IR-2046, IR-2044
**Trap:** Do not supply an answer letter; the key is not held. (✅ owner 06)
**Mains hook:** Not Mains; use for Prelims statement coverage.
**Back:** *"Twenty-four two-state, thirty-seven towns, ninety-four Arab Peace: map the concept, skip the key."*

---

### GROUP — 07 India-Africa Development and Digital Partnership

### Flashcard IR-2055 — India-Africa partnership: human capital, institutions and digital layer
**Front:** What are the pillars of India's Africa partnership?
**Definition (learn):** A demand-driven partnership built on human capital (training, scholarships), joint institutions, digital connectivity and co-development. (✅ owner 07)
**Breakdown:**
- Political framework: India-Africa Forum Summit (IR-2056).
- Capacity: USD 500 million HRD grant (IR-2059) and 1600 training positions (IR-2060).
- Institutions: nineteen joint institutions (IR-2061).
- Students: 10,000–15,000 (IR-2062).
- Digital: Pan African e-Network to e-VBAB (IR-2063, IR-2064).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed. (✅ owner 07)
**Links:** IR-2056, IR-2059, IR-2064, IR-2076
**Trap:** Africa is not primarily a trade-and-investment story in this owner's framing. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Structure answers on the pillars, then test delivery.
**Back:** *"Train, build, connect, co-develop: the African pillars."*

### Flashcard IR-2056 — India-Africa Forum Summit (IAFS) editions and date conflicts
**Front:** Name the four IAFS editions, venues and dates, and flag the conflicts.
**Definition (learn):** The umbrella political framework convening India and African states/AU. (✅ owner 07; ✅ Wikipedia: IAFS, fetched)
**Breakdown:**
- IAFS-I: New Delhi. Owner: 8–9 April 2008. Wikipedia: 4–8 April 2008. (⚠️ conflict: flagged)
- IAFS-II: Addis Ababa, 2011 (owner gives 24–25 May 2011; Wikipedia gives no date). (⚠️)
- IAFS-III: New Delhi. Owner: 26–29 October 2015. Wikipedia: 26–30 October, summit day 29 October. (⚠️ conflict: flagged)
- IAFS-IV: announced 23 April 2026; postponed 21 May 2026 (see IR-2057).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Dates above; conflicts flagged. (✅ owner 07; ✅ Wikipedia)
**Links:** IR-2057, IR-2058, IR-2055
**Trap:** The summit cycle is not regular; the decade gap after 2015 is an analytical point. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Use the gap to distinguish programme continuity from summit-level political continuity.
**Back:** *"Oh-eight Delhi, eleven Addis, fifteen Delhi: then the long wait."*

### Flashcard IR-2057 — IAFS-IV: announced 23 April 2026, postponed 21 May 2026
**Front:** What happened to the fourth India-Africa Forum Summit in 2026?
**Definition (learn):** IAFS-IV was announced for New Delhi and then postponed by India and the African Union. (✅ owner 07 dates; ✅ Wikipedia: scheduled 28–31 May 2026)
**Breakdown:**
- Announced: 23 April 2026 (owner).
- Scheduled: 28–31 May 2026 (Wikipedia).
- Postponed: 21 May 2026 (owner and Wikipedia).
- Reason: owner says "health situation"; Wikipedia says Ebola outbreak in the DRC, citing the joint statement's "evolving health situation in parts of Africa." (⚠️ conflict in the reason wording; flagged)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 23 Apr 2026; 21 May 2026; 28–31 May 2026 scheduled. (✅ owner; ✅ Wikipedia)
**Links:** IR-2056, IR-2080, IR-2083
**Trap:** IAFS-IV is announced and postponed, not held. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Use the postponement honestly; do not claim a fourth summit outcome.
**Back:** *"Announced April, postponed May: a summit that did not happen."*

### Flashcard IR-2058 — IAFS-I (2008): African partners and absences
**Front:** Who attended IAFS-I in New Delhi in 2008 and what did it cover?
**Definition (learn):** The first meeting of India's leaders with African countries chosen by the AU. (✅ Wikipedia: IAFS)
**Breakdown:**
- Fourteen African countries chosen by the AU (Wikipedia).
- Libya and Egypt heads of state did not attend (Wikipedia).
- Agenda: agriculture, trade, industry and investment, peace and security, good governance, ICT (Wikipedia).
- Pledge: support for health and education projects (Wikipedia).
- Top concerns: rising oil and food prices (Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2008 New Delhi. (✅ Wikipedia)
**Links:** IR-2056, IR-2059
**Trap:** The $500 million grant is not on the Wikipedia outcome list (IR-2059). (✅ Wikipedia; ⚠️ owner)
**Mains hook:** Use IAFS-I to show that agenda breadth preceded the later capacity pillar.
**Back:** *"Oh-eight Delhi: fourteen states, two absences, health and schools promised."*

### Flashcard IR-2059 — USD 500 million HRD and capacity-building grant at IAFS-I
**Front:** What was the $500 million grant, and how firm is the attribution?
**Definition (learn):** India announced a USD 500 million grant for human resource development and capacity building at the first India-Africa Forum Summit (Tharoor). (✅ owner 07, Tharoor)
**Breakdown:**
- Purpose: HRD and capacity building.
- Source: Tharoor, *Pax Indica*.
- Verification: fetched Wikipedia IAFS page does not list this grant (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** USD 500 million; first IAFS. (✅ owner 07; ⚠️ not confirmed by fetched page)
**Links:** IR-2056, IR-2058, IR-2060
**Trap:** Do not treat a grant as disbursed funds for every project. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Use as the capacity-building anchor, flagged as Tharoor-sourced.
**Back:** *"Five hundred million for people first: a Tharoor figure, verify before you quote."*

### Flashcard IR-2060 — 1600 technical-cooperation training positions for Africa
**Front:** What is the 1600 figure and how does it differ from ITEC's global figure?
**Definition (learn):** 1600 training positions offered under India's technical cooperation programme specifically to Africa (Tharoor). (✅ owner 07)
**Breakdown:**
- Africa-specific.
- Separate from ITEC's global "more than two lakh" figure (IR-2067).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1600 positions. (✅ owner 07)
**Links:** IR-2059, IR-2067
**Trap:** Do not merge 1600 Africa positions with ITEC's global trainee total. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Use 1600 as the Africa-specific capacity figure and ITEC as global context.
**Back:** *"Sixteen hundred for Africa, two lakh for the world: keep them apart."*

### Flashcard IR-2061 — Nineteen jointly established institutions
**Front:** Name the nineteen institutions India is establishing with the AU Commission and member states.
**Definition (learn):** Institutions being established on African soil jointly with the African Union Commission and member states. (✅ owner 07, Tharoor)
**Breakdown:**
- India-Africa Institute of Information Technology.
- India-Africa Institute of Foreign Trade.
- India-Africa Institute of Educational Planning and Administration.
- India-Africa Diamond Institute.
- Ten vocational training centres.
- Five human settlement institutes.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 19 institutions. (✅ owner 07)
**Links:** IR-2055, IR-2078, IR-2079
**Trap:** Announcement is not operational status; verify functioning. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Joint governance is evidence of co-development, provided the institutions are functioning.
**Back:** *"Nineteen: IT, trade, planning, diamonds, ten vocational, five settlements."*

### Flashcard IR-2062 — 10,000–15,000 African students in India
**Front:** How many African students are studying in India at any time?
**Definition (learn):** Tharoor's estimate of African students in various parts of India at any given time. (✅ owner 07)
**Breakdown:**
- Range: 10,000–15,000.
- Channel: education diplomacy and human-capital multiplier.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Tharoor, undated estimate. (✅ owner 07)
**Links:** IR-2055, IR-2061
**Trap:** Treat as an estimate, not a dated count. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Use student mobility as a people-to-people link.
**Back:** *"Ten to fifteen thousand students: a link, not a census."*

### Flashcard IR-2063 — Pan African e-Network (concluded 2017)
**Front:** What was the Pan African e-Network and when did it conclude?
**Definition (learn):** A tele-education and ICT connectivity project to bridge the digital divide with African partners. (✅ owner 07, Tharoor)
**Breakdown:**
- Purpose: tele-education and ICT connectivity.
- Status: concluded in 2017 (MEA record, owner 07).
- Successor: e-VBAB (IR-2064).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Concluded 2017. (✅ owner 07)
**Links:** IR-2064, IR-2055, IR-2081
**Trap:** It is not India's ongoing flagship platform. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Trace the precursor-to-successor chain as evidence of institutional continuity.
**Back:** *"Built for the digital divide, closed in 2017, reborn as e-VBAB."*

### Flashcard IR-2064 — e-VBAB (e-VidyaBharati and e-ArogyaBharati) network
**Front:** What is e-VBAB, when was its MEA-TCIL agreement signed and when was it launched?
**Definition (learn):** The successor tele-education and tele-medicine network to the Pan African e-Network. (✅ owner 07)
**Breakdown:**
- Full name: e-VidyaBharati and e-ArogyaBharati Network Project.
- MEA-TCIL implementation agreement: 10 September 2018.
- Launched: 2019.
- Coverage (MEA April 2026 brief): 22 African countries; 15,116 scholarships offered.
- Scholarships offered are an input measure, not completions. (✅ owner 07)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 10 Sep 2018; 2019; 22 countries; 15,116. (✅ owner 07)
**Links:** IR-2063, IR-2065, IR-2073
**Trap:** Offered is not completed. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Use e-VBAB as the digital-partnership evidence, with the input-versus-outcome caveat.
**Back:** *"Ten September 2018 deal, 2019 launch, 22 countries, scholarships offered not earned."*

### Flashcard IR-2065 — India-SADC Memorandum of Understanding on Economic Cooperation (2 July 2024)
**Front:** When and where was the India-SADC MoU signed, and what does it cover?
**Definition (learn):** A dated MoU on economic cooperation with the Southern African Development Community. (✅ owner 07)
**Breakdown:**
- Signed: 2 July 2024, at the SADC Secretariat (owner 07; venue city not stated in owner).
- Signatories: SADC Executive Secretary Elias Mpedi Magosi; India's High Commissioner to Botswana and SADC Special Representative Bharath Kumar Kuthati.
- Frame: SADC RISDP 2020–2030 and Digital Transformation Strategy.
- Ten areas: industrialisation; human and social development; new and emerging technologies (ICT, connectivity, access, DPI); trade and investment; disaster risk management; women-led development; private sector; space; green growth; research and innovation.
- Renews an India-SADC MoU of 14 October 1997.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2 July 2024; 14 October 1997. (✅ owner 07)
**Links:** IR-2063, IR-2064, IR-2066, IR-2080
**Trap:** An MoU is intent; entry into force and delivery are separate. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Use as the current dated digital and DPI instrument; avoid claiming continent-wide rollout.
**Back:** *"Second of July twenty-twenty-four: ten pillars, one SADC, MoU not milestone."*

### Flashcard IR-2066 — Lines of Credit and trade to Africa (MEA, April 2026)
**Front:** What are the MEA figures for Lines of Credit and India-Africa trade?
**Definition (learn):** MEA's April 2026 Africa brief figures for India's development finance and trade with Africa. (📰 owner 07)
**Breakdown:**
- More than 190 Lines of Credit.
- Value: over USD 10 billion.
- Recipients: 41 African countries.
- India-Africa trade FY2024-25: USD 81.99 billion.
- LoCs extended are commitments, not disbursements. (⚠️)
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** April 2026 brief; FY2024-25 trade. (📰 owner 07)
**Links:** IR-2065, IR-2080, IR-2093
**Trap:** Extended LoC is not disbursed money. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Qualify the scale with commitment versus delivery.
**Back:** *"Over one hundred ninety lines, ten billion, forty-one states: commitments, not cash."*

### Flashcard IR-2067 — ITEC: more than two lakh trainees from 160+ countries
**Front:** What does ITEC do and what is its global training figure?
**Definition (learn):** The Indian Technical and Economic Cooperation Programme, a broader technical-cooperation architecture with civilian and defence training. (✅ owner 07; Economic Survey 2025-26)
**Breakdown:**
- Figure: trained more than two lakh persons from over 160 countries.
- Also a cultural-diplomacy tool, especially in the South Asian region.
- Global figure, not Africa-specific.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Economic Survey 2025-26. (✅ owner 07)
**Links:** IR-2060, IR-2099, IR-2135
**Trap:** The two-lakh figure is not Africa-specific. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Use ITEC as context and the 1600 figure as the Africa instrument.
**Back:** *"Two lakh worldwide, sixteen hundred for Africa: global context, Africa-specific figure."*

### Flashcard IR-2068 — India-Africa Defence Ministers Conclave and Defence Dialogue
**Front:** What are the India-Africa defence milestones at Lucknow (2020) and Gandhinagar (2022)?
**Definition (learn):** Institutional defence dialogue between India and African states. (✅ owner 07)
**Breakdown:**
- First conclave: Lucknow, February 2020, Lucknow Declaration.
- Second India-Africa Defence Dialogue: Gandhinagar, October 2022, declaration on defence and security cooperation.
- Covers training and capacity building.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Feb 2020; Oct 2022. (✅ owner 07)
**Links:** IR-2069, IR-2073
**Trap:** Declarations are not operational capability transfer. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Show the defence dimension is institutional and training-based.
**Back:** *"Lucknow twenty-twenty, Gandhinagar twenty-twenty-two: declarations, training, no alliance."*

### Flashcard IR-2069 — AFINDEX (Africa-India Field Training Exercise)
**Front:** What is AFINDEX and when was it held?
**Definition (learn):** The Africa-India Field Training Exercise, a peacekeeping and humanitarian mine-action cooperation route. (✅ owner 07)
**Breakdown:**
- Held in India in 2019 and March 2023.
- Purpose: interoperability and training networks.
- Limit: episodic exercises do not make an alliance.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019; March 2023. (✅ owner 07)
**Links:** IR-2068
**Trap:** Exercise is not alliance. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Use AFINDEX as proof of training depth.
**Back:** *"Nineteen and twenty-three: peacekeeping drills, not a pact."*

### Flashcard IR-2070 — African Union permanent G20 membership (9 September 2023)
**Front:** When and where did the African Union become a permanent G20 member?
**Definition (learn):** The AU was admitted as a permanent G20 member at the New Delhi Summit. (✅ owner 07)
**Breakdown:**
- Date: 9 September 2023.
- Venue: G20 New Delhi Summit.
- Limit: one forum's seat, not systemic UN or Bretton Woods reform.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 9 Sep 2023. (✅ owner 07)
**Links:** IR-2056, IR-2095, IR-2091
**Trap:** G20 membership does not solve Africa's financing or UN representation. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Use as an achieved representation outcome, scaled honestly.
**Back:** *"Nine September twenty-three: one seat at the G20 table, not the whole room."*

### Flashcard IR-2071 — International Solar Alliance (entered into force 6 December 2017; HQ Gurugram)
**Front:** When did the ISA Framework Agreement enter into force and where is its headquarters?
**Definition (learn):** A treaty-based intergovernmental solar organisation. (✅ owner 07)
**Breakdown:**
- Entry into force: 6 December 2017.
- HQ: Gurugram, India.
- Gives African members solar finance aggregation, capacity building and technology cooperation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 6 Dec 2017; Gurugram. (✅ owner 07)
**Links:** IR-2072, IR-2091
**Trap:** Membership does not prove a project was delivered. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Use ISA as a permanent institution converted from a Global South concern.
**Back:** *"Six December seventeen: solar treaty, Gurugram home, African members."*

### Flashcard IR-2072 — Coalition for Disaster Resilient Infrastructure (CDRI), launched 23 September 2019
**Front:** When and where was CDRI launched and why is it relevant to Africa?
**Definition (learn):** A New Delhi-headquartered coalition for resilient infrastructure launched at the UN Climate Action Summit. (✅ owner 07)
**Breakdown:**
- Launch: 23 September 2019, UN Climate Action Summit.
- HQ: New Delhi.
- Extends India-Africa cooperation to climate-resilient infrastructure.
- Membership count: see IR-2094.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 23 Sep 2019. (✅ owner 07)
**Links:** IR-2071, IR-2094
**Trap:** Membership is not delivered projects. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Climate-resilient infrastructure extends India's Africa offer beyond credit and digital.
**Back:** *"Twenty-three September nineteen: resilience coalition, Delhi headquarters, projects still to prove."*

### Flashcard IR-2073 — Health and pharmaceutical partnership (tele-medicine and generics)
**Front:** What health cooperation does India offer Africa, and what are its limits?
**Definition (learn):** Tele-medicine via e-ArogyaBharati plus affordable generic medicines and health training. (✅ owner 07 for tele-medicine; ⚠️ owner for pharma)
**Breakdown:**
- Tele-medicine: Pan African e-Network to e-VBAB (IR-2064).
- Pharmaceuticals: affordable generics, vaccines, health training (⚠️ owner).
- Limits: regulation, local manufacturing, procurement and supply continuity.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed. (✅/⚠️ owner 07)
**Links:** IR-2064, IR-2068
**Trap:** Low cost does not equal secure supply chain. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Pair access with local manufacturing and procurement questions.
**Back:** *"Tele-clinic and generic pill: access grows when supply and rules hold."*

### Flashcard IR-2074 — Sahel and West African coups, 2020–23 (Mali, Guinea, Burkina Faso, Niger)
**Front:** Which West African coups does the owner link to project delay and risk?
**Definition (learn):** Military coups in Mali, Guinea, Burkina Faso and Niger during 2020–23, which the owner uses to show political and security volatility. (⚠️ owner 07)
**Breakdown:**
- Effects: project delay, commercial risk, partner selection complexity.
- Limit: do not generalise Sahel instability to the whole continent.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2020–23. (⚠️ owner)
**Links:** IR-2080, IR-2083, IR-2084
**Trap:** Sahel is not all of Africa. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Use region-specific risk to argue for resilience-aware policy.
**Back:** *"Four Sahel-area coups, not a continent: region-specific risk."*

### Flashcard IR-2075 — FOCAC comparator (China-Africa)
**Front:** How should India's Africa model be compared with China's FOCAC route?
**Definition (learn):** The Forum on China-Africa Cooperation and Chinese infrastructure finance, a large-scale alternative partnership. (⚠️ owner 07)
**Breakdown:**
- India's comparative strengths: training, affordable technology, DPI, demand-driven projects.
- Compare delivery, finance terms, local ownership and sector fit.
- Do not caricature China as extractive.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated in owner. (⚠️)
**Links:** IR-2076, IR-2078
**Trap:** African states do not passively choose India or China. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Compare the two models on delivery, not rhetoric.
**Back:** *"FOCAC is large, India is trained and demand-led: compare outcomes, not slogans."*

### Flashcard IR-2076 — Co-development and non-intrusive model (Tharoor)
**Front:** What do "co-development," "demand-driven" and "non-intrusive" mean in India's Africa model?
**Definition (learn):** The 2025 PYQ framing of mutual respect, co-development and long-term institutional partnership; Tharoor's "non-intrusive" support to democratic institutions. (✅ owner 07)
**Breakdown:**
- Demand-driven: African partners set priorities.
- Non-intrusive: avoids conditionality.
- Co-development: joint institution governance.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Tharoor; undated. (✅ owner 07)
**Links:** IR-2055, IR-2077, IR-2078, IR-2082
**Trap:** Non-intrusive does not mean no political engagement. (✅ owner 07 advanced)
**Mains hook:** Distinguish the three terms and evidence each.
**Back:** *"Africa sets the agenda, India supplies capacity, nobody dictates the template."*

### Flashcard IR-2077 — Digital sovereignty and DPI in Africa
**Front:** What is the digital-sovereignty concern in India's Africa digital partnership?
**Definition (learn):** The concern that DPI projects should strengthen, not replace, an African partner's control over its digital infrastructure and data. (⚠️ owner 07)
**Breakdown:**
- India-SADC MoU frames its tech pillar against SADC's RISDP and Digital Transformation Strategy (IR-2065).
- Design evidence is not proof that data governance is resolved.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated. (⚠️)
**Links:** IR-2065, IR-2076, IR-2078
**Trap:** Framing a DPI deal as partner-led is not proof of sovereignty. (⚠️)
**Mains hook:** Use sovereignty as the constraint on DPI claims.
**Back:** *"DPI for the partner, not the partner's data for us: sovereignty is tested later."*

### Flashcard IR-2078 — Implementation and maintenance test
**Front:** What is the implementation and maintenance test for India-Africa partnership?
**Definition (learn):** The check of whether jointly built institutions and networks keep functioning, staffed and used after launch. (⚠️ owner 07 analytical test)
**Breakdown:**
- Tests "long-term institutional partnership" in the 2025 PYQ.
- Distinct from the announcement.
- Requires dated evidence.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Analytical. (⚠️)
**Links:** IR-2061, IR-2082, IR-2083
**Trap:** Announcement scale is not sustained functioning. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Make the test your 15-mark evaluation criterion.
**Back:** *"Launch is a photo, maintenance is the film."*

### Flashcard IR-2079 — Trap: India-Africa is primarily trade; Pan African e-Network is ongoing
**Front:** Two traps: is India's Africa partnership primarily trade, and is the Pan African e-Network still operating?
**Definition (learn):** No on both. The owner emphasises capacity and institutions; the e-Network concluded in 2017. (✅ owner 07)
**Breakdown:**
- Trade volume is Economy's domain (IR-2066).
- E-Network concluded 2017; e-VBAB succeeded it.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2017; 2019. (✅ owner 07)
**Links:** IR-2063, IR-2064, IR-2066
**Trap:** Both errors are routine. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Name the successor when citing the digital partnership.
**Back:** *"Trade is the ledger, not the story; e-Network closed, e-VBAB opened."*

### Flashcard IR-2080 — Trap: ITEC is Africa-specific; LoCs are disbursed; MoUs deliver
**Front:** Which three Africa figures and instruments are commonly overstated?
**Definition (learn):** ITEC's two-lakh figure is global; LoCs are commitments; MoUs are intent. (✅ owner 07)
**Breakdown:**
- ITEC: IR-2067.
- LoCs: IR-2066.
- MoU: IR-2065.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** See cards. (✅ owner 07)
**Links:** IR-2067, IR-2066, IR-2065
**Trap:** The three errors appear in many scored answers. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Qualify each figure by its instrument type.
**Back:** *"Global is not Africa, commitment is not cash, intent is not delivery."*

### Flashcard IR-2081 — Trap: Africa is a single actor; AU membership equals state membership
**Front:** Is Africa a single actor, and is AU membership the same as state membership in every regional community?
**Definition (learn):** No. Africa has many states and regional communities. (✅ owner 07)
**Breakdown:**
- AU membership differs from membership of each regional economic community.
- Line of credit is not a grant; approval is not completion.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Analytical. (✅ owner 07)
**Links:** IR-2070, IR-2074, IR-2066
**Trap:** Generalising "Africa" erases the country-specific delivery story. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Name the country and institution in every claim.
**Back:** *"Fifty-four states, many communities: name who, not Africa."*

### Flashcard IR-2082 — PYQ route: 2025 GS-II Q9, India-Africa digital partnership (10 marks, 150 words)
**Front:** How should the 2025 Q9 answer on India-Africa digital partnership be built?
**Definition (learn):** Verified PYQ: "India-Africa digital partnership is achieving mutual respect, co-development and long-term institutional partnerships. Elaborate." (✅ owner 07)
**Breakdown:**
- (1) Pan African e-Network to e-VBAB (IR-2063, IR-2064).
- (2) India-SADC MoU 2 July 2024 (IR-2065).
- (3) Nineteen joint institutions (IR-2061).
- (4) Non-intrusive demand-driven model and student mobility (IR-2076, IR-2062).
- (5) Avoid overstating continent-wide DPI (IR-2078).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 GS-II Q9; 10 marks; 150 words. (✅ owner 07)
**Links:** IR-2063, IR-2064, IR-2065, IR-2076, IR-2078
**Trap:** Do not reduce to digital cooperation only. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Separate mutual respect, co-development and long-term partnership, then evidence each.
**Back:** *"Respect, co-develop, last: three claims, three proofs."*

### Flashcard IR-2083 — PYQ route: 2021 GS-II Q9, India's influence in Africa (10 marks, 150 words)
**Front:** How should the 2021 question on India's influence in Africa be answered?
**Definition (learn):** Verified PYQ: "If the last few decades were of Asia's growth story, the next few are expected to be of Africa's. In the light of this statement, examine India's influence in Africa in recent years." (✅ owner 07)
**Breakdown:**
- Structure by development partnership, trade and investment, capacity building, diplomacy and implementation constraints.
- Do not reduce to digital cooperation.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2021 GS-II Q9; Examine; 10 marks; 150 words. (✅ owner 07)
**Links:** IR-2055, IR-2057, IR-2078
**Trap:** A single-sector answer fails the directive. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Use the growth framing, then test delivery and competition.
**Back:** *"Asia's decades, Africa's next: examine influence, not one sector."*

### Flashcard IR-2084 — Prelims coverage: 2023 Q98 and 2024 Q91 (Sahel coups; keys not inferred)
**Front:** What do the 2023 Q98 and 2024 Q91 objective demands cover?
**Definition (learn):** 2023 Q98: military coups in Chad, Guinea, Mali, Sudan and West Africa (key not held locally). 2024 Q91: instability and military coups in the Sahel (official Set-A key held locally; no answer recorded). (✅ owner 07)
**Breakdown:**
- Cover coups by country and region.
- Do not infer an answer letter.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2023 Q98; 2024 Q91. (✅ owner 07)
**Links:** IR-2074, IR-2057
**Trap:** The 2023 Q98 stem (Chad, Guinea, Mali, Sudan, West Africa) is broader than the Sahel-only 2024 Q91 stem; keep the two stems distinct. (✅ owner 07)
**Mains hook:** Not Mains; use for statement-level coverage.
**Back:** *"Ninety-eight coups, ninety-one Sahel: know who, skip the key."*

---

### GROUP — 08 Global South and Development Partnering

### Flashcard IR-2085 — Global South: a political-development category, not an organisation
**Front:** What is the Global South and why is it not a formal bloc?
**Definition (learn):** A political-development category for developing and least-developed countries across Asia, Africa and Latin America, defined by shared challenges and positioning rather than fixed institutional membership. (⚠️ owner 08 definition)
**Breakdown:**
- Not a treaty body.
- Engaged through G77, NAM, BRICS and dedicated summits.
- Core grievance: representation deficit (IR-2099).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Definition only. (⚠️)
**Links:** IR-2086, IR-2087, IR-2099, IR-2100
**Trap:** Global South and G77 are not synonyms. (✅ owner 08)
**Mains hook:** Define the category first, then specify coalitions.
**Back:** *"Category, not club: shared challenges, no fixed roll."*

### Flashcard IR-2086 — Comparison: Global South vs G77 vs NAM vs BRICS
**Front:** How do the Global South, G77, NAM and BRICS differ?
**Definition (learn):** Four overlapping but distinct categories or institutions. (✅ owner 08)
**Breakdown:**
- Global South: category (IR-2085).
- G77: specific UN-system negotiating coalition, over 120 members (IR-2087).
- NAM: historical bloc-avoidance heritage; narrowed salience (IR-2088).
- BRICS: distinct membership and financial-architecture agenda (IR-2097).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed. (✅ owner 08)
**Links:** IR-2085, IR-2087, IR-2088, IR-2097, IR-2107
**Trap:** BRICS is not the Global South; G77 is not the Global South. (✅ owner 08)
**Mains hook:** Use a four-row comparison when asked to distinguish categories.
**Back:** *"Category, Seventy-seven, Non-Aligned, BRICS: four lenses, not one."*

### Flashcard IR-2087 — G77: over 120 developing countries
**Front:** What is the G77 and how many members does it have?
**Definition (learn):** A long-standing UN-system negotiating coalition of developing countries. (✅ owner 08, Tharoor)
**Breakdown:**
- Over 120 developing countries (Tharoor).
- Negotiating bloc inside UN system.
- Distinct from the broad Global South category.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Over 120 (Tharoor, undated). (✅ owner 08)
**Links:** IR-2086, IR-2085
**Trap:** The name does not mean 77 countries; the figure is now over 120. (✅ owner 08; ⚠️ naming origin not fetched)
**Mains hook:** Use G77 as the negotiating bloc, not as a proxy for the whole South.
**Back:** *"Seventy-seven in name, over one hundred twenty in count."*

### Flashcard IR-2088 — Non-Aligned Movement (NAM): heritage and narrowed salience
**Front:** What is NAM's role in the Global South framing today?
**Definition (learn):** A historical bloc-avoidance grouping whose contemporary institutional salience has narrowed. (⚠️ owner 08)
**Breakdown:**
- Historical solidarity value.
- Narrowed institutional salience.
- Cross-link to topic 01 (non-alignment).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated in owner. (⚠️)
**Links:** IR-2086, IR-2085
**Trap:** NAM is not the same as the Global South; do not treat it as a current decision-making bloc. (⚠️)
**Mains hook:** Acknowledge historical solidarity and its narrowing.
**Back:** *"Non-aligned heritage, quieter voice: history, not current mandate."*

### Flashcard IR-2089 — Voice of Global South Summit (VoGSS) editions
**Front:** When and how were the three Voice of Global South Summits held?
**Definition (learn):** India's own virtual convening platform aggregating developing-country priorities. (✅ owner 08)
**Breakdown:**
- First: 12–13 January 2023, virtual.
- Second: 17 November 2023, virtual.
- Third: 17 August 2024, virtual.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 12–13 Jan 2023; 17 Nov 2023; 17 Aug 2024. (✅ owner 08)
**Links:** IR-2090, IR-2091, IR-2107
**Trap:** A convening platform is not a decision-making body. (✅ owner 08)
**Mains hook:** Cite the dated third edition as the convening example.
**Back:** *"Jan 23, Nov 23, Aug 24: three virtual rooms, no binding vote."*

### Flashcard IR-2090 — VoGSS fourth edition: not recorded as held or announced (as of 3 August 2026)
**Front:** Has a fourth Voice of Global South Summit been held or announced?
**Definition (learn):** The owner records no fourth edition as held or announced as of 3 August 2026, and the 6 September 2026 recheck says none is officially recorded. (✅ owner 08)
**Breakdown:**
- Status: not recorded.
- Do not invent a fourth edition.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As-of 3 Aug 2026; recheck 6 Sep 2026. (✅ owner 08)
**Links:** IR-2089, IR-2091
**Trap:** Fabricating a fourth edition is a serious error. (✅ owner 08)
**Mains hook:** State the convening gap honestly.
**Back:** *"Three so far, none announced: honest silence beats invented summits."*

### Flashcard IR-2091 — Global Development Compact (proposed 17 August 2024)
**Front:** What is the Global Development Compact, when was it proposed and what are its four parts?
**Definition (learn):** A four-fold framework proposed by India at the third VoGSS. (✅ owner 08)
**Breakdown:**
- Trade for development.
- Capacity building for sustainable growth.
- Technology sharing.
- Project-specific concessional finance and grants.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Proposed 17 Aug 2024. (✅ owner 08)
**Links:** IR-2089, IR-2090
**Trap:** It is a proposal, not an operational institution with a secretariat or budget. (✅ owner 08)
**Mains hook:** Treat as an agenda item unless a dated operational instrument is cited.
**Back:** *"Trade, capacity, technology, concessional finance: four proposals, no secretariat yet."*

### Flashcard IR-2092 — Outcome benchmark: AU permanent G20 membership (9 September 2023)
**Front:** Why is the AU's G20 membership the benchmark for "success" in Global South representation?
**Definition (learn):** The one concrete representation change achieved in this cycle. (✅ owner 08)
**Breakdown:**
- One forum, one seat.
- Not systemic redistribution.
- Full card: IR-2070.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 9 Sep 2023. (✅ owner 08)
**Links:** IR-2070, IR-2096
**Trap:** Scaling AU's seat up to reform of UN or IMF is an overclaim. (✅ owner 08)
**Mains hook:** Use it as realistic-scale success.
**Back:** *"One seat, one forum: success at realistic scale."*

### Flashcard IR-2093 — India's development-partnership footprint worldwide (MEA)
**Front:** What are the MEA global figures for Lines of Credit?
**Definition (learn):** More than 260 Lines of Credit worth over USD 26 billion across roughly 62 countries. (📰 owner 08, MEA)
**Breakdown:**
- Scope: worldwide, not Africa-only (Africa: IR-2066).
- Commitments, not disbursements (⚠️).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 260+; USD 26bn+; about 62 countries; as recorded by owner. (📰 owner 08)
**Links:** IR-2066, IR-2092
**Trap:** Africa-only and worldwide figures differ. (✅ owner 07 and 08)
**Mains hook:** Use global LoCs for context and Africa LoCs for the regional case.
**Back:** *"Two-sixty lines, twenty-six billion, sixty-two countries: worldwide commitments."*

### Flashcard IR-2094 — CDRI membership: 70 (58 countries and 12 partner organisations), June 2026
**Front:** What was CDRI's membership count as of June 2026?
**Definition (learn):** The Coalition for Disaster Resilient Infrastructure's membership as recorded by the owner. (📰 owner 08)
**Breakdown:**
- Total: 70.
- Countries: 58.
- Partner organisations: 12.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** June 2026. (📰 owner 08)
**Links:** IR-2072, IR-2095
**Trap:** Membership counts are not delivered projects. (✅ owner 08)
**Mains hook:** Use membership as evidence of coalition, not of project delivery.
**Back:** *"Seventy members in June 2026: fifty-eight countries, twelve partners, zero projects proved."*

### Flashcard IR-2095 — Global Biofuels Alliance (launched 9 September 2023)
**Front:** When was the Global Biofuels Alliance launched and how many members did it have as of 30 July 2026?
**Definition (learn):** An India-initiated plurilateral platform for biofuels. (📰 owner 08)
**Breakdown:**
- Launched: 9 September 2023.
- Members as of 30 July 2026: 25 countries and 12 international organisations agreed to join (owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 9 Sep 2023; 30 Jul 2026 count. (📰 owner 08)
**Links:** IR-2094, IR-2070
**Trap:** Agreeing to join is not the same as membership proven by a formal accession list. (⚠️)
**Mains hook:** Use it as a plurilateral platform example, dated.
**Back:** *"Nine September twenty-three: biofuel alliance, twenty-five countries, count dated."*

### Flashcard IR-2096 — India's UNSC candidature for 2028–29 (launched 13 July 2026)
**Front:** When did India launch its UNSC non-permanent candidature for 2028–29?
**Definition (learn):** A concrete institutional ask linking the representation-deficit argument to the UN Security Council. (📰 owner 08)
**Breakdown:**
- Launched: 13 July 2026.
- Term sought: non-permanent seat 2028–29.
- Reform architecture: topic 12.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 13 July 2026. (📰 owner 08)
**Links:** IR-2099, IR-2092
**Trap:** A candidature is not an election result. (✅ owner 08)
**Mains hook:** Present the candidature as a live test of the representation argument.
**Back:** *"Thirteen July twenty-six: a bid for 2028–29, not a seat won."*

### Flashcard IR-2097 — BRICS: distinct membership and partner-country category (24 October 2024)
**Front:** What is BRICS's distinct role, and when was its partner-country category created?
**Definition (learn):** BRICS is a financial-voice grouping with its own membership and agenda, partially overlapping with the Global South. (⚠️ owner 08)
**Breakdown:**
- 17th summit: Rio de Janeiro Declaration, 6–7 July 2025 (owner, topic 10 for full treatment).
- Partner-country category: 24 October 2024 (owner).
- Full members and partner countries differ in rights.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 6–7 Jul 2025; 24 Oct 2024. (⚠️ owner)
**Links:** IR-2086, IR-2098
**Trap:** BRICS is not a larger G77. (✅ owner 08)
**Mains hook:** Use the partner-category point to show participation without membership rights.
**Back:** *"Rio July twenty-five, partners October twenty-four: wider door, same room."*

### Flashcard IR-2098 — South-South cooperation: complement, not replacement
**Front:** What is South-South cooperation and how is it positioned against North-South aid?
**Definition (learn):** Development cooperation among developing countries (technology, capacity, trade). (⚠️ owner 08)
**Breakdown:**
- Complements North-South assistance.
- Does not replace it.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not dated. (⚠️)
**Links:** IR-2097, IR-2085, IR-2091
**Trap:** South-South is not a substitute for North-South aid. (✅ owner 08)
**Mains hook:** Present South-South as complement with clear limits.
**Back:** *"South helps South, North still matters: complement, not substitute."*

### Flashcard IR-2099 — Representation deficit: the unifying Global South grievance
**Front:** What is the representation deficit and why is it the unifying grievance?
**Definition (learn):** The argument that UNSC composition and IMF/World Bank governance under-represent developing countries relative to population and economic weight. (⚠️ owner 08)
**Breakdown:**
- UNSC permanent membership structure.
- IMF/World Bank quota structures.
- Feeds into UN and Bretton Woods reform debate (topic 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Structural. (⚠️)
**Links:** IR-2096, IR-2100, IR-2101
**Trap:** Agenda-setting on representation is not institutional reform. (✅ owner 08)
**Mains hook:** Use as the unifying thread in a "grievance not homogeneity" answer.
**Back:** *"Old seats, new population: the grievance that unites a diverse South."*

### Flashcard IR-2100 — Trap: Global South is homogeneous
**Front:** Is the Global South a unified negotiating bloc?
**Definition (learn):** No. It contains diverse income levels, regional blocs and great-power alignments. (✅ owner 08)
**Breakdown:**
- Internal contestation: climate burden-sharing (small island states, resource exporters, emerging economies).
- Unity on climate finance is negotiated, not natural.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Analytical. (✅ owner 08)
**Links:** IR-2085, IR-2099
**Trap:** Treating the South as unified is a scoring error. (✅ owner 08)
**Mains hook:** Name the internal divides when claiming solidarity.
**Back:** *"One grievance, many interests: unity is negotiated, not given."*

### Flashcard IR-2101 — India's "potential leader" framing (Sikri)
**Front:** What does Sikri mean by India as a "potential leader" for weaker states?
**Definition (learn):** Aspirational framing: weaker states seeking to rally behind a stronger independent global player. (✅ owner 08)
**Breakdown:**
- Aspirational, not settled status.
- Depends on issue-specific alignment.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Sikri, undated. (✅ owner 08)
**Links:** IR-2102, IR-2106, IR-2110
**Trap:** Treating "potential leader" as achieved leadership. (✅ owner 08)
**Mains hook:** Frame India's role as aspirational leadership with verifiable outcomes required.
**Back:** *"Potential, not presidency: rally to India only where interests align."*

### Flashcard IR-2102 — Brazil and Latin America: bilateral and grouping overlap
**Front:** What is India's relationship with Brazil and what does grouping overlap mean?
**Definition (learn):** Brazil is a bilateral strategic partner and co-member of BRICS, IBSA, G20 and G4. (✅ owner 08)
**Breakdown:**
- Cooperation: biofuels, agriculture, pharmaceuticals, defence and global-governance reform.
- Grouping overlap does not replace bilateral delivery.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner, undated. (✅ owner 08)
**Links:** IR-2097, IR-2103, IR-2105
**Trap:** Overlapping membership does not make bilateral ties automatic. (✅ owner 08)
**Mains hook:** Separate grouping engagement from bilateral delivery.
**Back:** *"Brazil: BRICS, IBSA, G20, G4 on paper; bilateral work on the ground."*

### Flashcard IR-2103 — India-MERCOSUR Preferential Trade Agreement (2004; operational 1 June 2009)
**Front:** When was the India-MERCOSUR PTA signed and when did it become operational?
**Definition (learn):** A limited goods-preference agreement, not a comprehensive FTA. (✅ owner 08)
**Breakdown:**
- Signed: 2004.
- Operational: 1 June 2009.
- Type: limited goods preference, not comprehensive FTA.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2004; 1 June 2009. (✅ owner 08)
**Links:** IR-2102, IR-2104, IR-2105
**Trap:** PTA is not FTA; do not call it comprehensive. (✅ owner 08)
**Mains hook:** Use the PTA-versus-FTA distinction to show trade depth limits in Latin America.
**Back:** *"Two-thousand-four signed, two-thousand-nine live: a preference, not a free trade."*

### Flashcard IR-2104 — India-Chile PTA expansion (2017) and CEPA negotiations (2025)
**Front:** What are the dated milestones of the India-Chile trade track?
**Definition (learn):** The existing PTA was expanded in 2017; CEPA terms of reference were signed in May 2025; a fourth round concluded on 5 December 2025. (✅ owner 08)
**Breakdown:**
- PTA expansion: 2017.
- CEPA ToR: May 2025.
- Fourth round: 5 December 2025.
- Under negotiation, not concluded.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2017; May 2025; 5 Dec 2025. (✅ owner 08)
**Links:** IR-2103, IR-2102
**Trap:** Negotiation progress is not an agreement in force. (✅ owner 08)
**Mains hook:** Qualify trade claims with negotiation status.
**Back:** *"Seventeen expansion, twenty-five ToR, December round four: talks, not treaty."*

### Flashcard IR-2105 — India-CELAC dialogue and the 33-state region
**Front:** What is CELAC and how does India use it?
**Definition (learn):** The Community of Latin American and Caribbean States, a diverse 33-state regional grouping that gives India a regional diplomatic route. (⚠️ owner 08)
**Breakdown:**
- 33 states.
- Constraints: distance, limited connectivity, language, awareness, institutional density.
- Commercial conversion is modest.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 33 states. (⚠️ owner)
**Links:** IR-2102, IR-2103
**Trap:** Latin America is not a homogeneous commodity frontier. (✅ owner 08)
**Mains hook:** Present CELAC as a diplomatic route, not a market guarantee.
**Back:** *"Thirty-three states, distance and language: diplomatic door, modest trade."*

### Flashcard IR-2106 — Norm entrepreneurship on Global South issues
**Front:** What is norm entrepreneurship and how does it apply to India?
**Definition (learn):** Active agenda-setting on a normative claim to reshape how the international community frames an issue, such as vaccine equity, DPI as a public good or climate-finance justice. (⚠️ owner 08)
**Breakdown:**
- Vaccine equity.
- DPI-sharing as public good.
- Climate-finance justice.
- Distinct from coalition membership.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Analytical. (⚠️)
**Links:** IR-2101, IR-2110
**Trap:** Setting an agenda item is not securing the institutional reform (IMF quotas). (⚠️ owner 08 advanced)
**Mains hook:** Distinguish norm setting from institutional change.
**Back:** *"Agenda set, reform pending: norms move before institutions do."*

### Flashcard IR-2107 — Trap: "Global South" equals G77; BRICS equals Global South
**Front:** Two traps: is Global South the same as G77, and is BRICS the same as Global South?
**Definition (learn):** No on both. (✅ owner 08)
**Breakdown:**
- G77: specific UN coalition (IR-2087).
- BRICS: specific grouping (IR-2097).
- Global South: category (IR-2085).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** See cards. (✅ owner 08)
**Links:** IR-2085, IR-2086, IR-2097
**Trap:** Both are frequent scoring errors. (✅ owner 08)
**Mains hook:** Name the category and the institution separately.
**Back:** *"Category is not G77, BRICS is not South: name each box."*

### Flashcard IR-2108 — Trap: VoGSS is a decision body; GDC is operational
**Front:** Two traps: is VoGSS a decision-making body, and is the Global Development Compact an operating institution?
**Definition (learn):** No on both. VoGSS is convening; GDC is a proposal. (✅ owner 08)
**Breakdown:**
- VoGSS: agenda-setting platform (IR-2089).
- GDC: proposal (IR-2091).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 17 Aug 2024. (✅ owner 08)
**Links:** IR-2089, IR-2091
**Trap:** Treat both as agenda items unless a dated operational instrument is cited. (✅ owner 08)
**Mains hook:** Use "convening" and "proposal" as precise terms.
**Back:** *"Convene, propose, do not decide: no binding VoGSS, no operating GDC yet."*

### Flashcard IR-2109 — PYQ route: 2019 GS-II Q19, India's image as leader of oppressed nations (15 marks, 250 words)
**Front:** How should the 2019 question on India as leader of oppressed and marginalised nations be answered?
**Definition (learn):** Verified PYQ: India's image as leader of the oppressed and marginalised nations (Elaborate, 15 marks, 250 words; word limit taken from the instruction block, owner note). (✅ owner 08)
**Breakdown:**
- Context and shared grievance (IR-2099).
- Convening and coalition (IR-2089).
- Development instrument (IR-2091).
- Institutional outcome (IR-2092).
- Representation claim and delivery/legitimacy limit (IR-2101, IR-2100).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019 GS-II Q19; 15 marks; 250 words. (✅ owner 08)
**Links:** IR-2099, IR-2089, IR-2101, IR-2100
**Trap:** Claiming achieved leadership. (✅ owner 08)
**Mains hook:** Thesis: India can aggregate Global South priorities but earns leadership only through verifiable outcomes.
**Back:** *"Nineteen oppressed nations: aggregate, deliver, then claim."*

### Flashcard IR-2110 — Dual-track positioning: coalitions plus own convening platform
**Front:** What is India's dual-track Global South strategy?
**Definition (learn):** India participates in G77, NAM and BRICS while convening its own platform (VoGSS) and pursuing issue-specific norm entrepreneurship. (⚠️ owner 08 advanced)
**Breakdown:**
- Track 1: coalitions (IR-2086).
- Track 2: own platform (IR-2089).
- Track 3: norm entrepreneurship (IR-2106).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Analytical. (⚠️)
**Links:** IR-2101, IR-2106, IR-2089
**Trap:** Dual membership is not speaking "for" the South; "within" is the defensible framing. (✅ owner 08 advanced)
**Mains hook:** Argue "within, not for" as the defensible framing.
**Back:** *"Within the South, not for it: join, convene, set norms."*

### GROUP — 09 Indian Diaspora, Consular Protection and Soft Power

### Flashcard IR-2111 — Diaspora diplomacy: welfare track and soft-power track
**Front:** What are the two linked tracks of India's diaspora diplomacy?
**Definition (learn):** The welfare and consular-protection track, and the soft-power and cultural-projection track. (✅ owner 09)
**Breakdown:**
- Welfare: MADAD, Protector General of Emigrants, ICWF, PBBY, e-Migrate.
- Soft power: ICCR, ITEC, culture, yoga, Bollywood, cuisine, IT reputation.
- Diaspora as a two-way bridge, not a controllable lever (IR-2132).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner structure. (✅ owner 09)
**Links:** IR-2112, IR-2122, IR-2131, IR-2141
**Trap:** Welfare and soft power are separate tracks. (✅ owner 09)
**Mains hook:** Separate the two tracks in a 10-mark answer.
**Back:** *"Welfare protects, soft power projects: two tracks, one bridge."*

### Flashcard IR-2112 — Diaspora categories: NRI, PIO, OCI and labour migrants
**Front:** Distinguish NRIs, PIOs, OCI cardholders and labour migrants.
**Definition (learn):** NRIs are Indian citizens residing abroad; PIOs are foreign nationals of Indian origin (historical category); OCI cardholders are foreign citizens of Indian origin holding OCI status; labour migrants are a distinct migrant category, mainly in the Gulf. (✅ owner 09; ✅ Wikipedia: OCI)
**Breakdown:**
- NRI: Indian citizen abroad.
- PIO: foreign national of Indian descent (card merged into OCI in 2015; IR-2117).
- OCI: not dual citizenship (IR-2116).
- Labour migrant: ECR-category welfare (IR-2123).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner definitions. (✅ owner 09)
**Links:** IR-2113, IR-2114, IR-2116, IR-2117
**Trap:** They are not interchangeable categories. (✅ owner 09)
**Mains hook:** Use the categories to pick the right legal and welfare instrument.
**Back:** *"Citizen abroad, foreigner of Indian origin, OCI, labour migrant: four doors."*

### Flashcard IR-2113 — MEA overseas Indian population (as on January 2026)
**Front:** What does MEA's January 2026 population table record?
**Definition (learn):** The overseas-Indian population stock recorded by MEA as on January 2026. (✅ owner 09)
**Breakdown:**
- Total: 35,421,987.
- NRIs: 19,571,375.
- PIOs: 15,850,612.
- Top hosts: USA 6,079,221; UAE 4,344,008; Malaysia 2,902,370; Saudi Arabia 2,750,551.
- Population stock, not citizenship or OCI counts (⚠️ owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As on January 2026. (✅ owner 09)
**Links:** IR-2031, IR-2112, IR-2050
**Trap:** Not an OCI card count. (✅ owner 09)
**Mains hook:** Use as stock with date; not as citizenship.
**Back:** *"Thirty-five million, nineteen NRI, fifteen PIO: stock as on January 2026."*

### Flashcard IR-2114 — OCI scheme: origin in the Citizenship (Amendment) Act 2003 and launch on 9 January 2006
**Front:** What is the legal basis of OCI and when was it launched?
**Definition (learn):** Overseas Citizenship of India, a status for persons of Indian origin holding foreign citizenship, created by the Citizenship (Amendment) Act, 2003. (✅ Wikipedia: OCI)
**Breakdown:**
- Legal basis: Citizenship (Amendment) Act, 2003, inserting Section 7A into the Citizenship Act, 1955 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Launch: at the Pravasi Bharatiya Divas convention, Hyderabad, 9 January 2006 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Initial coverage: 16 specified countries under the 2003 Act (✅ Wikipedia).
- 2005 amendment widened eligibility to PIOs of all countries except Pakistan (✅ Wikipedia).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2003 Act; Section 7A; 9 Jan 2006; 16 countries initially. (✅ Wikipedia)
**Links:** IR-2115, IR-2116, IR-2117, IR-2118
**Trap:** Section 7A is the OCI registration provision, not a citizenship grant to all diaspora. (✅ Wikipedia)
**Mains hook:** Trace the legal basis when discussing diaspora status; keep detailed citizenship law to Polity.
**Back:** *"Two-thousand-three Act, Section 7A, Hyderabad 2006: a status, not a passport."*

### Flashcard IR-2115 — OCI eligibility rules (grandparent rule and exclusions)
**Front:** Who is eligible for OCI registration, and who is excluded?
**Definition (learn):** Registration-on-application categories and disqualifications under the OCI scheme. (✅ Wikipedia: OCI)
**Breakdown:**
- Foreign citizen who was an Indian citizen on or after 26 January 1950.
- Foreign citizen from territory that became part of India after 15 August 1947.
- Foreign citizen eligible for Indian citizenship on 26 January 1950.
- Children, grandchildren and great-grandchildren of such persons (grandparent rule).
- Minor child with an Indian parent.
