- Climate: carbon-border measures (IR-3058) and green-subsidy competition.
- India's dual position: diversification beneficiary (topic 03) and target of others' measures.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Owner 11 advanced diagram lists the three channels.
**Links:** IR-3042; IR-3058; IR-3062; IR-3018 (NATO-linked tech and sanctions transmission).
**Trap:** Classical tariff-only protectionism is not the full picture; a single instrument (CBAM) can be trade, industrial and climate policy at once.
**Mains hook:** Name the three channels, then give one dated example for each.
**Back:** *"Trade, tech, climate: three pipes into India."*

### Flashcard IR-3044 — Protectionism vs sanctions vs export controls (comparison pair)
**Front:** How do protectionism, sanctions and export controls differ?
**Definition (learn):** Protectionism is broad trade shielding via tariffs, quotas or local-content rules; sanctions and export controls restrict specific countries, entities or technologies for foreign-policy or security reasons (⚠️ owner 11).
**Breakdown:**
- Protectionism: across-the-board competitiveness; Economy owns tariff mechanics.
- Sanctions: target countries or entities for foreign-policy aims.
- Export controls: target specific technologies or transfers.
- All three are instruments of sovereign nationalism in the economic domain (⚠️ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** No statistic; definitional.
**Links:** IR-3042; IR-3043; IR-3016 (RCEP exit as a trade choice).
**Trap:** Do not treat them as one instrument; sanctions are targeted, protectionism is general.
**Mains hook:** The 15-mark route asks for mechanism plus effect on India: protection of domestic industry vs targeted restriction on partners.
**Back:** *"Tariffs fence the market; sanctions bar the actor; export controls bar the tech."*

### Flashcard IR-3045 — WTO as the background rules framework
**Front:** What does the WTO do now, and what are its founding date, seat and membership?
**Definition (learn):** The WTO is the reference framework for trade rules, dispute settlement and notifications, even as its negotiating function has weakened.
**Breakdown:**
- Founded 1 January 1995, Geneva; India a founding member (✅ owner 11; ✅ Wikipedia: 1 January 1995, Geneva).
- Membership: 166 (✅ Wikipedia).
- Predecessor: GATT (1947; Wikipedia states 1947 as a treaty of 23 countries and elsewhere 1948; ⚠️ source wording varies).
- Ministerial conferences: MC13 Abu Dhabi, 26 Feb-2 Mar 2024 (Abu Dhabi Ministerial Declaration); MC14 Yaoundé, Cameroon, 26-30 Mar 2026 (✅ owner 11).
- Wikipedia calls the 1996 Singapore meeting the inaugural ministerial (⚠️ numbering not given).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1 Jan 1995; Geneva; 166 members; MC13 2024; MC14 2026.
**Links:** IR-3046; IR-3047; IR-3048; IR-3049; IR-3065 (2018 WTO reform PYQ).
**Trap:** "Weakened" is not "irrelevant" (owner 11 trap). Do not confuse WTO with GATT.
**Mains hook:** Use WTO's dispute-settlement function as a continuing rule baseline even while negotiation stalls.
**Back:** *"Geneva ninety-five, one hundred sixty-six members, rules alive, talks weak."*

### Flashcard IR-3046 — Appellate Body crisis and India outside MPIA
**Front:** When did the WTO Appellate Body lose its quorum and its members, and what is India's position on the MPIA?
**Definition (learn):** The Appellate Body hears appeals in WTO dispute settlement; it lost its three-member quorum for new appeals and then its membership. The MPIA is an interim appeal-arbitration arrangement under DSU Article 25.
**Breakdown:**
- Lost its three-member quorum for new appeals: 10 December 2019 (✅ owner 11, 11 advanced).
- Zero members since 30 November 2020 (✅ owner 11).
- MPIA: about 30 participants; India is not among them (✅ owner 11 advanced).
- Effect: an adverse panel report against India can be appealed "into the void", and India cannot obtain binding appellate review against others (⚠️ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 10 Dec 2019 quorum lost; 30 Nov 2020 zero members; DSU Art 25.
**Links:** IR-3045; IR-3048; IR-3063 (WTO irrelevance trap).
**Trap:** The crisis is two-sided: it shields India from adverse rulings and denies India binding review of others (owner 11 advanced).
**Mains hook:** Argue that a weakened appellate tier raises the cost of trade disputes, not only their likelihood.
**Back:** *"Quorum lost 2019, members gone 2020, MPIA without India."*

### Flashcard IR-3047 — WTO Agreement on Fisheries Subsidies: adoption to acceptance
**Front:** Trace the Fisheries Subsidies Agreement from adoption to India's acceptance.
**Definition (learn):** A WTO agreement adopted at MC12 that enters into force on reaching two-thirds acceptance, then binds each member individually on its own acceptance.
**Breakdown:**
- Adopted at MC12, 17 June 2022 (✅ owner 11).
- Entered into force 15 September 2025 on two-thirds acceptance (✅ owner 11).
- India deposited its instrument of acceptance on 20 July 2026 (⚠️ owner 11, not fetched).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 17 Jun 2022; 15 Sep 2025; 20 Jul 2026 (India).
**Links:** IR-3045; IR-3048 (MC14 negotiations); IR-3016 (not all trade rules are FTA rules).
**Trap:** Adoption ≠ entry into force ≠ acceptance by an individual member; keep the three stages separate (IR-3050).
**Mains hook:** Use it as the worked example of the adopt → enter into force → accept sequence.
**Back:** *"Adopted 2022, live 2025, India accepts 2026."*

### Flashcard IR-3048 — MC14 Investment Facilitation for Development: consensus, not majority
**Front:** Why did the Investment Facilitation for Development agreement fail at MC14 despite near-unanimous support?
**Definition (learn):** WTO decisions rest on consensus, not majority. The IFD agreement's incorporation needed consensus and did not get it.
**Breakdown:**
- MC14 at Yaoundé, 26-30 March 2026 (✅ owner 11).
- 165 of 166 members supported incorporation; 129 parties supported implementation (✅ owner 11 advanced).
- Consensus absent, so not incorporated (✅ owner 11).
- Structural parallel: the procedural veto, as in the UN Security Council (IR-3076) (⚠️ owner 11 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 165/166 incorporation support; 129 implementation support; MC14 26-30 Mar 2026.
**Links:** IR-3045; IR-3046; IR-3076.
**Trap:** Near-unanimity is not consensus; a single absence can block (owner 11 trap).
**Mains hook:** Show that WTO vetoes are procedural; a 165-of-166 majority still fails.
**Back:** *"165 yes, one no, zero decision: consensus is the gate."*

### Flashcard IR-3049 — Agreement on Agriculture, Bali peace clause and India's public stockholding
**Front:** What three areas does the Agreement on Agriculture discipline, and what protects India's food-security procurement?
**Definition (learn):** The Agreement on Agriculture disciplines market access, domestic support and export competition. The 2013 Bali peace clause protects eligible developing-country public-stockholding programmes from specified legal challenge (✅ owner 11).
**Breakdown:**
- Three disciplines: market access, domestic support, export competition (✅ owner 11).
- Bali peace clause (2013): interim protection, with notification and programme conditions (✅ owner 11).
- India's demand: keep public stockholding and special and differential treatment; improve subsidy and market-access rules (⚠️ owner 11).
- Limitation: interim, not the permanent settlement India seeks (✅ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Bali 2013 peace clause; three disciplines.
**Links:** IR-3045; IR-3048; IR-3065.
**Trap:** The peace clause is interim protection, not a permanent solution.
**Mains hook:** WTO reform is distributive: producer support, consumer food security and export effects must be balanced.
**Back:** *"Three disciplines, one Bali shield for stockholding, still not permanent."*

### Flashcard IR-3050 — Legal stage ladder for trade agreements
**Front:** What are the stages from negotiation to economic effect for an FTA or CEPA?
**Definition (learn):** Negotiation launch, concluded text, signature, ratification, entry into force, utilisation and economic outcome are different stages; each has its own legal meaning (✅ owner 11 semantic ownership).
**Breakdown:**
- Launch → concluded (text agreed) → signed → ratified/operationalised → in force → utilised → economic outcome.
- Gross trade change does not by itself prove agreement impact (✅ owner 11).
- Legal classification depends on WTO membership and RTA notifications (✅ owner 11 verified note).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Use specific dates for each stage.
**Links:** IR-3051; IR-3052; IR-3053; IR-3054; IR-3055.
**Trap:** Concluded is not signed; signed is not in force; in force is not utilised.
**Mains hook:** Always name the exact legal stage when citing an FTA (owner 11 Mains angle).
**Back:** *"Launch, conclude, sign, ratify, enter, use, earn."*

### Flashcard IR-3051 — India-UAE CEPA and India-Australia ECTA (in force)
**Front:** When did the India-UAE CEPA and India-Australia ECTA enter into force?
**Definition (learn):** Two in-force bilateral trade agreements in India's FTA cycle.
**Breakdown:**
- India-UAE CEPA: in force 1 May 2022 (✅ owner 11).
- India-Australia ECTA: in force 29 December 2022 (✅ owner 11).
- Both are bilateral preferential arrangements (⚠️ scope and rules of origin not detailed in owner).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1 May 2022; 29 Dec 2022.
**Links:** IR-3050; IR-3052; IR-3055.
**Trap:** ECTA (Australia) is an Early Conclusion Trade Agreement, distinct from a full CEPA; do not treat the two terms as interchangeable (⚠️ term expansion from general knowledge).
**Mains hook:** Use as in-force examples of India's FTA turn that complements WTO engagement.
**Back:** *"UAE in May twenty-two, Australia in December twenty-two."*

### Flashcard IR-3052 — India-EFTA TEPA (signed 2024, in force 2025)
**Front:** When was the India-EFTA TEPA signed and when did it enter into force, and what is its investment aspiration?
**Definition (learn):** A Trade and Economic Partnership Agreement with the European Free Trade Association states.
**Breakdown:**
- Signed 10 March 2024 (✅ owner 11).
- In force 1 October 2025 (✅ owner 11).
- Shared objective: USD 100 billion of investment and one million direct jobs over 15 years; a shared objective, not realised outcome (⚠️ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 10 Mar 2024 signed; 1 Oct 2025 in force.
**Links:** IR-3050; IR-3053; IR-3057 (RCEP contrast).
**Trap:** The USD 100bn / one million jobs figures are targets, not outcomes.
**Mains hook:** TEPA shows India seeking market access while staying out of RCEP (owner 11 advanced).
**Back:** *"Signed March twenty-four, live October twenty-five, targets not results."*

### Flashcard IR-3053 — India-UK CETA and the Double Contributions Convention
**Front:** What is the date pattern of the India-UK CETA, and what came into force on the same day?
**Definition (learn):** A comprehensive economic and trade agreement with the United Kingdom.
**Breakdown:**
- Signed London, 24 July 2025 (✅ owner 11).
- In force 15 July 2026 (✅ owner 11; ⚠️ not fetched).
- Double Contributions Convention in force the same day (✅ owner 11; ⚠️ not fetched).
- Up to 60 months' home-country social-security coverage for detached workers (⚠️ owner 11 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 24 Jul 2025 signed; 15 Jul 2026 in force; DCC same day; up to 60 months.
**Links:** IR-3050; IR-3052; IR-3054.
**Trap:** Signature (July 2025) and entry into force (July 2026) are a year apart; do not swap them.
**Mains hook:** CETA plus DCC shows trade and labour mobility linked through social security.
**Back:** *"Signed July twenty-five, in force July twenty-six, two conventions, one day."*

### Flashcard IR-3054 — India-EU FTA: concluded, not signed, not in force
**Front:** What is the legal status of the India-EU FTA as of the owner's verification?
**Definition (learn):** Negotiations concluded at the 16th India-EU Summit; the agreement is not yet signed, ratified or in force.
**Breakdown:**
- Negotiations concluded 27 January 2026 at the 16th India-EU Summit (✅ owner 11; ⚠️ not fetched).
- Not signed, ratified or in force (⚠️ owner 11).
- Same summit: Security and Defence Partnership signed; negotiations on Security of Information Agreement launched; "Towards 2030: India-EU Joint Comprehensive Strategic Agenda" adopted (⚠️ owner 11 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 27 Jan 2026 concluded.
**Links:** IR-3050; IR-3053; IR-3058 (CBAM affects EU trade).
**Trap:** Concluded negotiations are not an agreement in force; signed, launched and adopted are three different legal verbs.
**Mains hook:** Use the India-EU case to show the status vocabulary in one communiqué.
**Back:** *"Concluded in January, unsigned, unratified, not yet live."*

### Flashcard IR-3055 — India-Oman CEPA, India-Chile CEPA and India-New Zealand FTA (status dates)
**Front:** What are the statuses and dates of the Oman, Chile and New Zealand agreements?
**Definition (learn):** Three further entries in India's FTA cycle, each at a different legal stage.
**Breakdown:**
- India-Oman CEPA: signed 18 December 2025 (✅ owner 11).
- India-Chile CEPA: negotiations concluded 5 December 2025 (✅ owner 11).
- India-New Zealand FTA: signed 27 April 2026 (✅ owner 11).
- None of these is recorded as in force in the owner (⚠️ owner status as of 3 August 2026).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 18 Dec 2025; 5 Dec 2025; 27 Apr 2026.
**Links:** IR-3050; IR-3051; IR-3054.
**Trap:** Signed is not in force; "concluded" is not "signed"; do not assume in force.
**Mains hook:** Map the three stages across five agreements to show the status vocabulary.
**Back:** *"Oman signed, Chile concluded, New Zealand signed, none yet claimed in force."*

### Flashcard IR-3056 — India-US trade relations and the 2025-26 tariff cycle
**Front:** What US measures shaped India's market access in 2025-26, and what is their legal character?
**Definition (learn):** A partner's unilateral executive action reshaped market access within months; these are another state's domestic measures creating exposure, not obligations on India.
**Breakdown:**
- 25% reciprocal duty effective 7 August 2025 (✅ owner 11; ⚠️ not fetched).
- Additional 25% Russian-oil-linked duty effective 27 August 2025; removed 7 February 2026 (✅ owner 11).
- IEEPA additional duties ended February 2026 (✅ owner 11).
- Separate 10% Section 301 duty from 24 July 2026 (✅ owner 11).
- Interim framework and continuing negotiation; no comprehensive agreement in force (✅ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As of owner verification 3 Aug 2026.
**Links:** IR-3042; IR-3043; IR-3050.
**Trap:** Duties are a partner's measures; do not describe them as India's obligations. Avoid stating unverified rates beyond those listed.
**Mains hook:** Volatility, not level, is the transmission mechanism: a measure lasting under six months still shaped a whole negotiating cycle (⚠️ owner 11 advanced).
**Back:** *"August twenty-five at twenty-five percent, removed February twenty-six, section 301 next."*

### Flashcard IR-3057 — RCEP: India announced it would not join (4 November 2019)
**Front:** When did India announce it would not join RCEP, and which states does RCEP link?
**Definition (learn):** RCEP links ASEAN with Australia, China, Japan, South Korea and New Zealand.
**Breakdown:**
- India announced non-participation on 4 November 2019 (✅ owner 11).
- ASEAN has separate FTAs with India and China, but RCEP is a single regional agreement with five other partners (✅ owner 10).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 4 Nov 2019.
**Links:** IR-3016; IR-3055; IR-3042.
**Trap:** India's ASEAN FTA ≠ RCEP membership.
**Mains hook:** RCEP exit alongside FTA turn shows selective openness.
**Back:** *"Four November, nineteen: RCEP without India, FTAs with India."*

### Flashcard IR-3058 — EU Carbon Border Adjustment Mechanism (definitive regime, 1 January 2026)
**Front:** When did the EU CBAM enter its definitive regime, and why is it a trade-and-climate hybrid?
**Definition (learn):** A carbon-border measure that is simultaneously a trade measure, an industrial-policy tool and a climate instrument (✅ owner 11; ⚠️ owner-only).
**Breakdown:**
- Definitive regime from 1 January 2026 (✅ owner 11, 📰).
- Affects Indian exporters into the EU; India is not bound by EU internal law (⚠️ owner 11).
- Risk: perceived as disguised protectionism; contested on proportionality (⚠️ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1 Jan 2026.
**Links:** IR-3043; IR-3059 (India NDC); IR-3060 (loss and damage).
**Trap:** CBAM is not purely environmental (owner 11 trap); its scope needs separate dated verification.
**Mains hook:** Climate policy as trade policy is a classification challenge (owner 11 advanced).
**Back:** *"Carbon at the border, definitive from January twenty-six."*

### Flashcard IR-3059 — India's updated 2022 NDC targets
**Front:** What are India's 2030 targets under the updated 2022 NDC?
**Definition (learn):** India's climate commitments under the UNFCCC framework, updated in 2022.
**Breakdown:**
- By 2030: reduce GDP emissions intensity by 45% from 2005 levels (✅ owner 11).
- About 50% cumulative installed electric-power capacity from non-fossil sources by 2030 (✅ owner 11).
- Net-zero 2070: long-term target (✅ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 45% (2005 base); 50% non-fossil capacity; net-zero 2070.
**Links:** IR-3058; IR-3066 (2022 climate fora PYQ); IR-3060.
**Trap:** Targets are commitments, not proof of outcomes; the 2005 base year must not be dropped.
**Mains hook:** India's position combines equity and CBDR-RC, finance and technology access with domestic mitigation (⚠️ owner analysis).
**Back:** *"Forty-five by thirty, half from clean power, seventy net-zero."*

### Flashcard IR-3060 — Loss and Damage funding arrangements (COP28)
**Front:** What did COP28 operationalise on loss and damage, and what remains contested?
**Definition (learn):** Funding arrangements for vulnerable-country loss and damage beyond mitigation and adaptation.
**Breakdown:**
- Operationalised at COP28 (✅ owner 11).
- Significance: recognition of loss beyond mitigation and adaptation (✅ owner 11).
- Contested: adequacy, access, contributor scale (✅ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** COP28.
**Links:** IR-3059; IR-3058; IR-3066.
**Trap:** Operationalisation is not full adequacy.
**Mains hook:** Use loss and damage as the Global South equity argument in climate diplomacy.
**Back:** *"COP twenty-eight opened the loss purse; size remains the argument."*

### Flashcard IR-3061 — Harvard Economic Complexity Index: India's rank (2023)
**Front:** What was India's rank on the Harvard Economic Complexity Index in 2023?
**Definition (learn):** An index of export diversification and productive knowledge across 145 countries.
**Breakdown:**
- India ranked 44th of 145 in 2023 (✅ owner 11, citing Economic Survey 2025-26).
- Up from 57th in 2013; unchanged since 2019 (✅ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 44th of 145 (2023); 57th in 2013.
**Links:** IR-3042; IR-3043.
**Trap:** A structural indicator, not a trade volume figure; do not mix with trade-surplus numbers.
**Mains hook:** Use as an export-diversification indicator behind India's FTA and supply-chain push.
**Back:** *"Forty-four of one-forty-five, climbing from fifty-seven, then flat."*

### Flashcard IR-3062 — De-risking vs decoupling vs friend-shoring (supply-chain responses)
**Front:** What is de-risking and how does it differ from decoupling?
**Definition (learn):** De-risking reduces over-concentration in one supplier or partner while sustaining engagement; decoupling is full separation (⚠️ owner 11 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- De-risking: moderate, diversifying, keeps trade and investment flows (⚠️).
- Decoupling: full separation of supply chains (⚠️).
- Friend-shoring: relocating supply to aligned partners; topic 03 owns detail (⚠️ owner 11).
- India as a diversification destination (topic 03; owner 11 basic).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Analytical; no statistic.
**Links:** IR-3043; IR-3042; IR-3056.
**Trap:** De-risking is not decoupling; treat it as the precise term for the moderate response.
**Mains hook:** Transition costs are real: alternative capacity is neither instant nor costless (owner 11).
**Back:** *"De-risk to diversify; decouple to disconnect."*

### Flashcard IR-3063 — FTA proliferation as globalisation continuity, not nationalist retreat
**Front:** Why is the FTA turn not evidence of anti-globalisation?
**Definition (learn):** FTAs are a globalisation instrument at the bilateral or plurilateral level, pursued because multilateral liberalisation stalled (⚠️ owner 11).
**Breakdown:**
- Liberalisation continues through a different institutional form (✅ owner 11 advanced).
- Cost: patchwork of rules of origin and standards, compliance complexity (⚠️).
- WTO remains baseline rules framework; FTAs do not replace it (IR-3045).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Qualitative.
**Links:** IR-3042; IR-3045; IR-3050.
**Trap:** "FTAs are inherently anti-globalisation" is wrong (owner 11 trap).
**Mains hook:** "FTA proliferation reflects continuity, not retreat" (owner 11 advanced Mains route).
**Back:** *"Bilateral deals carry globalisation through another door."*

### Flashcard IR-3064 — PYQ 2025 GS-II Q10: waning globalisation and sovereign nationalism (10 marks, 150 words)
**Front:** Outline the answer route to the 2025 Q10 demand.
**Definition (learn):** "With the waning of globalization, post-Cold War world is becoming a site of sovereign nationalism. Elucidate." (✅ owner 11; ✅ stem confirmed against the locally held 2025 paper per owner note).
**Breakdown:**
- Define both forces as co-present (IR-3042).
- Evidence: protectionism (US tariff cycle, IR-3056), sanctions and export controls (IR-3044), FTA-over-WTO shift (IR-3063), MC14 IFD failure (IR-3048), EU CBAM (IR-3058).
- India's dual navigation: TEPA and CETA for market access, RCEP exit, strategic-sector protection (IR-3052, IR-3053, IR-3057).
- Avoid fabricated statistics; cite only date-stamped measures.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 GS-II Q10; 10 marks; 150 words.
**Links:** IR-3042; IR-3043; IR-3048; IR-3056; IR-3058.
**Trap:** Do not use "ended" or sequential framing.
**Mains hook:** Co-presence and India's dual role carry the answer.
**Back:** *"Waning not ended; three channels; India in both lanes."*

### Flashcard IR-3065 — PYQ 2018 GS-II Q19: key areas of WTO reform in a trade war (15 marks, 250 words)
**Front:** What does the 2018 Q19 demand ask, and what are the key reform areas?
**Definition (learn):** "What are the key areas of WTO reform in the context of trade war?" (✅ owner 11 ledger; 2018 paper not held locally, so the stem is not reconstructed here).
**Breakdown:**
- Demand: 15 marks, 250 words (✅ owner 11 ledger).
- Reform areas to cover: dispute-settlement reform (IR-3046), special and differential treatment, agriculture and public stockholding (IR-3049), consensus-based decision-making (IR-3048), e-commerce and fisheries (IR-3047).
- Note: the owner's MC13 list includes dispute-settlement reform, S&DT, LDC graduation and e-commerce (✅ owner 11).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2018 GS-II Q19; 15 marks.
**Links:** IR-3045; IR-3046; IR-3049.
**Trap:** Do not present reform areas as completed; the MC14 e-commerce moratorium remained unresolved (✅ owner 11).
**Mains hook:** Link reform areas to trade-war pressures: tariffs and dispute settlement failures.
**Back:** *"Dispute, subsidy, consensus, e-commerce: the WTO reform list."*

### Flashcard IR-3066 — PYQ 2022 GS-II Q20: India's climate policy in international fora (15 marks, 250 words)
**Front:** How does the 2022 Q20 demand divide between IR (topic 11) and institutions (topic 12)?
**Definition (learn):** "Describe briefly" India's changing climate policy in international fora; cross-cutting between climate regime and multilateral fora (✅ owner 11 and owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- Trade and negotiating half: NDC targets (IR-3059), CBDR-RC, climate finance, CBAM (IR-3058). Owner 11 holds this half.
- Institutional half: UNFCCC, COP process and multilateral fora (owner 12 holds institutional structure).
- Route: context, core dimensions, evidence, counterpoint, conclusion.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2022 GS-II Q20; 15 marks; 250 words.
**Links:** IR-3059; IR-3058; IR-3060; IR-3090 (UN institutional routing).
**Trap:** The 2022 demand is cross-cutting; do not write only one half.
**Mains hook:** Show both the negotiating strategy and institutional venues.
**Back:** *"Two halves: trade and climate at the table, UN institutions in the room."*

### Flashcard IR-3067 — Current anchors for globalisation and trade (dated, with stage)
**Front:** Which dated markers anchor any current trade-and-globalisation answer?
**Definition (learn):** Dated events with legal stage recorded in owner 11 (✅ owner 11 📰; each needs separate verification before quoting).
**Breakdown:**
- 1 January 2026: EU CBAM definitive regime.
- 27 January 2026: India-EU FTA negotiations concluded.
- 15 July 2026: India-UK CETA in force.
- 1 October 2025: India-EFTA TEPA in force.
- 26-30 March 2026: WTO MC14, Yaoundé.
- 20 July 2026: India's fisheries acceptance (⚠️ not fetched).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed.
**Links:** IR-3050; IR-3052; IR-3053; IR-3054; IR-3058; IR-3048; IR-3047.
**Trap:** Name the stage (concluded, signed, in force), not "signed" by default.
**Mains hook:** Date-stamp each claim individually.
**Back:** *"CBAM January, EU talks January, TEPA October, CETA July, MC14 March."*

### GROUP — UN and International Institutions: Global Governance

### Flashcard IR-3068 — UN organs and the legal character of their outputs (concept)
**Front:** How do UN organs, specialised agencies, treaty bodies and courts differ in legal basis, and which outputs bind?
**Definition (learn):** UN organ, specialised agency, related organisation, treaty body and independent court each have distinct legal bases; General Assembly recommendations differ from binding Security Council decisions, and ICJ contentious judgments differ from advisory opinions (✅ owner 12 semantic ownership).
**Breakdown:**
- Principal organs (Charter): UNGA, UNSC, ECOSOC, ICJ, Secretariat (✅ owner 12).
- Specialised agencies: separate constitutions, e.g. UNESCO (1945), WHO (✅ owner 12).
- Treaty bodies and courts: created by treaty (ICC is a separate treaty court, IR-3092).
- Binding: UNSC decisions under Charter; UNGA recommendations are not binding; advisory opinions are authoritative but not binding (⚠️ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Verify membership, voting rule, jurisdiction and mandate from constitutive instruments (owner 12).
**Links:** IR-3069; IR-3070; IR-3073; IR-3091; IR-3092.
**Trap:** Reform proposal is not an adopted Charter amendment (owner 12 trap).
**Mains hook:** Institution test: origin/legal basis, membership/organs, mandate/instruments, implementation/enforcement, India role, reform/legitimacy (owner 12 answer architecture).
**Back:** *"Organs recommend or decide; agencies serve treaties; courts judge or advise."*

### Flashcard IR-3069 — UN General Assembly: deliberative organ and India's strengthening demand
**Front:** What is the UNGA's composition and function, and what does India want changed?
**Definition (learn):** The UNGA is the universal-membership deliberative organ. Its outputs are recommendations, and India wants it to become the primary intergovernmental legislative body (✅ owner 12 quoting Tharoor).
**Breakdown:**
- Universal membership; deliberative (✅ owner 12).
- Recommends rather than binds (✅ owner 12).
- Tharoor: India, like many developing countries, wants the General Assembly strengthened "as the primary intergovernmental legislative body, which it is not yet" (✅ owner 12 quoting Tharoor).
- Criticism: "too often a rhetorical forum, prone to declaratory effulgences without effect" (✅ owner 12 advanced).
- ⚠️ Membership count (193 states) and voting (one state, one vote; important questions two-thirds) are standard knowledge, not fetched.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Universal; recommendations.
**Links:** IR-3068; IR-3084 (dual-track reform); IR-3080 (IGN is UNGA-mandated).
**Trap:** UNGA recommendations are not binding; do not treat UNGA resolutions as Council decisions.
**Mains hook:** Distinguish the UNSC expansion demand from the UNGA-strengthening demand (owner 12 Mains probable).
**Back:** *"Universal and deliberative: the assembly that recommends, India wants it to legislate."*

### Flashcard IR-3070 — UN Security Council: composition and term structure
**Front:** How many members does the UNSC have, how are they split, and what are the terms?
**Definition (learn):** The UNSC has primary responsibility for international peace and security (Charter Article 24, per Wikipedia).
**Breakdown:**
- Total 15 (✅ owner 12; ✅ Wikipedia).
- Five permanent: China, France, Russia, UK, US (✅ owner 12; ✅ Wikipedia).
- Ten non-permanent, elected by the General Assembly for two-year terms (✅ owner 12; ✅ Wikipedia).
- Charter Article 23 governs composition (⚠️ not fetched; standard knowledge).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 15; 5 P; 10 NP; two-year terms.
**Links:** IR-3071; IR-3077 (G4); IR-3079 (African model); IR-3089 (India's terms).
**Trap:** Non-permanent members cannot veto; "permanent with veto" applies only to the five.
**Mains hook:** Representation deficit: 1945 composition against 21st-century weight (owner 12 advanced).
**Back:** *"Fifteen seats, five keep the veto, ten rotate for two years."*

### Flashcard IR-3071 — UNSC voting: veto on substantive matters, not procedure
**Front:** Which decisions can each permanent member block with its veto?
**Definition (learn):** Each permanent member can block a substantive draft resolution regardless of support among others; procedural matters are not subject to the veto (✅ Wikipedia).
**Breakdown:**
- Veto applies to substantive draft resolutions (✅ Wikipedia).
- Procedural matters: no veto (✅ Wikipedia).
- Voting threshold: nine affirmative votes; substantive decisions require nine including all five permanent members' concurrence (⚠️ standard knowledge from Charter Article 27; Wikipedia truncated before the voting section).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Nine votes (⚠️).
**Links:** IR-3070; IR-3076 (dual character); IR-3048 (WTO's procedural veto parallel).
**Trap:** The veto is not a general power to block all decisions; procedural votes cannot be vetoed (✅ Wikipedia).
**Mains hook:** The veto blocks reform as well as crises; this is the central 2025 PYQ issue (IR-3112).
**Back:** *"Substance can be vetoed; procedure cannot; nine votes always needed (⚠️)."*

### Flashcard IR-3072 — ECOSOC: economic and social coordination
**Front:** What does ECOSOC do, and what should you verify before stating its composition?
**Definition (learn):** ECOSOC coordinates economic and social work of the UN (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- One of the six principal organs (⚠️ standard knowledge).
- Coordinates economic and social cooperation (✅ owner 12).
- Membership size, term length and functional commissions: ⚠️ not verified; Wikipedia disambiguation page only, no fetched article.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** No verified numeric data from fetch.
**Links:** IR-3068; IR-3069.
**Trap:** Do not confuse ECOSOC coordination with UNSC enforcement.
**Mains hook:** Use as the economic and social coordination leg of the Charter system.
**Back:** *"ECOSOC coordinates economy and society; the Council enforces peace."*

### Flashcard IR-3073 — International Court of Justice: organ, disputes and President
**Front:** Who is the ICJ President and what are its two outputs?
**Definition (learn):** The ICJ is the UN principal judicial organ deciding disputes between states and giving advisory opinions (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- President: Yuji Iwasawa, elected 3 March 2025 (✅ owner 12).
- Outputs: contentious judgments between states (binding on parties) and advisory opinions (authoritative, not binding) (✅ owner 12).
- Seat: The Hague (⚠️ standard knowledge).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** President Yuji Iwasawa (3 Mar 2025).
**Links:** IR-3091 (climate AO); IR-3092 (ICJ vs ICC); IR-3068.
**Trap:** ICJ judges states; ICC prosecutes individuals (IR-3092).
**Mains hook:** Adjudication advances where the Council is deadlocked (owner 12 advanced).
**Back:** *"States' court, advice not orders, Iwasawa since March twenty-five."*

### Flashcard IR-3074 — UN Secretariat and the 2026 Secretary-General selection
**Front:** When does Guterres's second term end, and how was the next selection formally started?
**Definition (learn):** The Secretariat is the administrative organ led by the Secretary-General.
**Breakdown:**
- António Guterres's second term ends 31 December 2026 (✅ owner 12).
- Selection formally initiated by a joint letter of the President of the General Assembly and the President of the Security Council on 25 November 2025 (✅ owner 12).
- Process under resolution 79/327 of 5 September 2025 (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 31 Dec 2026; 25 Nov 2025 letter; res 79/327.
**Links:** IR-3085 (UN80); IR-3068.
**Trap:** Selection process ≠ Council reform; the SG is not elected by the Council alone.
**Mains hook:** A Secretariat-level transition shows a parallel reform track that bypasses Charter amendment (owner 12 advanced).
**Back:** *"Guterres ends December twenty-six; letters in November twenty-five."*

### Flashcard IR-3075 — Four structural dimensions of UN reform (concept)
**Front:** What four dimensions should UN reform analysis separate?
**Definition (learn):** Representation, veto/decision-making, financing and compliance/enforcement are separate dimensions, and the 2025 PYQ's East-West framing targets the veto dimension (✅ owner 12 advanced analysis).
**Breakdown:**
- Representation: who sits at the table.
- Veto/decision-making: P5 veto power.
- Financing: who funds and who sets budget priorities.
- Compliance/enforcement: binding decisions vs member-state implementation gap.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Analytical framework.
**Links:** IR-3076; IR-3077; IR-3086 (CTC enforcement limits); IR-3104 (financing).
**Trap:** Treating UN reform as one undifferentiated issue loses marks.
**Mains hook:** Separate the four dimensions before analysing any proposal.
**Back:** *"Seats, veto, money, enforcement: four doors to reform."*

### Flashcard IR-3076 — Veto as stabiliser and blocker (dual character)
**Front:** Why did the P5 veto exist, and how does it now block reform?
**Definition (learn):** The veto was designed to keep major powers inside the UN system, avoiding a League-of-Nations-style exit; it now blocks reform needing P5 consent (⚠️ owner 12 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Stabilising rationale: keep great powers engaged (⚠️ owner 12).
- Blocking effect: Charter amendment requires P5 ratification (⚠️ owner 12).
- Contemporary blocker: US vs Russia-China alignment (IR-3112).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not a small-state protection.
**Links:** IR-3071; IR-3075; IR-3112.
**Trap:** The veto was not designed to protect small states (owner 12 trap).
**Mains hook:** Explain the stabilising vs blocking dual character as the analytical core of the 2025 PYQ.
**Back:** *"Built to keep giants in; now keeps reform out."*

### Flashcard IR-3077 — G4: India, Brazil, Germany, Japan
**Front:** Who are the G4 and what is their reform aim?
**Definition (learn):** A coalition of four states advocating UNSC expansion in both permanent and non-permanent categories (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- Members: India, Brazil, Germany, Japan (✅ owner 12).
- Active from 2010, more concrete than the Open-Ended Working Group, which Tharoor calls "feckless" (✅ owner 12 quoting Tharoor).
- 25 September 2025 Foreign Ministers' joint statement (IR-3082).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Active since at least 2010.
**Links:** IR-3078 (counter-coalition); IR-3079 (African model); IR-3081 (India's IGN intervention).
**Trap:** G4 and the Open-Ended Working Group are different mechanisms (owner 12 trap).
**Mains hook:** The G4 shows reform advocacy is more effective as a coalition than as a diffuse process.
**Back:** *"India, Brazil, Germany, Japan: four who asked for seats together."*

### Flashcard IR-3078 — Uniting for Consensus: the counter-coalition
**Front:** What is the counter-coalition to the G4's reform model?
**Definition (learn):** A coalition of states that oppose or question new permanent seats and favour expansion centred on non-permanent or elected representation (⚠️ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- Position: longer-term or non-permanent seats rather than new permanent seats (⚠️ owner 12).
- Heterogeneous: not reducible to opposition to all expansion (⚠️ owner 12 advanced).
- Current position: needs independent, dated verification (⚠️ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** None verified.
**Links:** IR-3077; IR-3079; IR-3075.
**Trap:** Counter-coalition opposes a model, not necessarily all expansion.
**Mains hook:** Reform is blocked by disagreement among the wider membership too, not only by P5 rivalry (owner 12).
**Back:** *"Against new permanent seats, not always against seats."*

### Flashcard IR-3079 — Ezulwini Consensus and Sirte Declaration (Common African Position) and the African model
**Front:** What is the Common African Position and what Council model does it support?
**Definition (learn):** The Common African Position, enshrined in the Ezulwini Consensus and Sirte Declaration, supports African representation in both permanent and non-permanent categories, which the G4 backs (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- G4 FM statement (25 Sep 2025) backs the Common African Position (✅ owner 12).
- India's IGN position (20 Apr 2026) is on the African model: 26-member Council (✅ owner 12).
- Veto principle: existing and new permanent members must have "the same privileges and responsibilities" (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 26-member Council in India's position.
**Links:** IR-3081; IR-3082; IR-3077.
**Trap:** The African model is a model, not an adopted reform.
**Mains hook:** The African model is India's bridge to Global South consensus (owner 12 advanced).
**Back:** *"Ezulwini and Sirte set the African model; twenty-six seats in India's pitch."*

### Flashcard IR-3080 — Intergovernmental Negotiations (IGN): status and what has not happened
**Front:** Has the IGN adopted a consolidated model or started text-based negotiations?
**Definition (learn):** The UNGA-mandated informal-plenary forum where UNSC reform is discussed, without a fixed timeline (⚠️ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- Co-chairs for the 80th session, appointed 31 October 2025: Kuwait and the Netherlands (✅ owner 12).
- As of 3 August 2026, no consolidated model or decision commencing text-based negotiations (⚠️ owner 12).
- GA decided in July 2026 to continue negotiations into its 81st session (✅ owner 12 verified note; ⚠️ not fetched).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Co-chairs appointed 31 Oct 2025.
**Links:** IR-3068; IR-3069; IR-3081; IR-3113 (reform discussed vs achieved).
**Trap:** The IGN has not moved to text-based negotiation (owner 12 trap).
**Mains hook:** Describe the IGN as a process of discussion, not a negotiating text.
**Back:** *"Talks yes, text no, co-chairs Kuwait and the Netherlands."*

### Flashcard IR-3081 — India's IGN intervention, 20 April 2026
**Front:** What did India's 20 April 2026 IGN intervention set out?
**Definition (learn):** India's national position on Security Council reform, delivered on the African model (✅ owner 12; ⚠️ not fetched).
**Breakdown:**
- Aligned with L.69 and G4 statements (✅ owner 12).
- 26-member Council (✅ owner 12).
- Expansion in both categories (✅ owner 12).
- Veto "all or none": "there cannot be a sub-category within the permanent category" (✅ owner 12 advanced).
- Larger role for troop-contributing countries in mandate design (✅ owner 12 advanced).
- Non-permanent-only expansion "would not result in real and substantive reform" (✅ owner 12 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 20 Apr 2026; 26 seats.
**Links:** IR-3079; IR-3077; IR-3089.
**Trap:** A negotiating position, not reform achieved (owner 12 flags).
**Mains hook:** Use India's stated position as the dated Indian evidence in any UN-reform answer.
**Back:** *"Twenty-six seats, equal veto, no second class, April twenty-six."*

### Flashcard IR-3082 — G4 Foreign Ministers' joint statement (New York, 25 September 2025)
**Front:** What does the G4 statement say, and what is its key procedural claim?
**Definition (learn):** A joint statement issued on the margins of the 80th UN General Assembly (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- Reaffirms expansion in both permanent and non-permanent categories (✅ owner 12).
- Supports the Common African Position, Ezulwini Consensus and Sirte Declaration (✅ owner 12).
- "Strong concern over the continued absence of concrete progress" in the IGN (✅ owner 12).
- Intention to develop a "consolidated model" leading to text-based negotiations (✅ owner 12).
- "Consensus is not a decision-making requirement" (✅ owner 12).
- Reform need "not be confined to the IGN" (✅ owner 12 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 25 Sep 2025; 80th UNGA margins.
**Links:** IR-3077; IR-3079; IR-3080; IR-3112.
**Trap:** "Consensus is not a decision-making requirement" is a G4 claim, not a legal rule (owner 12 advanced).
**Mains hook:** Reframes the blockage as a contested procedural convention, not a legal bar.
**Back:** *"Twenty-five September: concern, a model, and consensus is not a requirement."*

### Flashcard IR-3083 — UN Pact for the Future (22 September 2024)
**Front:** What is the Pact for the Future, and what are its two annexes?
**Definition (learn):** A summit-level outcome document adopted at the Summit of the Future; it expresses reform intent but is not a Charter amendment (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- Adopted 22 September 2024 (✅ owner 12).
- Annex I: Global Digital Compact (✅ owner 12).
- Annex II: Declaration on Future Generations (✅ owner 12).
- Status: political framework, not binding structural change (⚠️ owner 12 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 22 Sep 2024.
**Links:** IR-3105 (digital compact); IR-3112; IR-3113.
**Trap:** The Pact is not completed UN reform (owner 12 trap).
**Mains hook:** Cite as evidence of sustained attention, not delivery (announcement-delivery gap, owner 12 study link).
**Back:** *"Twenty-two September twenty-four: digital compact and future generations, no Charter change."*

### Flashcard IR-3084 — "Renewed, not retired" UN and India's dual-track reform demand
**Front:** What is Tharoor's reform philosophy, and what are India's two reform tracks?
**Definition (learn):** "The UN is as necessary today as it was in 1945... Our search has been... for a renewed, not a retired, UN" (✅ owner 12 quoting Tharoor).
**Breakdown:**
- Track 1: UNSC expansion via G4 (IR-3077).
- Track 2: UNGA strengthening as the primary legislative body (IR-3069).
- Rejects both status quo complacency and radical UN replacement (✅ owner 12 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Tharoor, *Pax Indica*.
**Links:** IR-3069; IR-3077; IR-3113.
**Trap:** Treating only the Council dimension as "the" reform question understates India's position.
**Mains hook:** "Reform, not replacement" is the precise framing for "should the UN be reformed or replaced?"
**Back:** *"Renewed, not retired: Council seats and Assembly teeth."*

### Flashcard IR-3085 — UN80 Initiative (12 March 2025) versus Security Council reform
**Front:** What is UN80 and how does it differ from Security Council reform?
**Definition (learn):** The Secretary-General's system-wide reform framework on Secretariat efficiency, mandate review and structural and programme realignment (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- Launched 12 March 2025 (✅ owner 12).
- Administrative track, distinct from Charter-level Council reform (⚠️ owner 12).
- Council reform requires Charter amendment and runs through the IGN (✅ owner 12 trap).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 12 Mar 2025.
**Links:** IR-3074; IR-3080; IR-3075.
**Trap:** UN80 is not Security Council reform (owner 12 trap).
**Mains hook:** Institutional change migrates to tracks that do not need P5 ratification (owner 12 advanced).
**Back:** *"UN80 trims the house; Council reform rebuilds the room."*

### Flashcard IR-3086 — UNSC Counter-Terrorism Committee (CTC): mandate and India's chairmanship
**Front:** What does the CTC do, when was it created, and what did India do with it?
**Definition (learn):** A UNSC subsidiary body created by resolution 1373 to coordinate and monitor counter-terrorism cooperation (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- Created by resolution 1373, adopted 28 September 2001 (✅ owner 12).
- Coordinates and assesses; does not enforce; member states implement (✅ owner 12).
- India chaired the CTC during its 2011-12 non-permanent term (✅ owner 12 quoting Tharoor).
- Special meeting in India, 28-29 October 2022, adopting the Delhi Declaration (29 October 2022) on new and emerging technologies for terrorist purposes (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 28 Sep 2001; 2011-12 chair; Delhi Declaration 29 Oct 2022.
**Links:** IR-3087; IR-3088; IR-3111 (2024 PYQ); IR-3089.
**Trap:** CTC does not enforce; it coordinates and monitors (owner 12 trap).
**Mains hook:** Evaluate CTC by its coordinating mandate and dependence on member-state capacity (IR-3111).
**Back:** *"Resolution 1373 in 2001; India chaired it; coordinate, don't enforce."*

### Flashcard IR-3087 — CTED and the 1267/1989/2253 listing committee (CTC's partners)
**Front:** Which body assesses counter-terrorism compliance, and which body lists individuals and entities?
**Definition (learn):** CTED is the CTC's expert arm; the 1267/1989/2253 ISIL (Da'esh) and Al-Qaida Sanctions Committee handles listings and is supported by a Monitoring Team (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- CTED mandate runs to 5 January 2029 under resolution 2810 (2025) (✅ owner 12).
- 1267/1989/2253 Committee: listings (✅ owner 12).
- Monitoring Team supports the Sanctions Committee (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** CTED to 5 Jan 2029.
**Links:** IR-3086; IR-3088; IR-3111.
**Trap:** Effectiveness questions often address the wrong body; listings sit with the Sanctions Committee, not the CTC (owner 12 advanced).
**Mains hook:** Name the body before evaluating it.
**Back:** *"CTED assesses to twenty-twenty-nine; the 1267 committee lists."*

### Flashcard IR-3088 — CTC vs FATF (counter-terrorism financing trap)
**Front:** How do the UNSC CTC and the FATF differ?
**Definition (learn):** The CTC monitors resolution 1373 through UN machinery; FATF is a separate intergovernmental standard-setter for anti-money-laundering and counter-terrorist-financing systems (⚠️ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- CTC: UN machinery, resolution 1373 (IR-3086).
- FATF: standard-setter for AML and CFT systems (⚠️ owner 12).
- Neither substitutes for national investigation and prosecution (⚠️ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Not numeric.
**Links:** IR-3086; IR-3087.
**Trap:** Do not treat FATF as a UN body.
**Mains hook:** Different parts of counter-terrorism governance: standards vs coordination.
**Back:** *"UN watches resolutions, FATF sets money rules."*

### Flashcard IR-3089 — India's UN Security Council record and 2028-29 candidature
**Front:** When has India served on the UNSC, and what is its latest candidature?
**Definition (learn):** India has held non-permanent seats for 2011-12 and 2021-22 and launched its candidature for 2028-29.
**Breakdown:**
- 2011-12: elected "by record margin", with Germany and South Africa (✅ owner 12 quoting Tharoor).
- Brazil and Nigeria were halfway through their terms (✅ owner 12 advanced).
- 2021-22: most recent term (✅ owner 12).
- 13 July 2026: candidature for 2028-29 launched (📰 owner 12; ⚠️ not fetched).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2011-12; 2021-22; 13 Jul 2026.
**Links:** IR-3070; IR-3086; IR-3081.
**Trap:** Candidature is not election; launch is not outcome.
**Mains hook:** Seat-holding shows responsible participation, not a permanent seat.
**Back:** *"Eleven-twelve by record, twenty-one-twenty-two again, twenty-eight-twenty-nine launched."*

### Flashcard IR-3090 — UN peacekeeping principles and India's contribution
**Front:** What are the principles of UN peacekeeping and what has India contributed?
**Definition (learn):** Security Council mandates implemented with voluntarily contributed personnel under consent, impartiality and non-use of force except in self-defence and defence of the mandate (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- Principles: consent, impartiality, non-use of force except self-defence and defence of mandate (✅ owner 12).
- India: more than 275,000 personnel in over 50 missions since 1948 (✅ owner 12).
- First all-women Formed Police Unit to Liberia in 2007 (✅ owner 12).
- Limitation: troop contribution does not yield a permanent seat (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 275,000+; 50+ missions; 1948 onward; Liberia 2007.
**Links:** IR-3089; IR-3106 (2026 prelims ledger); IR-3107.
**Trap:** Contribution is not a Council seat.
**Mains hook:** Responsible multilateralism strengthens the reform claim, without guaranteeing it.
**Back:** *"Consent, impartiality, minimum force; two hundred seventy-five thousand blue helmets."*

### Flashcard IR-3091 — ICJ climate advisory opinion (23 July 2025)
**Front:** What did the ICJ advisory opinion on climate change say and what is its legal force?
**Definition (learn):** The Court's advisory opinion on *Obligations of States in respect of Climate Change*, delivered 23 July 2025 (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- Climate duties under treaty and customary law require due diligence, prevention of significant harm and cooperation (✅ owner 12).
- Breach can engage State responsibility (✅ owner 12).
- Authoritative but not binding (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 23 Jul 2025.
**Links:** IR-3073; IR-3068; IR-3058.
**Trap:** Advisory ≠ binding judgment.
**Mains hook:** Adjudication advances where the Council is deadlocked (owner 12).
**Back:** *"July twenty-five: due diligence, not a court order."*

### Flashcard IR-3092 — ICJ versus ICC (trap pair)
**Front:** How do the ICJ and ICC differ, and what is India's status under the Rome Statute?
**Definition (learn):** The ICJ is a UN principal organ deciding inter-state disputes and giving advisory opinions; the ICC is a separate treaty court created by the 1998 Rome Statute to prosecute individuals for specified international crimes (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- ICJ: states; UN organ; advisory and contentious (✅ owner 12).
- ICC: individuals; Rome Statute 1998 (✅ owner 12).
- India: neither a signatory nor a State Party to the Rome Statute (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Rome Statute 1998.
**Links:** IR-3073; IR-3068.
**Trap:** Not all international courts are the same; an ICC case does not bind India.
**Mains hook:** Distinguish courts by legal basis and who they judge.
**Back:** *"States in The Hague's ICJ; individuals in the ICC; India outside Rome."*

### Flashcard IR-3093 — UNESCO: founding, governance and World Heritage link
**Front:** Describe UNESCO's founding, governing organs and its World Heritage role.
**Definition (learn):** UNESCO promotes cooperation in education, science, culture and communication (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- Created in 1945 (✅ owner 12).
- General Conference: all members (✅ owner 12).
- Executive Board: 58 members, oversight (✅ owner 12).
- Secretariat led by the Director-General (✅ owner 12).
- Administers the 1972 World Heritage Convention through the World Heritage Committee and List (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 1945; 58-member Executive Board; 1972 convention.
**Links:** IR-3094; IR-3095; IR-3109 (2019 PYQ).
**Trap:** The Executive Board provides oversight; the General Conference is the all-members body.
**Mains hook:** Specialised agencies are vulnerable to major-power funding and legitimacy choices (owner 12).
**Back:** *"Forty-five, fifty-eight on the board, seventy-two heritage."*

### Flashcard IR-3094 — US withdrawal from UNESCO: notification versus completed exit
**Front:** When did the US announce, complete and reverse its UNESCO withdrawal, and what is the latest notice?
**Definition (learn):** A funding and legitimacy case study showing that a notified withdrawal is not a completed withdrawal (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- Announced 2017; effective 31 December 2018 (✅ owner 12).
- Returned 2023 with an arrears-payment plan (✅ owner 12).
- Notified another withdrawal July 2025, effective end-December 2026 (✅ owner 12).
- As of 3 August 2026, still a member (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2017; 31 Dec 2018; 2023 return; Jul 2025 notice; end-Dec 2026.
**Links:** IR-3093; IR-3095; IR-3109.
**Trap:** Notification is not withdrawal; the US remained a member as of August 2026.
**Mains hook:** Specialised agencies face funding and legitimacy risk from major powers (owner 12).
**Back:** *"Left in 2018, back in 2023, leaving again in December 2026."*

### Flashcard IR-3095 — India re-elected to UNESCO Executive Board (2025-29)
**Front:** What is India's UNESCO Executive Board position?
**Definition (learn):** India was re-elected to the UNESCO Executive Board for 2025-29 (📰 owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- Office creates agenda and oversight access (📰 owner 12).
- Election does not establish delivery or control (📰 owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025-29.
**Links:** IR-3093; IR-3094.
**Trap:** Election is access, not control.
**Mains hook:** Use board membership as a channel of agenda influence.
**Back:** *"Twenty-five to twenty-nine: a seat with access, not command."*

### Flashcard IR-3096 — WHO governance: World Health Assembly, Executive Board, Director-General
**Front:** Which WHO organ is supreme, and what does each organ do?
**Definition (learn):** The WHO's organs and their roles (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- World Health Assembly: supreme decision-making body (✅ owner 12).
- Executive Board: gives effect to Assembly decisions (✅ owner 12).
- Secretariat under the Director-General: implements programmes (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** WHA supreme.
**Links:** IR-3097; IR-3099; IR-3110 (2020 PYQ).
**Trap:** The Executive Board does not outrank the Assembly.
**Mains hook:** Governance architecture determines who can enforce health decisions.
**Back:** *"Assembly decides, Board directs, Secretariat delivers."*

### Flashcard IR-3097 — WHO and the COVID-19 PHEIC (30 January 2020)
**Front:** When did the IHR Emergency Committee first meet, and when did WHO declare the PHEIC?
**Definition (learn):** A public health emergency of international concern under the International Health Regulations (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- First IHR Emergency Committee meeting, 22-23 January 2020: no determination (✅ owner 12).
- Reconvened; WHO declared a PHEIC on 30 January 2020 (✅ owner 12).
- Limitation: divided early assessment and dependence on state reporting exposed weak enforcement (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 22-23 Jan 2020 no determination; 30 Jan 2020 PHEIC.
**Links:** IR-3096; IR-3098; IR-3110.
**Trap:** A committee's first meeting is not automatically a PHEIC declaration.
**Mains hook:** WHO gave an alert platform; enforcement and reporting depend on states.
**Back:** *"Twenty-two January, no call; thirty January, PHEIC."*

### Flashcard IR-3098 — COVAX: pooled vaccine access and its limit
**Front:** What was COVAX, how many doses did it deliver, and what limited it?
**Definition (learn):** The WHO-Gavi-CEPI-UNICEF vaccine pooling mechanism (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- Close to two billion doses to 146 economies (✅ owner 12).
- Closed at the end of 2023 (✅ owner 12).
- Limit: supply nationalism and unequal coverage (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** About 2 billion doses; 146 economies; closed end-2023.
**Links:** IR-3096; IR-3097; IR-3099.
**Trap:** A delivery mechanism cannot secure equity alone.
**Mains hook:** Pooling widened access, but equity depends on supply and political choices.
**Back:** *"Two billion doses, one hundred forty-six economies, closed twenty-three."*

### Flashcard IR-3099 — WHO Pandemic Agreement: adopted, not open for signature
**Front:** When was the WHO Pandemic Agreement adopted, and what stops it from operating?
**Definition (learn):** Adopted by consensus at the 78th World Health Assembly, but not yet open for signature (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- Adopted 20 May 2025 at WHA78 (✅ owner 12).
- Pathogen Access and Benefit-Sharing (PABS) annex still under negotiation (✅ owner 12).
- Not open for signature as of 3 August 2026 (✅ owner 12).
- Entry into force needs 60 ratifications (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 20 May 2025; 60 ratifications.
**Links:** IR-3096; IR-3098; IR-3100.
**Trap:** Adoption is not operation; a treaty without a completed annex is not a regime.
**Mains hook:** Adoption is not legal operation (owner 12 advanced).
**Back:** *"Adopted May twenty-five, annex unfinished, sixty to live."*

### Flashcard IR-3100 — 2024 IHR amendments in force (19 September 2025)
**Front:** When did the 2024 International Health Regulations amendments enter into force?
**Definition (learn):** Amendments to the IHR, the legal framework for WHO health emergencies (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- Entered into force 19 September 2025 (✅ owner 12).
- Distinct from the Pandemic Agreement (IR-3099) (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 19 Sep 2025.
**Links:** IR-3099; IR-3097.
**Trap:** IHR amendments and the Pandemic Agreement are separate instruments.
**Mains hook:** Two health instruments: amended rules in force and a new treaty pending.
**Back:** *"IHR changed in September twenty-five; the pandemic treaty waits."*

### Flashcard IR-3101 — BBNJ Agreement (High Seas): entry into force and India's status
**Front:** When did the BBNJ Agreement enter into force, and what is India's status?
**Definition (learn):** The Biodiversity Beyond National Jurisdiction Agreement on high-seas biodiversity (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- Entered into force 17 January 2026 (✅ owner 12).
- India signed 25 September 2024; ratification not deposited (✅ owner 12).
- India is not a party as of owner verification (✅ owner 12 advanced).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 17 Jan 2026; India signed 25 Sep 2024.
**Links:** IR-3091; IR-3099.
**Trap:** Signature is not ratification or party status.
**Mains hook:** Treaty-making advances where the Council is deadlocked.
**Back:** *"Signed twenty-four, in force twenty-six, India not yet a party."*

### Flashcard IR-3102 — IMF: mandate, 16th quota review and India's quota share
**Front:** What is the IMF's mandate, what did the 16th quota review approve, and what are India's shares?
**Definition (learn):** The IMF promotes international monetary cooperation, exchange stability and temporary balance-of-payments support (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- 16th General Review of Quotas concluded December 2023: 50% equiproportional increase (✅ owner 12).
- Equiproportional increase leaves relative shares unchanged (✅ owner 12).
- India: quota share about 2.75%; voting share about 2.63% (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Dec 2023; 50%; 2.75%; 2.63%.
**Links:** IR-3103; IR-3104; IR-3075 (financing dimension).
**Trap:** Bigger quotas do not fix representation if shares are unchanged.
**Mains hook:** The gap between economic weight and formal voice supports India's reform case (owner 12).
**Back:** *"Fifty percent more, shares unchanged, India near two and three-quarters."*

### Flashcard IR-3103 — World Bank: development finance, IDA21 and voice reform
**Front:** What does the World Bank do, and what was IDA21's size?
**Definition (learn):** The World Bank provides development finance, reconstruction and poverty-reduction support (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- IDA21 finalised December 2024, mobilising USD 100 billion for FY2025-28 (✅ owner 12).
- Shareholding and Evolution Roadmap debates on voice (⚠️ owner 12).
- Limit: more finance does not itself redistribute voting power (⚠️ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** IDA21 USD 100bn FY2025-28; Dec 2024.
**Links:** IR-3102; IR-3104; IR-3013 (NDB) and IR-3028 (AIIB).
**Trap:** More money is not more voice.
**Mains hook:** Reform concerns representation, mandate and resource scale.
**Back:** *"Development finance, USD hundred billion, voice still to redistribute."*

### Flashcard IR-3104 — Bretton Woods and trade institutions: mandate box (IMF, World Bank, WTO)
**Front:** Give the core mandate of each of the three institutions.
**Definition (learn):** The IMF, World Bank and WTO each cover a different function in global economic governance (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- IMF: international monetary cooperation, exchange stability, temporary balance-of-payments support (✅ owner 12).
- World Bank: development finance, reconstruction and poverty reduction (✅ owner 12).
- WTO: administration of trade rules, negotiation, trade-policy monitoring, dispute settlement (✅ owner 12).
- Operational lending and trade mechanics are Economy's domain (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Mandates only.
**Links:** IR-3102; IR-3103; IR-3045.
**Trap:** The WTO is not a Bretton Woods financing institution, though it is a trade institution linked to the system (⚠️).
**Mains hook:** Distinguish the three by function before discussing governance reform.
**Back:** *"Monetary, development, trade: three functions, three rulebooks."*

### Flashcard IR-3105 — Multilateral technology governance: GDC, GPAI and ITU
**Front:** What do the Global Digital Compact, GPAI and ITU do in technology governance?
**Definition (learn):** Three non-equivalent instruments and bodies that shape digital and AI governance (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- Global Digital Compact: adopted in 2024 as an annex to the Pact for the Future; a political framework, not a binding treaty (✅ owner 12).
- GPAI: multistakeholder partnership on responsible, human-centric AI; India argues for inclusive innovation and Global South participation (✅ owner 12).
- ITU: telecommunications and ICT standards and capacity; not an AI regulator (✅ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** GDC 2024.
**Links:** IR-3083; IR-3070 (UN organ routing).
**Trap:** ITU is not an AI regulator; GPAI is not a UN treaty body; the GDC is not binding.
**Mains hook:** Non-binding outcomes limit enforceability; safety must combine with development access.
**Back:** *"Compact is a promise, GPAI is a club, ITU sets the wires."*

### Flashcard IR-3106 — Prelims ledger 2022: UN Credentials Committee, Polar Code, observer status and refugee settlements
**Front:** Which 2022 objective demands route to the UN owner, and what must be covered?
**Definition (learn):** Four 2022 Prelims demands route here; their keys are not held locally, so none is answered (✅ owner 12).
**Breakdown:**
- Q76: UN Credentials Committee structure and mandate (⚠️ standard knowledge: it reviews delegates' credentials in the UNGA).
- Q77: International Polar Code purpose and scope (⚠️ standard knowledge: an IMO instrument for polar shipping).
- Q78: UNGA observer status and Permanent Observers (⚠️ standard knowledge).
- Q82: Bidibidi and Dadaab refugee settlements in East Africa (⚠️ standard knowledge: Uganda and Kenya).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Keys unavailable locally.
**Links:** IR-3069; IR-3090; IR-3107.
**Trap:** Statement-level: distinguish permanent observers from members.
**Mains hook:** Prelims-only; the Credentials, observer and peacekeeping items feed the UN and peacekeeping answers in IR-3069 and IR-3090.
**Back:** *"Credentials check, Polar code, observers watch, refugees in Uganda and Kenya."*

### Flashcard IR-3107 — Prelims ledger 2023: GCM, famine countries and conflict-zone matches
**Front:** Which 2023 objective demands route to the UN owner?
**Definition (learn):** Three 2023 Prelims demands on migration, famine and conflict geography (✅ owner 12; keys not held locally).
**Breakdown:**
- Q78: country with decades of civil strife and famine (no key; country-level recall).
- Q90: Global Compact for Safe, Orderly and Regular Migration (⚠️ standard knowledge: non-binding, adopted 2018).
- Q93: conflict zones North Kivu, Nagorno-Karabakh, Kherson, Zaporizhzhia.
- Q97: conflict zones Donbas, Kachin, Tigray.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Keys unavailable.
**Links:** IR-3105; IR-3108; IR-3068.
**Trap:** Non-binding migration compact vs binding treaty; match zones to countries, not to regions.
**Mains hook:** Prelims-only; the migration compact and conflict-zone items feed the humanitarian governance answer in IR-3105 and IR-3068.
**Back:** *"GCM is non-binding; zones match countries."*

### Flashcard IR-3109 — PYQ 2019 GS-II Q10: UNESCO funding stress and US withdrawal (10 marks, 150 words)
**Front:** Outline the answer route for the 2019 UNESCO question.
**Definition (learn):** Discuss UNESCO's funding and political stress after the US withdrawal (✅ owner 12 ledger; word limit from instruction block).
**Breakdown:**
- Route: UNESCO's organs and budget (IR-3093).
- Evidence: US 2017 announcement, 31 Dec 2018 effect, 2023 return, 2025 notice (IR-3094).
- Counterpoint: specialised agencies are exposed to donor choices; India's board role (IR-3095).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2019 GS-II Q10; 10 marks; 150 words.
**Links:** IR-3093; IR-3094; IR-3095.
**Trap:** Notice of withdrawal is not completed exit.
**Mains hook:** Funding dependence makes agency mandates vulnerable to major power choices.
**Back:** *"Exit announced, exit deferred, funding fragile."*

### Flashcard IR-3110 — PYQ 2020 GS-II Q9: WHO role in global health security (10 marks, 150 words)
**Front:** Outline the answer route for the 2020 WHO question.
**Definition (learn):** Critically examine WHO's role in global health security during the pandemic (✅ owner 12 ledger; 2020 paper not held locally).
**Breakdown:**

- Evidence: 30 January 2020 PHEIC; first committee no-decision (IR-3097).
- Limit: state reporting and compliance; COVAX equity gap (IR-3098).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2020 GS-II Q9; 10 marks; 150 words.
**Links:** IR-3096; IR-3097; IR-3098; IR-3099.
**Trap:** Critically examine means weigh both the coordinating role and enforcement limits.
**Mains hook:** WHO supplied alert and guidance; enforcement depended on states.
**Back:** *"Alert yes, enforcement no, equity lagged."*

### Flashcard IR-3111 — PYQ 2024 GS-II Q19: effectiveness of the UNSC CTC (15 marks, 250 words)
**Front:** How should the 2024 Q19 answer evaluate the CTC?
**Definition (learn):** "Terrorism has become a significant threat... Evaluate the effectiveness of the UNSC's Counter Terrorism Committee and its associated bodies..." (✅ owner 12; stem confirmed against 2024 paper per owner).
**Breakdown:**
- Define mandate: resolution 1373 (IR-3086).
- Separate the listing committee (IR-3087).
- Dependence on member-state capacity and will; no enforcement (IR-3086).
- Credit CTED assessment to 2029 and India's chairmanship and Delhi Declaration (IR-3087; IR-3086).
- Conclude: effective as coordination, limited as enforcement.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2024 GS-II Q19; 15 marks; 250 words.
**Links:** IR-3086; IR-3087; IR-3088.
**Trap:** Do not claim the CTC enforces compliance.
**Mains hook:** Coordination quality is measured by member-state implementation.
**Back:** *"Coordinate, assess, cannot enforce: effective only as far as states comply."*

### Flashcard IR-3112 — PYQ 2025 GS-II Q20: UN reform and East-West confrontation (15 marks, 250 words)
**Front:** How should the 2025 Q20 answer handle the claim that UN reform is unresolved because of East-West imbalance?
**Definition (learn):** "The reform process in the United Nations remains unresolved, because of the delicate imbalance of East and West and entanglement of the USA vs. Russo-Chinese alliance. Examine and critically evaluate..." (✅ owner 12; stem confirmed per owner note).
**Breakdown:**
- Separate the veto's stabilising and blocking roles (IR-3076).
- Cite G4/IGN, Pact for the Future (IR-3083) and G4 statement (IR-3082) as sustained but unresolved.
- Note counter-coalition opposition (IR-3078) and that East-West is one fault line among several.
- Note the G4's claim that consensus is not a legal requirement (IR-3082).
- Do not predict outcome.
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** 2025 GS-II Q20; 15 marks; 250 words.
**Links:** IR-3075; IR-3076; IR-3080; IR-3082; IR-3083.
**Trap:** Do not present East-West as the sole explanation.
**Mains hook:** Reform discussed is not reform achieved.
**Back:** *"Veto blocks, talks continue, East-West is one fault line."*

### Flashcard IR-3113 — Trap: reform discussed vs reform achieved (concept)
**Front:** Why must "reform discussed" never be written as "reform achieved"?
**Definition (learn):** Summit documents, co-chairs and interventions show activity; only a Charter amendment changes the Council (✅ owner 12 advanced).
**Breakdown:**
- Discussed: IGN, G4 statement, Pact, India's intervention (IR-3080 to IR-3083).
- Achieved: none of these changes the Charter (✅ owner 12 advanced).
- As of 3 August 2026, no consolidated model or text-based negotiation decision (⚠️ owner 12).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** Status as of owner date.
**Links:** IR-3080; IR-3083; IR-3084.
**Trap:** Announcement and delivery are different (owner 12 study link).
**Mains hook:** The single most important status fact for any UN-reform answer (owner 12).
**Back:** *"Talked, drafted, not amended."*

### Flashcard IR-3114 — Current anchor for UN reform and governance (dated, with stage)
**Front:** Which dated markers anchor a current UN-governance answer?
**Definition (learn):** Dated events with stage recorded in owner 12 (✅ owner 12 📰).
**Breakdown:**
- 25 September 2025: G4 joint statement (IR-3082).
- 20 April 2026: India's IGN intervention (IR-3081).
- July 2026: GA decision to continue negotiations into its 81st session (📰 owner 12 verified note; ⚠️ not fetched).
- 13 July 2026: India's 2028-29 candidature launched (IR-3089).
- 17 January 2026: BBNJ in force (IR-3101).
- 31 December 2026: Guterres's term ends (IR-3074).
**Key facts / Dates / Data:** As listed.
**Links:** IR-3080; IR-3081; IR-3082; IR-3089; IR-3101; IR-3074.
**Trap:** Name the legal stage (proposal, adoption, in force).
**Mains hook:** Cite dated instruments to avoid undated "UN reform" claims.
**Back:** *"G4 twenty-five, India twenty-six, GA continues, Guterres exits December twenty-six."*
